# Builder Queue

> Work items queued by **Planner** for **Builder** to clear one PR per cycle.
> Planner appends rows + spec/plan links; Builder claims a 📋 row whose dependencies are all met, ships it as a PR, then marks it ✅.

> **Last updated:** 2026-09-05 (Planner) — **Two small plans written: `TTS-11` 📋 and `OPS-7` 📋.** Both rows are defects, both were sized to the work rather than given the arc treatment — a 2,000-line plan for a 0.5 d fix is its own defect. ⭐ **THE HEADLINE IS THAT `TTS-11` WAS FILED FOR THREE LOGGING PATHS AND THERE ARE ELEVEN.** A sweep of every `Log*` call in the solution found eight more, and **the two worst were not among the three filed**: `AnnouncementService.cs:40` logs the announcement **untruncated** — every other site clips to 47 or 50 characters — and it is the shared entry point for both `NotificationsController` and phone-call announcements; and `SoundFlowMasterMixer.cs:101-103` logs `source.Name` from **generic mixer code that has no idea it is holding speech**. That second one reframes the row: the defect is not three lines that each chose to log text, it is **one property — `TTSEventSource.Name` is user content wearing a display name's clothes — plus a habit of logging payloads**, so any code that logs an audio source's name is a leak, now or later. ⚠ **Three line numbers in the record are stale and are corrected in the plans rather than in the punch list, which another pass holds** (`C-82`, `C-84`): the ducking argument is at **`AudioManager.cs:515`**, not `:501` — lines 499 and 501 are comment prose after `PHN-1f` and `PHN-2` both edited that method — and the whole-string line is **`TTSEventSource.cs:109`**, not `:92`, which is also wrong in the comment at `EventPlaybackServiceTests.cs:1218`. **The row's own hedge is what saved it:** it said *"quoted rather than trusted to the number"*, and the quote is how both were re-found. **Two sites were never filed at all** — a second `AudioManager` arm at `:523-525` (`C-83`, added by `PHN-1f`) and a second mixer site at `:117-119`. ⭐ **Decision on what replaces the content:** a hash prefix **plus** a length (`txt:9f2ab41c/142`) in the shape `GvMediaCache.MaskFor` already establishes; and for the four `.Name` sites, **drop the name and log `Type` + `Id`** — the mixer already logs `Id`, so `Name` is redundant there today. **This makes operator diagnosis better rather than worse**, which is what the row's warning was really asking for: today three sites truncate the same utterance at 50, 47 and not-at-all, so nobody can tell they are one event, and a stable token makes that chain traceable for the first time. ⚠ **The helper's doc must say it is a correlation token, NOT a confidentiality boundary** — announcement text has a small candidate space and a short utterance's hash is recoverable by enumeration. Claiming otherwise would be the fourth overclaiming comment `CLAUDE.md` § *Pre-Merge Review* exists to catch. ⚠ **`NeitherTheTextNorTheRawMediaIdReachesAnyLogLineThisSeamWrites` IS NOT WIDENED, and that is the deliberate answer to the trap that named it.** `TTSFactory` constructs its own `HttpClient`, so a real factory still cannot run offline and the harness **still** cannot observe the wider property; the plan drives the real `TTSEventSource`, `TTSFactory` and `SoundFlowMasterMixer` in new tests instead and leaves the honest name alone. **When a harness cannot observe a property, the fix is a harness that can, not a name that sounds like one.** **Every pin in both plans names its falsifying mutation and Builder must run it**, per the six consecutive cycles that each found a test passing against a deliberately broken implementation. ⚠ **`TTS-11` is estimated 1.5 d, not the 0.5 h on the punch list** — that figure was honest for three lines and the set is eleven. 🆕 **`OPS-7` carries a second defect in the same fourteen lines** (`C-88`): `:230`'s *"Remote file move failed!"* check reads a `$LASTEXITCODE` that `:222` has already overwritten, and since **both** `$configDir` values ship a seed file, `:221` is always true — so a genuine failure of the file move at `:217` is **always** masked and the message names the wrong step. **`OPS-7` got its own plan file rather than a plan-in-the-row because the fix is nine lines and the verification is not:** the obvious test is the one that can destroy the secret, so §3 uses the script's own `-TargetPath` against a scratch install path and **runs the pre-fix mutation first**, so the defect is observed rather than read. ⚠ **`-TargetPath` does not isolate everything** — the WirePlumber block at `:299-318` is parameterised by neither it nor `-NoRestart` and can cycle BT/audio on the box. **Swept for the same seed/copy shape elsewhere: one instance**, and the fix's exemplar (`Sync-WpRule`, `:268-297`, which has always guarded per destination) is forty lines below the bug in the same file. ⛔ **One thing deliberately NOT filed, because it needs an owner decision rather than a planner's judgement.** The same sweep found **four phone-number sites** — `PhoneContactLookupService.cs:62` logs a full unmasked number **and** the contact's name at Information, `:102` logs a full number at **Warning** (so it survives every log-level tightening including `LOG-11`'s), `:78`/`:96` at Debug, and `PhoneCallIntegrationService.cs:127` logs a caller name that falls back to the raw number. They are **not** folded into `TTS-11`: different data class (third-party PII, not household-authored content), different idiom (`PhoneContactLookupService.cs:87-90` **already masks**, three lines below `:78` which does not — so the fix there is *apply the local idiom consistently*, not *adopt the new helper*), and a different exposure profile because of the Warning-level line. They are recorded in `TTS-11` plan §6.1 and its Task 8 adds them to `design/FUTURE-WORK.md`. **Whether they become a row is the owner's call; the honest recommendation is yes, and `:102` at Warning is the strongest single argument.**

> **Last updated:** 2026-09-04 (Builder) — ✅ **`PHN-1e` MERGED as [#561](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/561), and the 🚫 it carried is cleared. Both blockers resolved, in OPPOSITE directions.** ⭐ **B1 cost no code at all.** Owner decision **`D30`** — *"If the page reloads mid-voicemail, the audio should fail. If the user wants to hear it they can replay."* — says the measured refresh behaviour is the **wanted** behaviour, so `AttendedPlaybackCircuitHandler` shipped **byte-for-byte as built**, with no grace period, no circuit-identity matching and no survival mechanism; the correction landed on ADR-029 §7.3's *reasoning* rather than on the branch. ⚠ **What fell with it is worth knowing before citing §7.3:** its worked example (circuit B is "already open" when A closes — the close strictly precedes the open), its retention-window claim (the 10 minutes covers *unexpected* disconnects only), and its self-description as *"the weakest of the three defences"* — the rule is **promoted** to the mechanism that implements `D30`, with the 300 s cap as the backstop behind **it**. ⭐ **B2 is the one that needed code, and it was found by the comment-accuracy reviewer rather than by any test.** ADR §7.5's `/sleep` rule never fired on the **30-minute idle timer** — the case §7.5's own motivating sentence names — because `idle-dimmer.js` reaches `/sleep` by `window.location.href` and deliberately calls nothing server-side, so `IsSleeping` is **false** there. Fixed with a **second edge** on the `ENC-6` seam, `SetSleepScreenVisibleAsync(true)`, beside the existing `EnterSleepAsync` hook. ⭐ **The predicate is the REPORT ITSELF, not a transition of `ConsoleWakeState` — and that is a pre-merge correction, not the original design.** Written the first way, the rule could be **suppressed by a stale flag**: `Sleep.razor`'s dispose report is best-effort behind a 2 s timeout and `MainLayout`'s corrective `false` is fire-and-forget, so losing both leaves the flag reading `true` while the console is awake, the next idle timeout is not a *change*, and the rule does not fire — §7.5's own failure produced by the rule meant to prevent it. **Still never polled** (no loop, no timer, and `WakeState` is not consulted at all). ⚠ **`ISleepService.SetSleepScreenVisible` became `SetSleepScreenVisibleAsync` and the stop is AWAITED**, on **C-49**'s own lesson — a `Radio.Core` signature change with one production call site. ⚠ **That false claim had propagated through FOUR documents** (ADR §7.5 → plan C-51 → the branch's own comment → the PR body), each restating the one before it, and **not one checked `idle-dimmer.js`**, which contradicts it in a comment written for the purpose. ⭐ **THE CAP IS NOW PROVED, NOT ASSUMED, AND IT HAD TO BE:** Amendment 2's argument that the network-drop path is unreachable *depends* on the 300 s cap firing, and the cap ships with this PR rather than existing on `main`. Mutation-tested — removing `ArmDurationCap` reds 2 tests, and keeping the timer while dropping its dispatched `StopAsync` reds the same 2, so the tests pin the **stop** and not merely the timer. **`D30` filed in [`docs/HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) §7**, which ADR-029 deliberately left for this cycle. **Two adversarial reviewers ran** (Copilot still out of quota) and they earned it a third cycle running. ⭐ **The sharpest finding was a behavioural defect in the day’s OWN fix, and it was found by attacking a comment rather than the code:** the sleep rule was written as *"stop when `ConsoleWakeState` leaves `Awake`"* and its remark claimed *"it cannot suppress a stop"* — it can, whenever `_isSleepScreenVisible` is left stale-`true` (both reports that would clear it are best-effort, and the tree says so in comments), because the next idle timeout is then not a *change*. The predicate is now **the report itself**. Also fixed: a **wedged stop that made the console UNWAKEABLE** (the sleep stop runs under the same `_lock` `WakeAsync` needs — now time-boxed, and the falsification **hangs** rather than failing), a cap timer callback that was a **process kill**, a circuit-open throw that could **strand the count so `D30`’s rule never fires again**, a missing upper clamp on `MaxPlaybackSeconds`, and **four tests that could not fail**. ⛔ **One defect deferred with the deferral logged** — `AudioStateStore.NotifyAsync` awaits only the last subscriber of a multicast delegate, pre-existing across all eleven events on that store; **PR 6 is its real deadline** and it wants a Planner row. ⚠ **`PHN-1f` does NOT become claimable** — it has no plan; its 🔒 clears only on a Planner pass. *Previous two entries preserved below.*

> **Last updated:** 2026-09-04 (Builder) — ⛔ **`PHN-1e` is 🚫 BLOCKED, not done. The branch is complete, green and pushed as [#561](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/561); it is NOT merged and must not be.** ⭐ **The reason is the one thing the plan said only the box could answer, and it resolved against the design.** Plan §0.3 **U3** predicted a browser refresh goes 1 → 2 → 1 and never touches zero. Measured twice on the appliance at `dd8e85ec`: with two tabs it is **2 → 1 → 2** — the close arrives BEFORE the open — so with a single browser it is **1 → 0 → 1** and the last-circuit backstop stops the playback at the zero. A 49-second voicemail died 7 seconds in, on a reload. ⚠ **This is ADR-level, not a code bug.** §7.3 replaced the owner-circuit rule specifically to survive refreshes, reasoning that the replacement circuit is *already open* when the old one closes. It is not. On a single-kiosk box — the normal state of this appliance — the last-circuit rule reproduces the exact failure it was written to remove, and reproduces it faster. The 10-minute `DisconnectedCircuitRetentionPeriod` never enters this path: it covers *unexpected* disconnects, while a reload sends a graceful close that disposes the circuit at once. ⭐ **Consequence worth the Planner's attention: as built, every reachable firing of this backstop is a false positive** — a graceful close fires immediately (usually a user who is still there), and an unexpected drop waits 10 minutes, by which time the 300 s cap has already stopped the audio. The plan's own instruction for this outcome was *"stop and re-plan, do not paper over it with a delay"*; that was followed, and no delay was added. ⚠ **A second, independent blocker from the review: §7.5's `/sleep` rule does not cover the idle timer**, which is the case §7.5 was written for. `idle-dimmer.js` navigates to `/sleep` *without* calling `SetSleepAsync`, by design, so the 30-minute idle path never reaches `SleepService.EnterSleepAsync` and lands the kiosk on a no-transport surface with a voicemail playing. **ADR §7.5 is itself wrong about this**, citing `idle-dimmer.js` line numbers that no longer exist — so it is a Planner correction. ✅ **What IS settled, so a re-plan need not re-litigate it:** **U1** — the payload survives the real `JsonHubProtocol` (the typed handler received it; `GET /current` and the hub agree on `"state":"Playing"` as a string), which is also what makes `PHN-1f`'s new state deployable without a lockstep Web build; **U2** — a singleton `CircuitHandler` does receive every circuit's callbacks (1 → 2 across two circuits), so Task 7's shape is right; **the max-duration cap works on real hardware** (cap 20 s vs a 42 s recording → stopped, with the WRN line in journald); and **the `/sleep` rule works for the path that reaches it** (Playing → sleep → Stopped, and waking does not resume). ⚠ **Review coverage was degraded again** — Copilot is still out of quota — so two adversarial reviewers ran with different briefs, as on `PHN-1d`. They earned it: **5 HIGH and 5 MEDIUM**, including that the cap's central justification comment is false (*both* event sources DO observe the token; a `CancelAfter` cap would be **worse** than the "changes nothing" the comment claims, leaving audio running while killing the transition that would have ended it), and that `EventPlaybackRegistrationTests` — named in a shipped comment as what keeps the eager-construction claim honest — fakes three of the four dependencies and says so itself. ⭐ **And one test in this row could not fail.** `ANaturalEndDisarmsTheDurationCap`, exactly as the plan specified it, passed **62/62 with the disarm deleted** — `ClaimTerminal` absorbs the late stop before it reaches the source, so the `StopCalls` assertion was pinning a different property. Rewritten to assert on the cap's own log line; now red under that mutation. **Two plan defects filed** in the Blocker section: that, and Task 12's scope gate contradicting Task 10c. *Previous entry preserved below.*

Filing it separately would have created a duplicate that two rows both claim. **Two ordering notes filed** in § *Dependency / ordering notes*: `PHN-1f` **before** PR 6 (`D28`'s own reasoning — the queue goes where its visibility goes), and `PHN-1f` is **where PR 4's fade-window residual closes** — with the deadline named honestly as *"before anything ever enables phone integration"* rather than *"before PR 6"*, because no row in this arc flips `PhoneIntegration:Enabled`. ⚠ **The § ordering note that said the arc is seven PRs is corrected in place**, with its previous sequence preserved — it listed PR 5 as one item and is the note a reader would have trusted. **Three punch-list rows filed in the same pass, all from `PHN-1d`'s Builder or from today's owner report:** **`TTS-11`** (§4.4) — `AudioManager`'s ducking-started line logs `TriggeringSource?.Name`, which for speech is the SMS body, and it is a **second independent path** to the defect `design/FUTURE-WORK.md` § *TTS seam* item 5 already has; **`TEST-8`** (§4.6) — `NotificationsController`'s `Priority ?? 8` is exercised by **zero** tests, and it is the coupling that decides whether `PHN-1d` preempts at all; and **`ENC-21`** (§3.5) — ✅ the owner's reversed knobs, **fixed with no code change and no deploy.** ⚠ **`ENC-8`'s field count was wrong and is corrected**: *"all 24 fields × 4 encoders"* → **11 per encoder = 44, plus `steps_per_detent` = 45 compared**, counted off `RotaryEncoderConfigVerifier.Compare`. ⚠ **`D28`'s register entry is amended, not rewritten**: it says the queue *"ships in PR 5, not PR 4"*, and PR 5 is now two PRs — it ships in **PR 5b, `PHN-1f`**.

> **Prior entry — 2026-09-04 (Builder):** **`PHN-1d` shipped ✅ as [#558](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/558). ADR-029 PR 4 is in: priority stops being decorative.** `DuckingService` now announces **every source that joins the ducking set**, and `EventPlaybackService` stops attended playback when a source starts at or above `GvMedia:PreemptAtPriority` (8) — the key's first reader in its life, and the first load-bearing read of `GetPriority` in this system. ⭐ **Verified on the box, not deferred:** a 29.9 s voicemail was stopped mid-play by a doorbell posted to `/api/notifications/announce` at its default priority 8; a priority-5 announcement mixed with it instead; and a playback posted at `priority: 8` did **not** stop itself. Exactly one preemption warning across all three, which is the evidence for the latter two. The file sink also proves the two things the plan said only the box could — `activeEvents=2` (a second concurrent source now raises at all) and `Ducking ended: volume restored` after each preemption. ⚠ **THE ARC'S REVIEW COVERAGE IS DEGRADED AND THIS ROW COMPENSATED FOR IT.** The automated GitHub reviewer is out of quota and did not run (it errored on #556 too), so this PR was given **two** adversarial reviewers with different briefs rather than one. They earned it: between them they found a real behavioural defect and **five wrong *reasons*** — the `CLAUDE.md` § Pre-Merge Review class, five more instances, on a PR written specifically to avoid it. ⭐ **The defect worth remembering: `DuckingService` raises its TRANSITION event AFTER awaiting the attack fade**, so a stop for that same source landing inside that ~100 ms window deletes its priority override first and a *synchronous* `GetPriority` still answers the category default 8 — an announcement that explicitly claimed 3 would preempt. Unreachable while `PhoneIntegration:Enabled` is false; reachable the moment this arc flips it. Guarded by rejecting a start raise whose `ActiveEventCount` is 0, which is that race's signature and never a healthy start. ⭐ **And the headline correction: `PreemptAtPriority`'s whole "safe to lower, trap to raise" argument named a mechanism no live path uses.** All four `StartDuckingAsync` call sites call `SetPriority` first, so `GetPriority`'s category-default fallback never answers a start raise. The conclusion survives; the real reason is **`NotificationsController.Announce`'s `request.Priority ?? 8`** — that is where every doorbell's 8 comes from, and **it is pinned by no test anywhere**: change it to `?? 5` and preemption silently stops with nothing red. Worth a row. ⭐ **Both gaps this PR had DECLARED are now closed**, which is the part to copy: the `_gate` serialisation and the synchronous priority read were each shipped as "no test catches this" and each now has one that reds under its own mutation — a `PlayGate` seam asserting *ordering* rather than counting, and a fake that records *which thread* called `GetPriority`. ⚠ **`AudioManager.OnDuckingStateChanged` logs the TTS utterance at Information** (`AudioManager.cs:501` logs `TriggeringSource?.Name`, which for speech is the SMS body). Pre-existing, observed live during UAT, **not fixed here** — out of this row's file list and it changes an operator-facing line. Worth a row. ⚠ **The mirror direction is still PR 5's** per owner decision `D28`, and today's mixing is pinned by `APlaybackStartedUnderAHigherPrioritySourceStillMixes_TODAY` — **update that assertion when you build the queue, do not delete it.** ⚠ **`PHN-1c`'s review had moved the ground under the plan**: `TearDownAsync` no longer does its own teardown and *is* reachable outside `_gate`, so Task 5 was re-planned rather than pasted; and `Current` now RETAINS the terminal snapshot, so every `Assert.Null(service.Current)` the plan prescribed would have been wrong. Running §0.3's re-check list first is what caught both.

The plan proposed `D27`, which is **wrong and already taken** by the `prefers-reduced-motion` decision of 2026-09-02. **The mirror direction ships in PR 5, not PR 4**, on the owner's own reasoning turned back on the queue: he rejected refusing because *"press play, get an error, nothing happens"* is the punch list's tier (b) shape, and **a waiting state nobody can see is that same complaint for longer** — visibility is PR 5's `/hubs/audio` broadcast plus PR 6's chip, so the queue goes where its visibility goes. PR 4 pins today's mixing as a characterization test instead, so PR 5's queue arrives as an edited assertion rather than a silent behavioural shift. ⚠ **`INTEGRATIONS.md:566` is now cited BY CONTENT here and in the plan, because the line number has been wrong in three successive documents.** Three merged plans instruct *"do not touch `INTEGRATIONS.md:566`"*; **`:566` is a code fence in the RotaryPhone hub runbook and has never held the ducking claim.** The claim was at `:932` before #556 — and the banner immediately below this one, written the same day, records its new position as `:980`, which is **also wrong**: `:980` is inside a Python example, and the claim is at **`:997`** at `b77ffe12`. **A number that is wrong three times running is a citation-format defect, not three typos.** Both places now quote the sentence and name the file, with a note saying why the number was dropped. ⚠ **THE PUNCH LIST'S §7 DECISION REGISTER WAS MISSING TWO OF ITS OWN ANSWERS AND DOUBLE-BOOKING A THIRD.** §7 describes itself as *"no longer a list of questions — it is a record of answers"*; it was short two. **`D6`** (tone is out in full — cited as CLOSED in that document's own header since 2026-08-19) and **`D27`** (`prefers-reduced-motion` keeps the shipped sweeping ring) were both referenced in the header and, for `D27`, in the §6 parked table — and **neither had a register entry at all.** Both are filed. **`D26` was carrying two unrelated decisions**: the straight-sided knobs (2026-09-02, Designer Rev 5 §9.5) and the eSpeak removal (2026-09-03, `TTS-9`). **The knobs decision is renumbered `D29` and the eSpeak one keeps `D26`** — because the eSpeak number is cited from [ADR-029](../design/decisions/2026-08-03-gv-audio-through-engine.md) and `design/CONFIGURATION.md`, and an ADR citation is the one that must not be allowed to rot. Every file that used the old number keeps it greppable next to the new one. ⚠ **§9's P1 tier count is corrected 37 → 38 listed; *34 open* was right by coincidence and is unchanged.** `XR-1` is ✅ resolved by `D17` but was being counted open, and `XR-6` (new 2026-09-03) was absent from the cell entirely — the two errors cancelled exactly. The full arithmetic is in the PR description so it can be checked rather than trusted.

> **Prior entry — 2026-09-04 (Builder):** **`PHN-1c` shipped ✅ as [#556](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/556). ADR-029 PR 3 is in: `EventPlaybackService`, the six `/api/audio/events` routes, and both arms. This is the first PR of the phone arc a user can reach.** ⭐ **The find of the cycle is a test that could not fail.** The deadlock invariant — `PlaybackCompleted` is raised *synchronously* from inside `StopCoreAsync`, which `TearDownAsync` calls **while `_gate` is held**, so an `OnSourceCompleted` that waited on `_gate` deadlocks the appliance on every user stop — was documented in two places and **tested nowhere**, because the test fake never raised on `StopAsync`. Proven in both directions: with the fake fixed and the deadlock injected the run **hung and was killed at 90 s**; with the old silent fake and *the same deadlock present*, **39/39 passed in 3 seconds**. ⚠ **A resource leak found by reading committed code, not plan text:** `TearDownAsync` could only release a source the playback already *owned*, which it did not for the whole of acquisition — so a stop mid-fetch released nothing and the finished source was dropped undisposed (on the RemoteMedia arm, an open `FileStream` that also blocks `GvMediaCache`'s evictor on Windows). Fixed with single-owner handover (`TryAdopt`/`ClaimSourceForRelease`); the adjacent ducking→play window is **closed**, not argued benign, because PR 4's preemption lands on it. ⚠ **The plan contradicted itself and Task 9 was the correct half:** `Current` now **RETAINS** the terminal snapshot, because the `202` shape leaves no response to carry an acquisition failure — so `204` means *"nothing started since boot"*, not *"nothing playing"*, and a transport call against a just-completed id answers **409, not 404**. Ten tests failed on this before it was spotted; `IEventPlaybackService.Current` carries a `<remarks>` so the shipped summary did not silently become false. **PR 5 must know: seed `AudioStateStore` from `/current` reading `state`, not nullness.** ⚠ **Three plan defects worth not rediscovering.** (1) The plan's engine check used `Enum.TryParse` alone — which **accepts numeric strings**, and `TTSEngine` is numbered from 1, so `engine: "0"` would have passed validation and thrown later; `Enum.IsDefined` is required and is proven at the route. (2) The plan's C-25 fixture used `Speed`/`Pitch` of `1.0f`, **identical to `TTSParameters`' own initializers**, so it would have passed against the exact bug it exists to catch; it now uses `1.15f`/`0.85f` and was falsified. (3) Task 13's gate 1 (`grep "AddSource"` must be 0) **contradicts Task 4's own prescribed comment** — the substantive gate is *no call site*, and there is none. ⚠ **`TTS-9` had made parts of this row false and the row is now corrected in place:** the eSpeak argument-injection item is **discharged, not re-filed** (`GenerateESpeakAsync` no longer exists; filing a hole that does not exist costs a future session a cycle), and the `TTSParameters` trap is **two fields wide, not four** — `Engine`/`Voice` are nullable since #548, only `Speed`/`Pitch` still pin. The surviving class question is **`SEC-5`** and belongs to Planner. ⚠ **`INTEGRATIONS.md:566` does not exist as three merged plans claim** — `:566` is a code fence; the ducking-priority claim PR 4 owns was at `:932` and this PR's own insertion moved it to `:980`. **Cite it by content, never by line number.** ⚠ **A real residual filed, not fixed:** the *real* `TTSFactory` and `TTSEventSource` log the utterance — one of them **in full** — and since `LOG-11` that is private SMS text at rest in the log file. The seam itself is clean; `design/FUTURE-WORK.md` § *TTS seam* item 5 has it. **UAT was live, not claimed:** 202 `Preparing` → `Failed`/`MediaTransport` through `GET /current` with no network, all four 400s returning **named** reasons, and the raw media id appearing in **no** log line — only the mask `gvm:3886337d`. **Not verified and not claimed: that a voicemail is audible.** That needs the box and is PR 6's, along with `./data/gvmedia` writability, which is **re-carried** because `GvMedia:Enabled` still ships `false`.

> **Prior entry — 2026-09-03 (Builder):** **`ENC-20` shipped ✅ as [#555](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/555). The first defects the owner found with his own hand on the panel — and one of them was a safety justification whose arithmetic was wrong by a factor of two.** ⭐ **`ENC-17`'s harness paid for itself on its first real job.** It measured **4 volume points per detent before and 1 after**, and a per-event clamp of **12 points before and 4 after** — the first points-per-detent figures in this repo that came from a measurement rather than from arithmetic. Every prior encoder row reasoned about this number; the owner's report is what that cost. ⚠ **The wrong claim, so nobody re-derives it as new:** `RotaryEncoderConfigDefaults` promised *"a maximum slew of 6 points per 80 ms"* and *"silence to full takes at least 1.33 seconds"*. Real figures: **12 points**, **0.67 s**, and **0.33 s** at the fastest rate tier 2 actually fires at. It was **not a typo** — handoff §5.4's table computes points as `multiplier × 2`, true only at `step_size 1`, and **`ENC-11` [#509](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/509) shipped `step: 2` while inheriting that justification**. Code and spec disagreed from that commit onward and nothing mechanical noticed. ⚠ **Two corrections now recorded wherever the claim lived** (code, handoff Rev 8, punch list, `HANDOFF-NEXT-SESSION`, `INTEGRATIONS.md`, the harness, the tests): **a tier threshold is a maximum interval, not the rate the user turns at**, so no rate-based floor follows from it — the honest bound is points **per detent**, and any figure in seconds must name the spin rate it assumes. ⚠ **The boot window is now strictly *safer*, not merely nicer:** a factory `×50` event was clamped to 12 points and is now clamped to 4. `VolumeClampUnverified` stayed at `2` and thereby *tightened* — it bounded 4 points, it now bounds 2. **Do not raise it to 4 to "match" the new clamp**; that would equal the normal clamp and destroy the tightening while still being classified, reported and tested. ⚠ **The dim-wake fix is NOT an `ENC-6` regression** — `ENC-6` owns the `/sleep` model at the **30-minute** boundary; this was the separate **5-minute** dim, which it correctly never touched. Encoder input is a SignalR push and **never becomes a DOM event**, so `idle-dimmer.js` could not see it. The input is **not consumed** (deliberately unlike ENC-6 Rule 1), and the two stay separate **by construction**: `Sleep.razor` is on `EmptyLayout`, so `MainLayout` is not mounted on `/sleep`. ⚠ **A UAT-instrument trap worth not rediscovering:** wrapping `window.radioSleepManager.wake` to count calls records **nothing** if any encoder input happened earlier in the page's life — Blazor caches the resolved JS function on first `InvokeVoidAsync`, so a later wrapper is never reached. The first run showed an empty log against a screen that had visibly undimmed. Reload, wrap, *then* turn. ⭐ **The harness also under-reported the real knob by exactly 2× until this row**, because `turn 0 1` emits one *unit* while VOLUME's `step_size` was 2 — and its own docstring claimed the designed config used `step_size 1`. A mechanical instrument existed and still missed the defect a hand found in seconds. Verified on `radio` at `6b2e580`: read-back `Configured` with **0 fields disagreeing**, `flash = MatchesCurrentDesign`, and the real encoder rebound with no harness residue.

> **Prior entry — 2026-09-03 (Builder):** **`TTS-9` shipped ✅ as [#548](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/548). eSpeak is gone, and with it `SEC-4`, `TTS-7` and the root of `TTS-3`.** The owner chose removal over remediating `SEC-4` in place: comparable effort, three rows closed instead of one guarded. `GenerateESpeakAsync` — the `$"-v {voice} …"` interpolation reachable **unauthenticated** and capable of an arbitrary file write via `-w <path>` — no longer exists, along with `IsESpeakAvailable`, `GetESpeakVoicesAsync`, the `TTSEngine.ESpeak` member and `TTSOptions.ESpeakPath`. **`O11` is recorded as DISCHARGED, not deleted**, because it is the record of a hazard that existed and how it was retired. ⚠ **Three things a future session must not rediscover as bugs.** (1) **There is now no TTS at all without network** — eSpeak was the only `IsOffline` engine. Accepted trade-off, owner decision `D26`: announcements are smart-home events, so if the network is down the events are not arriving either. (2) **The eSpeak defaults did NOT become Google.** Several were defaults *precisely because* eSpeak needed no API key, so substituting a cloud engine would have converted them into defaults that fail with no key configured. `TTS:DefaultEngine` / `DefaultVoice` are now **empty**, and an unset or unknown value **throws naming the valid set** — explicit selection over a silent substitution. `ParseEngine`'s silent ESpeak fallback (the `TTS-1` failure shape: the console announces nothing and reports success) now throws as well. (3) ⚠ **The enum is deliberately numbered `{ Google = 1, Azure = 2 }` — do not renumber it from 0.** `ESpeak` was the zero value; letting the remaining members renumber would make `default(TTSEngine)` silently equal `Google`, a cloud engine reached by accident rather than by choice. `TTSParameters.Engine` is now `TTSEngine?` so "unspecified" is representable and resolved once — which is the cleanup **ADR-029 §9.3 had already flagged as "the root cause"**, so **`PHN-1c` branches cut before 2026-09-03 must rebase onto it.** A regression test pins the numbering: `Assert.False(Enum.IsDefined(default(TTSEngine)))`. ⚠ **The stored `TTS:LastEngine = "ESpeak"` was left in place deliberately and needs no migration** — it is a `string` with **zero readers**, and nothing parses it into a `TTSEngine`, so it binds harmlessly. Verified on the box rather than assumed: startup clean, Settings page clean, `tts:eSpeakPath` now orphaned and inert. ⭐ **One new row, `SEC-5`, found by this cycle's own pre-merge pass and not by the reviewer:** `BuildAzureSsml` interpolated the caller-supplied voice id raw into `<voice name='{voice}'>` while carefully escaping the *text* one line below, so the **same unauthenticated route** could break out of the single-quoted attribute with `engine=Azure`. The one-line escape shipped here — **falsified before being trusted**: reverting it makes the new test fail with `</voice><voice` present in the rendered document. **The allow-list decision is `SEC-5` and belongs to Planner.** `SEC-4` closed the *instance*, not the *class*, and the very next engine having the same defect shape is the evidence. Deployed and verified on `radio` at `5e571b8`: the engines endpoint lists only Google and Azure, and a Google announcement was confirmed **in the file sink** — not by its `200`, which proves nothing while `TTS-2` remains open by design. ——— **Appended 2026-09-03 by the tracking audit, because the entry above does not carry these and a reader stops at the newest banner.** ⚠ **The encoder arc is COMPLETE — all 12 encoder P0s have shipped**, so no `ENC-*` row below is claimable as new work; `ENC-17` (input injection via `/dev/uhid`), `ENC-18` (the `ENC-0` presence seam) and `ENC-19` (the firmware re-flash check) are P1 follow-ups, not part of the arc. **P0 remaining is 2, both in the phone arc** — `PHN-1` (PRs 3-7 of seven) and `PHN-2`; **`PHN-1c` is the claimable row.** ⚠ **`ENC-15`'s gate FAILED, so panel blanking is withdrawn permanently — do not reinstate it.** ⚠ **Roughly half of every encoder row's UAT could not be run** (there is no software path to inject encoder input); every row recorded that as an uncovered gap rather than a pass, which is why `ENC-17` exists. ⚠ **"0 warnings" has never been true of this tree, and the number depends on the command.** `dotnet build RadioConsole.sln -c Release --no-incremental` produces **53** `IDE0011` warnings across **15** files — which **confirms** the figure this banner has carried, measured 2026-09-03 at `5e571b88`. ⚠ **Without `--no-incremental` the same commit reports 30 across 13**, because MSBuild skips up-to-date projects and does not re-emit their analyzer warnings — so always quote the number with the command. All 53 are deliberately exempted from warnings-as-errors at `Directory.Build.props:6-7`; a warning of any other class still fails the build. **The honest local test gate is "adds no new failures", not "zero failures"**: four `SrcVariableResamplerTests` fail on every Windows dev machine because the native library is `libsamplerate.so.0`. See punch list `TEST-5` / `TEST-6`.

> **Prior entry — 2026-09-02 (Builder):** **`ENC-6` shipped ✅ as [#539](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/539). The knobs no longer act on a screen showing a clock.** Sleep is now three states — `Awake` / `Ambient` / `Standby` — derived from `IsSleeping` plus a new `IsSleepScreenVisible` that `Sleep.razor` reports about itself through `POST /api/system/sleep-screen`. That single change also collapses the two `/sleep` states into one: reached by the 30-minute idle timer the server previously knew **nothing at all**, so every knob acted normally and invisibly overnight. ⚠ **The blanking half did NOT ship and must not be reinstated** — `ENC-15`'s gate failed on the box. ⚠ **The plan's `_currentValuePublishers` literal was wrong and would have shipped the exact bug its own test exists to catch.** It spelled the **pre-`ENC-5`** order `[Volume, Tuning, Source, Viz]`; the merged tables are `[Volume, Source, Viz, Tuning]`, so following it verbatim would have put a TUNING readout on the SOURCE band. Worse, **the plan's four-array guard would not have caught it** — it asserted only `card.EncoderIndex`, which every publisher threads through from its own parameter, so it reads correctly no matter which publisher ran. Pre-merge review caught that; the test now pins the **label** per index and was falsified against the plan's ordering (3 of 4 fail) before being reverted. ⚠ **Two more plan/tree mismatches worth carrying forward:** `SystemControllerTests` is an `IClassFixture<CustomWebApplicationFactory<Program>>` HTTP class, not the unit class the plan assumed — its `CreateController`/`CreateSleepService` helpers do not exist — and **"0 warnings" has never been true of this tree**: `main` builds with **53** pre-existing `IDE0011` warnings across 15 files. The warning set was captured before and after and is byte-identical, so the honest gate is "adds no warnings", not "zero". ⚠ **Recorded as follow-up, not fixed:** `IsSleepScreenVisible` lives in memory on `radio-api`, so an API restart while the kiosk sits on `/sleep` silently reinstates this row's own headline defect until something re-renders — and **every deploy restarts `radio-api`**. `design/FUTURE-WORK.md` §7 carries it with the mechanism that closes it; `SetSleepScreenVisible` was made edge-triggered on this row specifically so that heartbeat cannot wipe an in-flight wake claim.

> **Prior entry — 2026-09-02 (Builder):** **`ENC-7` shipped ✅ as [#541](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/541). The encoder remap is COMPLETE: `0 = VOLUME · 1 = SOURCE · 2 = PRESETS · 3 = TUNING`**, matching the escutcheon and what `ENC-11` has pushed to the device since #509. Verified live on the appliance — `GET /api/integrations/encoder/mapping` read `"Cycle visualization mode"` for index 2 before the deploy and `"Preview a saved preset"` after, and the Settings page's *“does not match the cabinet”* banner is now empty. ⭐ **`ENC-5`'s five shared artefacts were consumed UNCHANGED** — the seven-row window and the `SelectorNotice` phase it built speculatively both worked as delivered, and the two new overlay tests passed against the shipped component with no edits. ⚠ **Four defects worth not rediscovering.** **(1)** `EncoderLongPressGesture` raises `HoldStarted` on the **press-down edge**, not at the 300 ms ring threshold — so publishing a hold card for a selector knob replaced the 440 px overlay on *every* press until the press resolved. A hold phase is not a selector phase; index 2 now publishes the start edge only, and only while the list is not already up. **(2)** `.encoder-hud-ring` lived **inside** `EncoderHud`'s volume branch, so a PRESETS hold card — which carries no volume — would have drawn no ring at all **with a fully green suite**. Same class as `ENC-5`'s `IsKnownPhase` trap and `ENC-8`'s undeserializable page. **(3)** ⛔ **`VisualizationModeService` now has NO WRITER.** Removing the router's dependency removed the last caller of `CycleMode`/`ToggleEnabled`, so `ModeChanged` cannot fire and the `VisualizationModeChanged` broadcast plus `VisualizerPanel`'s subscription are unreachable. **The plan's claim that the on-screen picker and a System Config dropdown drive that service is FALSE** — `VisualizerPanel.SelectMode` mutates a private Web enum and saves a preference, and that Settings tab is FFT size / smoothing / peak-hold with no mode dropdown. The capability survives on the picker; the cross-circuit sync does not, and the knob was the only thing that ever produced it. Nothing was deleted — recorded in `FUTURE-WORK` §17 as **`ENC-9`'s to resolve**, which makes `ENC-9` (P1, still unqueued) more load-bearing than the plan assumed. **(4)** `RadioPreset.GetDefaultName` formats `{frequency:F1}` while `RadioPreset.Frequency` holds **hertz**, so any caller letting it generate a name gets `FM - 101500000.0`; `AddPresetAsync` reaches it on any blank name. Logged, not fixed — shipped `public static` with other callers. ⚠ **The plan was also wrong that there are 7 preset slots** — `MaxPresets` is 50 globally, there is no `SlotNumber` anywhere, and the ordinal is re-derived per request; “never overwrites” is free by construction. ⭐ **The `MEMORY → PRESETS` rename is a DECLARED deviation** from [`HANDOFF-saved-station-display.md`](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-saved-station-display.md), now recorded in a fourth place — a note at the head of that handoff itself. **Polisher must not revert it.** ⚠ **UAT gap, declared not hand-waved:** Test Plan sections A–H need a hand on the physical knobs and no agent can inject a HID report (`cafe:4005` is hidraw-only with no evdev node, there is no encoder-event seam in the API, and `/dev/uhid` needs root). **C4 — recall from Bluetooth, the plan's own highest-weighted check — is therefore unverified on hardware** and is the first thing to try by hand.

> **Prior entry — 2026-09-02 (Builder):** **`ENC-12` shipped ✅ in [#535](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/535). `ENC-11`'s safety response finally has a voice:** an amber/red fault badge on the Settings nav pill that is correct on every route, and one Radzen notification per browser session on escalation only. A healthy boot stays completely silent. ⚠ **The defect worth not rediscovering: the badge seeded only from change-only SignalR broadcasts, and that made it dead exactly when it mattered.** On a deploy `radio-api` and `radio-web` restart together, so the API's boot config push broadcasts the tier while the web hub is still in its connect-retry loop — the broadcast lands on nobody, the cache stays null, and the badge never appears for the life of the process. Pre-merge review caught it; the fix pulls `GET /api/integrations/encoder/provisioning` (`ENC-8`'s endpoint, no new one) and treats broadcasts as the update channel, not the source. **Verified live: the tier changed before the browser existed and the badge + toast still appeared.** ⚠ **`AudioStateStore` had never been constructed in its life** — zero consumers in `src/Radio.Web`, so its hub cache had never once run. Anything else that plans to "read the cached state" from it needs a consumer first. ⚠ **How to induce the fault tiers on hardware, so nobody re-derives it:** set `RotaryEncoderConfigDefaults` TUNING `max: 99` → `max: 0`; the firmware validates `min >= max` and rejects the **entire** config atomically, so the device keeps its flashed (correct) config and the only mismatch is that one non-safety field → **`Degraded`**, with the knobs still behaving correctly. Then `PUT /api/integrations/encoder/reverse/{i}` → the rejected push also disagrees on a safety field → **`HardFault`**, live, with no redeploy and no lost browser circuit. `sudo sh -c 'echo 3-2.3:1.0 > /sys/bus/usb/drivers/usbhid/unbind'` (and `bind`) gives a genuine mid-session disconnect. ⚠ **A `Degraded` console has its volume clamped exactly as hard as a hard-faulted one** — `VolumeClampFor` returns the tight 2-unit clamp for every tier except `Configured`, while handoff §7.6's table shows it only on the Hard-fault row. The toast copy is spec-verbatim and was **not** changed; the spec defect is logged for Planner in `design/FUTURE-WORK.md`.
>
> **Prior entry — 2026-09-02 (Builder):** **`PHN-1b` shipped ✅ as [#534](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/534). ADR-029 PR 2 is in: `GvMediaClient`, the bounded LRU cache, the API-side `X-RotaryPhone-Auth` handler, and Radio.API's `GvMedia` config block.** Still no endpoint and nothing that plays a sound — PR 3 is the first PR a user can reach. ⚠ **Pre-merge review found 3 HIGH and 5 MEDIUM, and the two worst were in the plan's own literal code.** (1) `File.WriteAllBytesAsync` wrote straight to the final cache path, so a cancelled fetch left a **0-byte file that `TryGetPath` served forever** — and because every hit touches `LastWriteTimeUtc`, the poisoned entry became most-recently-used and was the **last** thing eviction removed. Self-preserving, and PR 3 will pass `HttpContext.RequestAborted`, so a kiosk reload was enough to trigger it. Now staged to `.tmp` + `File.Move(overwrite: true)` under the write lock. (2) The client's `try` covered only `GetAsync`, so **body-phase failures escaped the taxonomy entirely** — a mid-body reset threw raw `IOException`, and `HttpClient.Timeout` elapsing during the body (the *common* case under `ResponseHeadersRead`) threw a bare `TaskCanceledException`; neither carried a `Reason`, bypassing C-18 for the likeliest failure and making the class summary's *"failures throw … carrying a reason"* false. ⚠ **C-14 was half wrong and the box settled it.** The plan says the deploy copies **one** per-machine overlay to both services, so the auth key is *"two keys in one file"*. Measured on `radio`: `api/appsettings.Production.json` is **1057 B, mtime 2026-03-05**, `web/`'s is **75 B, mtime 2026-07-31** — different files, long diverged, and the `test -f` seed guard means neither is re-seeded. So it is **two keys in two files**, setting it is **two** hand edits plus two restarts, and `GvMediaStartupCheck`'s *"both services load the same file"* justification was corrected before merge. What C-14 got right: `src/Radio.API/appsettings.json` **is** replaced wholesale every deploy (rsync excludes only the Production overlay — confirmed live: the deployed file's mtime was that day's deploy, the overlay's six months older), so the non-secret `GvMedia` defaults do reach the appliance. ⚠ **The boot check cannot detect the failure it was written for** — it fires only on an *empty* `AuthKey`; two differing non-empty keys, which is what a mismatch means and the only state producing the 401, warn about nothing. Now stated in the code and pinned by a test rather than left as prose. 🎁 **First container guard in the repo with eager validation** (`ValidateOnBuild` + `ValidateScopes`) — but note honestly that `AddHttpClient<T>` registers the typed client through a **factory**, so `ValidateOnBuild` cannot introspect its constructor; the real constructor exercise is the `GetRequiredService` call beside it. ⚠ **"ADR-029 §17" does not exist** — the ADR's last numbered section is **§15**. The prior entry below cites "§4.2/§17" for the truncation instruction; §4.2 is real, §17 is not, and `MaxSpeechChars` ships as **rejection** deliberately (`PHN-3` owns visible client-side truncation). 📝 **Filed `PHN-5`:** `PhoneContactLookupService` logs raw phone numbers on **four** lines — including `:62` at **Information**, with the contact's real name, into the file sink that persists since `LOG-11` — and the one line it calls masked still logs `contact.Name` in full. ADR-029 §5.1 cites that file as the masking *example*, which is how it propagates. Latent today (its only caller sits behind `PhoneIntegration:Enabled=false`) but one config flip from live. ⚠ **No UAT was run and none was owed** — this PR has no route, no UI and no sound. What *was* verified live: `radio-api` boots with the new registration and `GvMediaStartupCheck` emits its Warning. **Carried to PR 3:** the real gvbridge route's status codes and whether it sends `Content-Length` — the size bound takes a different branch either way, and only the live service settles it. **Still PR 6's:** `PHN-1a`'s three device-only checks.
>
> **Prior entry — 2026-09-02 (Builder):** **`PHN-1a` shipped ✅ as [#528](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/528). The head of the seven-PR phone arc is in, and the ADR claim that scoped it was false.** ADR-029 §8.3 calls D4 *"a lift rather than an invention"* because `FilePlayerAudioSource` *"already implements seeking through `SoundFlowPlaybackService`"*. **Both halves are false**, re-verified against the tree: `SoundFlowPlaybackService` had **no seek method of any kind** across its complete 20-member public surface, and `FilePlayerAudioSource.SeekCoreAsync` assigns `_position = position` — the same field `Position` reads back — under an `IsSeekable => true`. So PR 1 **built** the primitive instead of lifting one; `GetPosition` had **zero callers repo-wide**, confirmed independently, so the fix carried no blast radius. `FilePlayerAudioSource` is **not** fixed — logged to `design/FUTURE-WORK.md` §14a with its own UAT requirement, and it is a candidate row of its own. ⚠ **Pre-merge review found 2 HIGH and 4 MEDIUM, and 7 of the 14 findings were defects in the PLAN, not the implementation.** Both HIGHs were in the plan's own literal code for `AudioFileEventSource`'s completion wait: a `CancellationTokenSource` disposed outside the lock that a waiter was about to read `.Token` from (`ObjectDisposedException` → `Error` state), and `SignalTransportChange()` firing *before* the base class assigns `State`, so the re-armed wait read the pre-transport state — which would have fired `EndOfContent` **while paused**, the exact defect the mechanism was written to prevent. A MEDIUM found the plan's `ValidateMediaId` deny-list defeated by a **scheme-bearing** id (`http:evil.example`): under RFC 3986 a relative reference carrying a scheme resolves as **absolute**, so `new Uri(base, id)` in PR 2 would have escaped the base — an SSRF hole inside the validator written to prevent one. All fixed; an allow-list backstop was added **after** the named checks so every pinned rejection reason is unchanged. **All four `DuckingServiceCharacterizationTests` passed as written, so PR 4's premise is intact** — ducking is still binary and transition-only, and priority still changes no duck level. **Update those tests in PR 4, never delete them.** ⚠ **No browser UAT was run and none was owed** — PR 1 ships no user-visible surface. Three device-only checks (that `Seek` repositions a real MP3, that `Time` advances, that a paused TTS source no longer reports completion) are carried to PR 6; all three degrade benignly. **PRs 2-7 remain unplanned and need a Planner pass each.** Two obligations recorded on them: PR 2 must **mask on every line** (ADR §5.1's cited example masks on one and leaks on two) and must add the first build-and-resolve DI guard in this arc; PR 3 must mint its **own** `playbackId` (`IAudioSource.Id` and `AudioFileEventSource._playbackId` are not equal, while `TTSEventSource` uses `Id` directly) and should settle whether `IEventAudioSource.SeekAsync` widens to `Task<bool>` so a player-refused seek is visible at the seam. Also for Planner: `MaxSpeechChars` ships as a **rejection**, while ADR §4.2/§17 specify **truncation** — the plan governed, but PR 3 should make that call explicitly.
>
> **Prior entry — 2026-09-02 (Builder):** **`ENC-8` shipped ✅ in [#527](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/527).** The encoder Settings surface is live: five cards under System Config → Integrations → Rotary Encoders, the mapping table now served from the router's own dispatch array, and nine false statements corrected in `design/INTEGRATIONS.md`. **`ENC-12` is unblocked and is the next row** — its toast copy ends *"Open encoder settings"*, and that destination now exists. ⚠ **Three defects worth not rediscovering.** **(1) Task 4's hardware gate caught a HIGH regression and is the reason it exists**: unifying the boot and maintenance read-back paths deadlocked the boot push by timeout — the read loop is now the only reader, so awaiting the push before the loop starts means nothing can complete it. It does not hang, it settles in `Degraded`, which tightens the volume clamp to 2 units per event on **every boot**. **(2) The page could never deserialize its own API response** — `Radio.API` serializes enums as strings and the Web DTO enums had no converter, so every card sat on a spinner. **No automated gate can catch this class**: the bUnit rig fails every HTTP call by design, so a null result is the expected state and a green suite is indistinguishable from a dead page. `ApiModels.cs` already documented the identical bug on `ConfidenceBucket`. **(3) Radzen renders only the selected tab's body**, so every markup-absence assertion about a non-default tab passes vacuously unless the test proves the panel rendered first. ⚠ **Two plan claims were false and are corrected in the PR**: `Radio.Web` *can* reference `Radio.Core` statics (the cabinet names are no longer duplicated), and the DTO enums do *not* cross the wire as ints. ⚠ **`Reset counters` copy deviates from the plan deliberately** — it said counters were *"zeroed on the device"*, which the protocol cannot confirm, two lines under a comment saying exactly that.

A seek on the file player reports success, moves the readout, and changes nothing audible. **That is a fourth instance of the `CLAUDE.md` § Pre-Merge Review comment-accuracy class, and `SoundFlowPlaybackService.GetPosition`'s `// Position tracking not available in current SoundFlow API` is a fifth** — `ISoundPlayer.Time` and `bool Seek(TimeSpan, SeekOrigin)` both exist in the restored SoundFlow 1.4.1. PR 1 therefore **builds** the primitive rather than lifting one; the estimate holds. **`FilePlayerAudioSource` is deliberately not fixed in this PR** — it is a live primary-source path whose persisted resume position hangs off the same field, so it needs its own UAT; it is logged to `design/FUTURE-WORK.md` by the plan's Task 13 and is a candidate row of its own. ⚠ **Read the plan's §5 before claiming PR 4 later:** every ADR claim about the ducking subsystem is independently confirmed (`GetActiveEventsByPriority` and `StopAllDuckingAsync` have zero non-test callers; `GetPriority` is read only inside the dead one; `DuckingStateChanged` fires only on the transition), so PR 4's premise is sound — but it is the first load-bearing use of dead code, and `PHN-1a` ships characterization tests specifically so PR 4's change to it is **visible in PR 4's diff**. Those tests are to be **updated, never deleted**.

All three are fixed in #519 with a regression test each. 🔵 **One deviation is left for the owner and is NOT decided:** under `prefers-reduced-motion` the progress ring keeps sweeping rather than becoming handoff §6.5's "filling bar" — defensible, since freezing it hides that a 600 ms hold is arming standby, but undeclared in the plan's §0.3 table. ⛔ **The router index→handler remap was deliberately NOT done** and still belongs to `ENC-5`/`ENC-7`; a test pins the current mapping so the change has to be made on purpose.

> **Prior entry — 2026-09-02 (Planner):** **`ENC-8` and `ENC-12` queued 📋 as a sequenced pair**, planned together in one pass because they were about to build the same card twice. **`ENC-8` owns the entire encoder Settings page** — all four cards, the field-level `Sent` vs `Read back` table, and every action button; **`ENC-12` adds no markup to that page**, only the cross-route topbar badge and the once-per-session notification, and it links to `ENC-8`'s page. **Claim `ENC-8` first**: `ENC-12`'s toast copy ends in *“Open encoder settings”*, and shipping the sign before the destination teaches the owner the link is useless. **Five findings Builder must not rediscover.** **(1)** **A reversed knob will become a permanent hard fault with a permanently tightened volume clamp** unless the `reverse` override reaches the *verifier* and not only the wire — `reverse` is a safety field, `Classify` turns a safety mismatch into `HardFault` immediately, and `VolumeClampFor` then drops volume movement from 6 units per event to 2. `ENC-8` §2.3, with a test. **(2)** **`ENC-8` Task 4 is a hardware gate, not a formality** — four later tasks assume a `stream.Write` from a request thread reaches the device while the read loop is parked in an infinite `ReadAsync`. If it fails, **stop and report**; do not switch to a finite `ReadTimeout`, which the shipped code already rejected in writing because HidSharp may surface the expiry as `IOException` and tear the connection down once per timeout forever. **(3)** **The `SystemConfigPage` mapping table is no longer wrong** — quick win #6 ([#489](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/489)) corrected the text, so do not “fix” it; what survives is the second source of truth, and that fix is structural. **(4)** **There is no MudBlazor in this repository** despite `CLAUDE.md` saying so — `Radio.Web` references `Radzen.Blazor` and nothing else; `ENC-8` Task 20 corrects `CLAUDE.md`. **(5)** **`MainLayout` cannot be rendered in bUnit** — its test file is a documented stub, and nothing in `tests/` asserts `.topbar-mute-chip` or `.phone-nav-fault` in markup. `ENC-12` therefore puts every decision in a pure `EncoderFaultRules` class the way `BellHealthRules` already does. **Eleven contradictions between the punch list, the handoff and the shipped code are resolved in the two plans' §0.4 sections; five of them are statements that are false today.**

> **Prior entry — 2026-09-02 (Planner):** **`ENC-5` and `ENC-7` queued 📋 as one planning pass producing two sequenced PRs.** The punch list is explicit that these are *"one component with two lists … build them together or back to back; building them apart is how they drift"*, so `ENC-5` builds the shared selector component and `ENC-7` consumes it unchanged. Plans: [`ENC-5-source-overlay.md`](../design/plans/ENC-5-source-overlay.md) (18 tasks) and [`ENC-7-presets-knob.md`](../design/plans/ENC-7-presets-knob.md) (11 tasks). **`ENC-4`'s implementation landed on `main` at `29acc01` while these plans were being written**, so `ENC-5` is claimable as soon as that row is marked ✅; `ENC-7` is hard-blocked on `ENC-5`. `ENC-5` edits four `ENC-4` files, so **Builder should re-read them as merged before Task 1** — where the plan and the shipped code differ, the code wins and the PR should say where. ⭐ **The router remap belongs to these two rows and is sequenced so the tree is never in a state where a knob points at a handler that is not there:** `ENC-5` takes indices 1 and 3 and parks the visualiser on index 2 as a seat-warmer (leaving the old source cycler there would give two adjacent knobs two divergent copies of the source selection); `ENC-7` takes index 2. End state `0 = VOLUME · 1 = SOURCE · 2 = PRESETS · 3 = TUNING`, closing the deliberate mismatch recorded in `HANDOFF-NEXT-SESSION.md`. ⚠ **`ENC-4`'s mapping test goes red in `ENC-5`, and `ENC-5`'s goes red in `ENC-7` — both by design.** Each plan says which assertion moves and forbids chasing the red by reverting the remap. **Six findings from planning that Builder must not rediscover.** (1) ⚠ **There are no 7 slots in the preset bank** — the cap is **50**, `RadioPreset` has no `SlotNumber`, the ordinal is re-derived per request, and there is no next-free-slot logic because gaps cannot exist. The design survives intact; `ENC-7` §0.3 has the mapping. (2) **No tuner reports which bands it supports** — `IRadioControl` has no capability member and `GetAvailableBands()` is device-agnostic, so "composition resolves once per tuner" is unimplementable as written; `ENC-5` adds `SupportedBands` with a default interface implementation so the existing test double keeps compiling. (3) **Per-band last-tuned frequency does not exist** — `RadioPreferences` holds one flat `LastFrequency`, and today a band switch clamps the *previous* band's frequency into the new band, landing FM→AM at 1710 kHz every time. It gets **its own configuration section**, because `RadioPreferences` and `RadioOptions` both declare `SectionName = "Radio"` and that section already has two writers with different field sets. (4) **Recall cannot use `POST /api/radio/presets/{id}/load`** — it 400s when radio is not the active source, which is exactly the case the PRESETS knob exists to serve. (5) **`RadioControlPanel` already subscribes to `RadioStateChanged`**, so the "knob and band pills are one state" requirement is met today and the task is a regression guard, not a rebuild. (6) ⚠ **`ENC-9a` did not remove `VisualizerPanel`'s local `_currentMode`** — the punch list cites it as the template for exactly that and it added the missing *subscription* instead; the real template is "one authoritative owner + typed broadcast + a component that re-syncs without echoing". ⭐ **`ENC-7` carries a declared, deliberate deviation forward:** the on-screen bank becomes `PRESETS · n saved`, recorded in Rev 3's header, Designer §4.4 and punch-list §6 so a later consistency pass does not revert it — **Builder puts it in the PR body; Polisher does not flag it.** **One addition to the spec, surfaced rather than silently resolved:** holding PRESETS on a station already saved currently throws a duplicate error the handoff never covers, so `ENC-7` adds `ALREADY SAVED · slot NN`.

The row adds a dedicated push channel with its own ≥50 ms coalescer; that does **not** contradict the "do not add a second throttle" note in `HANDOFF-NEXT-SESSION.md`, which is about `VolumeChanged` and stays true. (2) **The Sleep-variant HUD is observable today, but only on the idle path.** `idle-dimmer.js:73-81` navigates to `/sleep` *without* calling `SetSleepAsync(true)`, so `IsSleeping` is false there and a knob turn acts and renders in place; reached via the Sleep pill it is true, the input is consumed by the wake, and the browser leaves the route — so UAT via the pill would produce a false failure. (3) **The handoff's “reuse `RadioControlPanel.LongPressThresholdMs`” is not literally possible** — it is a private const in a Razor component in `Radio.Web`, while the synthesis has to run in `Radio.API`. The plan promotes the value to `Radio.Core` and repoints the component at it. ⛔ **The row does NOT remap `RotaryEncoderActionRouter`'s index→handler table** — that stays with `ENC-5`/`ENC-7`, and the plan adds a test pinning the current mapping so a later change is deliberate.

> **Prior entry — 2026-08-18 (Builder):** **`KIOSK-2` shipped ✅ as [#480](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/480). The kiosk tranche is clear.** Verified on the live box across four `setup-kiosk.sh` runs, reading the installed copies under `/usr/local/bin` rather than the checkout: the healthy path is **silent** (`TIER=silent`, no dialog), a stopped `radio-web` was repaired by one tap with the kiosk up at **4 established connections** and the dialog reading *"The console wasn't running — it's started now."*, a hard failure **held the kiosk back** (dialog up, kiosk `inactive`, **0 processes, 0 connections**) with `Try again` producing a genuine second run and `Open anyway` then launching it, and the shutdown confirm sat untouched for **90 seconds** — GNOME's own powers off at 60 — leaving `uptime -s` unchanged. **The Google Voice bridge stayed at 13 processes with CDP `:9224` answering at every step**, including across `Exit to Desktop`, which took the kiosk 10 → 0. **⚠ Two things this row found that the plan had wrong, both worth carrying forward.** (1) **The accidental-shutdown mitigation was not actually being enforced.** Clearing the persisted DING icon positions works — and DING rewrites them moments later, so the one-shot unset races a writer that always wins. `keep-arranged true` is the setting that makes it stick, and it is now set alongside `arrangeorder 'NAME'` (which was **already** `'NAME'` and had never applied, because `keep-arranged` was `false`). **The live layout before this row genuinely had `Shutdown System` and `Exit Browser` adjacent** with `Radio Console` at the far end — the exact arrangement handoff §2.1 exists to prevent, so this was not a hypothetical risk. The durable framing, since the sort *direction* proved unstable across installs: `Radio Console` is the **middle of three**, and the middle of a three-element sort is the same element whichever way the sort runs — so any future rename must keep it sorting between the other two, not merely preserve `E… < R… < S…`. (2) **`--icon-name` being dropped from zenity 4 cost nothing.** `PROBES.md` framed it as a forced choice between the right glyph and two live buttons; zenity 4 accepts **`--extra-button`** on the stock types, so `--error --ok-label="Try again" --extra-button="Open anyway"` renders the red glyph **and** both buttons in the mockup's own order. **Handoff §6.1 shipped exactly as designed** — no glyph traded, no button dropped, no design decision needed. (`--icon` still parses and is **silently ignored**: the image node is byte-identical with a valid name, a bogus one, and none at all.) **⚠ One of the two "owner's eyes" checks was retired by finding a better instrument, and it generalises well beyond this row: AT-SPI works on this box.** `python3-gi` + `Atspi` expose every dialog's widget tree, **rendered label text, screen extents in pixels, and a working `click` action** — on Wayland, with no screenshot tool. That is how the 56 px buttons became a measurement (**58 px as shipped**; GTK 4's default is 44 px, the plan's `min-height` alone overshoots to 76 px, and `GTK_THEME=Yaru-dark` adds the last 2 px) and how `Try again` / `Open anyway` / `Cancel` were actually pressed. `CLAUDE.md` currently records this box as having **no** remote UI driving at all; that is now too strong. **Only icon legibility at the panel still needs the owner.** **⚠ The 24 px inter-button gap is NOT shipped and could not be** — GTK 4's message-dialog action area keeps a 1 px hairline under three different selectors. Its intent is met by a wider margin than the spec asked for (~444 px buttons, aim point ~222 px from the seam against ~100 px in the spec's model), and it is flagged for the owner rather than quietly worked around. **⚠ `T15` could not be observed live and is not claimed as if it were:** across 17 samples spanning two full refresh cycles the maximum `psidtsAgeSeconds` was **477**, i.e. the bridge is currently refreshing on a **~10-minute** cadence and never entered the 660–1200 trough at all. The trough is verified by **direct threshold testing** instead — 17 synthetic bodies including the exact body captured during a confirmed outage (`psidtsAgeSeconds: 707`, all three liar fields healthy) classifying as `online`/silent. **Do not tighten the `>1200` test to match today's ~500 reset**; a shorter cadence makes `>1200` more conservative, and matching the observation would misreport a dead session the moment the 20-minute cadence returns. **Five pre-merge findings were fixed and two of them made a tool lie:** `--print-status` reported `TIER=silent` while a service was down (`compute_tier` had no `down` arm) while `--dry-run` said `WOULD-START` about the same run; the RotaryPhone-owned ensure script was the one **unbounded** call, so a hang would have wedged the launcher with a `--no-cancel` dialog up and the `flock` held, killing the icon until a reboot (now `timeout 20`); and `· started just now` was being claimed for units that were already running but slower than the 2 s probe. **⚠ Also closed, and it explains a latent trap: `.gitignore`'s `[Bb]in/` was swallowing `deploy/debian-x64/kiosk/bin/`** — `KIOSK-1`'s scripts are in the repo only because they were force-added, and the next helper would have shipped to the box but never to the repo. **Merging alone changes nothing — `setup-kiosk.sh` must be re-run on the box; that has already been done on `radio`.** **Gates:** build 0 errors, `bash -n` clean on all five scripts, and 5 test failures that **reproduce identically on `main`** (4 × `SrcVariableResamplerTests`, a native `libsamplerate` P/Invoke absent on Windows; 1 × a real external NWS API call) — this branch changes no C# at all, which is the `TEST-1` false-signal problem in miniature.

`--timeout` / `--default-cancel` / `--width` / `--height` supported but **`--icon-name` is NOT** — zenity 4 dropped it, so per-tier iconography in `KIOSK-2` must come from the built-in `--info` / `--warning` / `--error` glyphs; rotary unit is **`rotary-phone.service`**). Recorded in [`docs/uat/2026-08-18-kiosk-launcher/PROBES.md`](uat/2026-08-18-kiosk-launcher/PROBES.md) so `KIOSK-2` does not re-derive them.

> **Prior entry — 2026-08-18 (Planner):** **two rows out of one approved design handoff, and the split between them is the main judgement in this pass: `KIOSK-1` (the kiosk launch contract) and `KIOSK-2` (the three-icon repair-and-open desktop).** The handoff is [`HANDOFF-kiosk-desktop-launcher.md`](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-kiosk-desktop-launcher.md), now **`[APPROVED 2026-08-18]`** with all five §11 questions answered in its new §12 — **none of it is to be re-brainstormed.** **⚠ Why two rows and not one, since this arrived as one approved scope.** `KIOSK-1` is an **ops-correctness** row that stands entirely on its own: `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1:376` relaunches the kiosk with `DISPLAY=:0` on a **Wayland** box — a defect its own comment at `:369-374` names and declines to fix — **so every deploy leaves the screen dead**, which the owner hit live today. Its fix is objectively verifiable end-to-end with no UI at all, and it is also the **single riskiest change in the body of work** (a brand-new Chrome profile), which is exactly why it should not be entangled with ~600 lines of new dialog shell. `KIOSK-2` is the **UX** row. Different failure mode, different verification, different fix shape — the same test this file already applies when it keeps `AUD-6`/`AUD-7` apart. **⚠ THE ORDERING BETWEEN THEM IS LOAD-BEARING AND IS NOT A PREFERENCE: claim `KIOSK-1` FIRST.** Every piece of `KIOSK-2` — the scoped exit, the single-instance guard, the launcher — depends on the dedicated `--user-data-dir` existing. Landing `KIOSK-2` first would force matching the kiosk on the **absence** of a flag, which the design explicitly rejects as fragile, and would leave `Exit to Desktop` still able to kill the Google Voice bridge. `KIOSK-2` is therefore **blocked** until `KIOSK-1` merges. **The verified replacement, so nobody re-derives it:** `systemd-run --user --collect google-chrome --kiosk … --ozone-platform=wayland …` inherits the graphical session's environment because the transient unit is started by that session's own service manager. **Confirmed by 2 established connections to `:5002` afterwards — and that count is the meaningful liveness check, not process existence: during the 2026-08-02 outage Chrome was running and `radio-web` returned 200 for ~33 hours while the count was 0.** **⚠ One finding is worth more than either row and should not be read as a side effect: nothing has ever installed the repo's `.desktop` files to `~/Desktop`.** The in-tree `radio-console.desktop` has carried `--password-store=basic` since Aug 11; the live copy still does not. **Three separate instances of that same drift turned up in one day** — the flags, the missing icon, and the 775 mode bit — and they are one root cause, not three bugs. Closing that loop is a first-class goal of `KIOSK-1`. **⚠ A fourth instance of the comment-accuracy pattern, recorded here rather than as its own row.** `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1:146-154` explains, correctly today, why it clears `~/.cache/google-chrome/Default/Cache` and `~/.config/google-chrome/Singleton*`. A `--user-data-dir` profile keeps **both inside itself**, so `KIOSK-1` makes that comment describe paths the script no longer touches **and** silently clears the wrong cache while leaving the kiosk's own lock in place. Written onto the row as a required hunk. **⚠ Recorded so it is not "finished" by a well-meaning Builder: the in-app Blazor favicon (`App.razor:9`) is OUT OF SCOPE.** The owner initially approved it, then **reversed on evidence**: `--kiosk` hides the tab strip, address bar and title bar, so that favicon is **never visible on the appliance** — it would only ever show in a remote tab or a bookmark, which does not justify carrying the dark-surface and 16–32 px legibility risks inside a launcher cleanup. Deferred to its own pass (handoff §12, Q4). **The *desktop* icon is unaffected and fully in scope** — that is the one the owner actually taps and the one that fixes the generic-document-glyph complaint. **⚠ Cross-repo boundary, and it is narrow on purpose:** `gv-bridge-ensure.sh`, its watchdog timer and its nightly restart timer are **RotaryPhone-owned** and a Builder is bringing them under version control in `D:\prj\RotaryPhone` **right now**. `KIOSK-2` **invokes and probes only** — never owns, reimplements, edits, installs or `pkill`s bridge startup — and resolves the script from a candidate list at runtime because its canonical path may move under it. The new-consumer note for the boundary doc's Change Log is a commit in *that* repo, so per the 2026-08-10 precedent it is § Cross-repo handoffs **#8**, not a claimable row. **Also folded in and already decided:** `Exit Browser` → `Exit to Desktop` (Q1); a shutdown confirm that **never auto-proceeds**, replacing GNOME's 60-second countdown that powers the box off on inaction (Q2, overriding the earlier “leave as-is”); the **stateless `psidtsAgeSeconds > 1200`** dead-session test with **no Architect escalation** (Q3 — owner confirmed the field live at `310`); and the four reported components stand at four (Q5). **Placement: appended at the end of the Queue. `TEST-1` stays first — the owner set that — and no existing row moved.** But flagging rather than acting on it, per the standing rule that Planner appends and the owner ranks: **`KIOSK-1` fires on *every* deploy and costs a dead screen each time**, which is a stronger case for promotion than anything else currently below `TEST-1`. Say the word and it moves. **One task in the plan is a gate, not work: Task A0 probes the box and pins four unknowns** — zenity's GTK major version (which decides whether the 56 px CSS goes in `gtk-3.0/` or `gtk-4.0/`), `--timeout` / `--icon-name` / `--default-cancel` support, whether `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` is present in `systemctl --user show-environment` (which the whole relaunch fix rests on), and the `rotary-phone` unit name. **Several later choices branch on its output; it must run first and its answers get committed as `docs/uat/2026-08-18-kiosk-launcher/PROBES.md`.** _Nothing in the entry below is superseded — this pass adds rows and notes only._
