| GV-6 | **Distinguish `409 markread_disabled` from a genuine mark-read failure.** ADR-024 §3.3 (amended 2026-07-31) documents the dark-state `409` RotaryPhone returns when their `GVBridge:EnableMarkRead=false`. Our client maps **every** non-2xx to `null`, so "the feature is switched off" is indistinguishable from "GV is unreachable." Degrades acceptably today — no crash, no wrong badge, next list fetch is authoritative — so this is **diagnostic quality, not correctness**. Fix: in `GvBridgeApiService`'s two mark methods, detect `409` + `markread_disabled` and treat it as a distinct outcome — log **once** at Warning (not per call, to avoid journald churn on the N100) and latch a flag that suppresses further calls until restart, rather than re-POSTing on every mark. **No user-visible affordance** — mark-read is silent-by-design (ADR-024 §6); this is for operators. Matters in exactly one state: config skew where ours is on and theirs is off. | 📋 | _plan TBD (small)_ | [ADR-024 §3.3](../design/decisions/2026-06-20-gv-mark-read-durable-readstate.md) | **GV-4** | `fix/gv-markread-dark-409` |
| GV-7 | **Render non-dialable SMS senders (short codes + opaque sender IDs).** The **display** counterpart to GV-5's send-side reply-ability gate (ADR-028 §8). Roughly a third of inbound SMS comes from senders that are not phone numbers — **numeric short codes** and **opaque 36-char sender IDs** — and the texts surface was designed assuming a dialable counterparty throughout. Scope: **thread-list row** (what occupies the name line when there is no name and the identifier is 36 chars — truncation, `--font-mono`?, avoid layout blowout at **1920×720**); **conversation header**; **contact resolution** — `ContactResolutionService` resolves number→name and by definition **cannot** match a short code or opaque ID, so it must degrade gracefully rather than render an empty or misleading name; and the **bubble/meta** treatment for these threads. **Critical constraint Builder confirmed: there is NO `fromName` anywhere in the GV payload** — only counterparty identifiers — so display names can come **only** from local contact resolution, which structurally cannot resolve these senders. Design for "identifier is all we will ever have." Reuse GV-5's `GvCounterparty.Classify` (do not write a second classifier). Composer behavior is **GV-5's** — this row must not re-decide it. **Live observations now EXIST — design against them, not assumptions.** The prior "⚠ LIVE OBSERVATIONS INCOMING — do not design blind" warning is **retired**: the [2026-07-31 UAT](uat/2026-07-31-gv-live-data/REPORT.md) observed this surface against real GV data, and its § "Findings for GV-7" (**G-1 … G-9**) is the input. **Read it before starting.** The findings that actually change the design: **G-1 — the "2" in `Texts 2` is an UNREAD COUNT, not a thread count. There are 20 threads.** Design against a 20-row list, not the 2-row list an earlier session assumed. **G-3 — zero opaque 36-char sender IDs are reachable from this surface**: the feed has no pagination or load-more control, so it caps at 20 threads and older threads are unreachable through the UI. That case is therefore **untested against real data, NOT proven absent** — do not conclude the corpus lacks them. **G-4 de-risks the layout concern this row was written around:** a synthetic 36-char ID was injected and measured at 1920×720 — it **fits with no ellipsis and no page overflow** in both the thread-list row and the conversation header (`document.scrollWidth` stayed exactly 1920 at every length tested), so **no truncation strategy is needed for layout safety**. Also in scope from the pass: **G-6** — when no name resolves, the two-line header renders the **identical string on both lines** (`.texts-conv-name` over `.texts-conv-number`), confirmed for all 14 unresolved threads; **G-8** — MMS previews embed a sender prefix (`+1XXXXXXXXXX - <text>`), i.e. a *second* phone number inside a row that already displays a counterparty; and **F-3** — the composer is **rendered-but-disabled with no stated reason** (no tooltip, no helper text, empty `title`). Whether to hide it or label it is **a design decision for this row, not a prescription** — the UAT flagged it deliberately without deciding. **Amended 2026-07-31 — that decision now has one more context than it had when this row was written.** GV-8 shipped an **error state** on this pane, and the GV-8 UAT's `O-2` observed the composer **still mounted behind it**: under a confirmed 502 the pane renders `cloud_off` + "Couldn't load messages." + `Retry` *and*, below it, a live `Message` field with `Send`. It is **inert** (`SendEnabled=false` is honoured — same as F-3), so this is **not a new defect and deliberately gets no row of its own**; it is routed here because it is the same composer decision, not a second one. But whatever this row decides — hide, label, or leave — now has to hold in **three** states rather than two: non-dialable counterparty, genuinely empty thread, and **failed load**. The third is the weakest case for leaving it: inviting a reply into a conversation the app has just admitted it could not read. **G-5 is a PASS and needs no work** (resolution degrades gracefully — the raw identifier is used as the name, never blank, never wrong) **but recalibrates the priority: 14 of 20 (70%) of live threads render an identifier rather than a name**, so the fallback is the **common** case on this surface, not an edge case. | 📋 | _plan TBD (design-led; consult the Designer handoff first)_ | [ADR-028 §8](../design/decisions/2026-07-30-gv-sms-send-contract.md) · [handoff Screen C/D](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-phone-messages-voicemail-sms.md) · [**UAT § Findings for GV-7**](uat/2026-07-31-gv-live-data/REPORT.md) | **GV-3** (texts surface). _The "wait for observations" caveat is **discharged** — they exist and are linked in the row. Coordinate with GV-5 if both are in flight: GV-5 adds `GvCounterparty` to `ApiModels.cs` and this row consumes it. **Coordinate with GV-8 too, and prefer GV-8 first:** GV-8 rewrites the conversation pane's state branches (`PhoneTextsPanel.razor:36-68`), which this row also touches — and designing the header/empty treatment on top of a pane that **cannot express "failed"** would bake the F-1 confusion into the new design._ | `feat/gv-messages-pr7-nondialable-senders` |
| GV-8 | **Distinguish a failed conversation load from an empty one (the silently-empty texts pane).** UAT **F-1** (HIGH): opening a text thread renders **"Start the conversation below."** with **no error, no spinner, no retry** — a 502 and a genuinely empty thread are indistinguishable on screen, and the failure is invisible to browser instrumentation (0 console errors, 0 failed requests — the page is Blazor Server, so the fetch happens server-side over SignalR). **Root-caused, confirmed by source:** `GvBridgeApiService.GetSmsThreadMessagesAsync` (`src/Radio.Web/Services/ApiClients/GvBridgeApiService.cs:204-218`) wraps `GetFromJsonAsync` in `catch (Exception) { log; return null; }`, and `GetFromJsonAsync` calls `EnsureSuccessStatusCode()`, so **every non-2xx, timeout and deserialization error collapses to `null`** with no status distinction; then `PhonePage.razor:632` does `_openThreadMessages = messages?.Messages.ToList() ?? new()`, after which the two states are **byte-identical**. No `_openThreadError` field exists. **Scope:** (a) make the API client express *outcomes* (result type / status) instead of a bare `null`; (b) add `_openThreadError` / `_openThreadLoading` page state and pass `Loading` + `Error` through `PhoneMessagesPanel.razor:184-191`, which today passes **neither** although **both parameters already exist** on `PhoneTextsPanel` (`PhoneTextsPanel.razor:172-173`) — this is also why the UAT saw **no spinner**: the skeleton branch is unreachable dead code in this hosting; (c) add the missing **error branch** to `PhoneTextsPanel.razor:36-68` (today exactly three branches — skeleton / empty / list, **no error branch at all**), reusing the **F-6** pattern verbatim from `PhoneMessagesPanel.razor:110-117` — `cloud_off` + **"Couldn't load messages."** + `Retry` — a string already specified at [`HANDOFF-phone-dark-theme-and-scrollbars.md:310`](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-phone-dark-theme-and-scrollbars.md); (d) fold in UAT **F-2** (MEDIUM) — the "Start the conversation below." copy must appear **only** for a genuine empty. Today it fires on failure *and* invites a reply into a hard-disabled composer (`SendEnabled=false`), which on a short-code thread is doubly wrong. **The fix template already exists one level up:** the thread list does this correctly — `PhonePage.razor:595-616` sets `_threadsError` (and toasts "Showing the last update" when a cached list exists) and `PhoneMessagesPanel.razor:110-117` renders `cloud_off` + `Retry` off that flag. **This is mostly copying an already-designed pattern down one level.** **Test gap is explicit:** `tests/Radio.Web.Tests/Services/GvBridgeApiServiceVoicemailSmsTests.cs:81` asserts **only** the happy path — add a non-2xx case there **plus** a bUnit case asserting the pane renders the **error** state, not the empty state. **Do NOT attempt a client-side escaping workaround for the group-thread `%2F` bug** (that is RotaryPhone's — § Cross-repo handoffs #5): both double-escaping (`%252F`) and a raw `/` were tested and both fail, the latter falling through to their SPA fallback and returning `index.html` with HTTP 200. | ✅ **PR #461** | [`plans/.../gv-texts-load-error-state.md`](superpowers/plans/2026-07-31-gv-texts-load-error-state.md) | [UAT F-1/F-2](uat/2026-07-31-gv-live-data/REPORT.md) · [root-cause § Defect C](uat/2026-07-31-gv-live-data/F-1-DIAGNOSIS.md) · [handoff error-state copy](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-phone-dark-theme-and-scrollbars.md) · [**GV-8 UAT — 10/10, 0 HIGH/MEDIUM**](uat/2026-07-31-gv8-error-state/REPORT.md) | **GV-3** (texts surface: `PhoneTextsPanel`, `PhoneMessagesPanel`, `PhonePage` thread-open path). _**SHIPPED 2026-07-31.** Verified against a **genuine upstream HTTP 502** during a natural auth blackout (`psidtsAgeSeconds` 707), not a synthesised failure: the pane rendered `cloud_off` + "Couldn't load messages." + a working `Retry`. C7 confirmed the opposite failure mode is **not** introduced — a genuinely empty group thread still reads as **empty**. **One user-visible behavioural delta, by design (Task 6): a failed thread open no longer marks the thread read** — the unread dot is restored rather than silently cleared for a conversation the user was never shown, and the durable write-through is skipped (which also avoids 2-3 more upstream Google calls inside a dead auth window). A **successful `Retry` then performs the read-marking the failed open skipped**, so the dot does not linger once the conversation actually renders. A *successful* load followed by a *failed* mark-read still keeps the optimistic flip and reconciles later — ADR-024 §2, deliberately unchanged. Pre-merge review found 0 HIGH / 3 MEDIUM and a Polisher pass 1 HIGH / 1 MEDIUM / 1 LOW; all seven fixed. Two LOW deferred with justification (skeleton shimmer amplitude — a design-token question spanning every skeleton in the app; and a deploy-script runtime default), triaged 2026-07-31 into **UX-1** (shimmer amplitude — new row) and **OPS-1 (c)** (the deploy runtime default — folded, not standalone); a third item Builder flagged forward, the missing thread-list `== null` guard, is folded into **GV-9**. **⚠ ONE THING IN THIS PR IS SHIPPED BUT NOT LIVE-VERIFIED — `M-1`. Do not record it as proven.** Polisher's M-1 fix is the `Error && Messages == null` ordering (`PhoneTextsPanel.razor:61`) — *arriving content outranks a stale error flag*, so an inbound SMS landing on an open-but-failed thread renders the message rather than staying on `cloud_off`. It is **unit-covered** (`PhoneTextsPanelTests:141` — `Conversation_ShowsMessages_WhenErrorSetButMessagesArrived`) but was **never observed live**: it needs a **real inbound SMS to land while a failed thread is open**, no inbound traffic arrived during the UAT window, and the Tester **correctly declined to inject a synthetic message** rather than manufacture a pass. What *is* established live is the branch ordering around it (skeleton → error → empty → list, all four observed). **It is cheap to close opportunistically and needs no cycle:** text the GV number during a blackout (`psidtsAgeSeconds` 660–1200) with the failed thread open, and watch the pane. Also tracked in § Dependency / ordering notes. **Explicitly NOT blocked on RotaryPhone.** Their two defects (§ Cross-repo handoffs #5/#6) make this failure **rare**; this row makes it **honest**. Neither subsumes the other — shipping only theirs would leave a surface whose only defence is that the backend seldom fails. **Same bug class as GV-6** (every non-2xx → `null`) and deliberately kept separate; the reasoning is in § Dependency / ordering notes._ | `fix/gv-texts-load-error-state` |
| GV-9 | **Texts-surface polish: overflow hardening + unread-row alignment.** Two LOW findings from the same UAT, both pure CSS on the same surface, grouped into one cycle. **F-4** — `.texts-conv-number` computes to `white-space: normal; overflow: visible; text-overflow: clip`, while **both** its siblings (`.list-item-title`, `.texts-conv-name`) carry `nowrap` + `overflow: hidden` + `text-overflow: ellipsis`; beyond ~60 characters it clips **mid-character** instead of ellipsizing. _Honest severity: **no live data triggers this**, and a 36-char opaque ID does **not** (measured at 1920×720, UAT G-4). This is consistency hardening, not a fix for an observed break — do not let it be re-filed as a bug._ **F-7** — unread rows sit **20px** out of alignment with read rows, because the `<span class="unread-dot">` is a sibling placed *before* `.list-item-identity` and therefore **displaces** the text rather than occupying a reserved gutter (unread name line x=**251px**, read x=**231px**). Every row jumps horizontally the moment it is marked read — newly visible now that GV-4 wired mark-read. Fix is a reserved-width gutter that holds the text steady in both states. **Third item, FOLDED IN 2026-07-31 out of the GV-8 cycle — the thread-list branch never got the `== null` guard that GV-8 shipped in conversation mode.** `PhoneTextsPanel.razor:162` is a bare `else if (Error)`; the canonical form on this surface is `Error && <collection> == null` — `PhoneMessagesPanel.razor:110` (threads) and `:78` (voicemail) — and GV-8's M-1 fix shipped exactly that one branch over at `PhoneTextsPanel.razor:61`. **Consequence if the branch ever goes live:** a stale error flag would outrank thread rows that had actually arrived — the same lie GV-8 was written to remove, just one level up. **Builder deliberately did not fix this in PR #461, and was right not to:** it was not in Polisher's findings, and the branch is **dead in production** — the panel's only call site (`PhoneMessagesPanel.razor:184`) mounts it solely under `_openThreadId != null`, i.e. conversation mode, which the file's own comment already records. It was flagged forward to this row instead. **Fix it here, because the file argues for it in its own words:** the comment above the dead skeleton markup says it is *"kept in sync so it isn't the next thing someone copies."* That is the project's stated policy for this exact branch; the guard falls under it. **Two consequences, stated so they are not a surprise mid-cycle.** (1) **This row is NO LONGER CSS-only** — it becomes CSS plus a one-line `.razor` condition, so budget a `Radio.Web.Tests` run rather than a visual check. (2) **Correction to the deferral note this arrived on:** the behaviour is *not* covered by pre-existing tests — `PhoneTextsPanelTests` sets `Error` only with `OpenThreadId` present (`:124`, `:141`, `:197`), and the three thread-list-mode tests (`:32`, `:41`, `:68`) never set it. It is **unasserted in both directions**, so the fix must **add** an assertion, not preserve one. **Why folded here rather than a standalone row:** GV-9 is already the row that owns this surface's polish and its dead-copy sync — F-7's reserved gutter has to behave in all three `.unread-dot` sites (`PhoneMessagesPanel.razor:663` live, `PhoneTextsPanel.razor:191` dead, `VoicemailRow.razor:10`), and the dead one sits **29 lines below** the bare `else if`. A separate row would be a third queue entry against the same file for a one-line change in unreachable code. | 📋 | _plan TBD (small; **no longer CSS-only** — two CSS fixes plus one `.razor` guard and its missing test)_ | [UAT F-4/F-7](uat/2026-07-31-gv-live-data/REPORT.md) · [GV-8 UAT (guard provenance)](uat/2026-07-31-gv8-error-state/REPORT.md) · [handoff](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-phone-dark-theme-and-scrollbars.md) | **GV-3** (texts surface). _No external dependency; claimable now. GV-8 is merged, so the guard's canonical form (`PhoneTextsPanel.razor:61`) is on `main` to copy from — read it before writing the thread-list version rather than re-deriving it. Same surface as GV-7: if both are in flight expect `PhoneTextsPanel` and the thread-list row markup to have moved._ | `fix/gv-texts-polish-overflow-unread-align` |
| GV-10 | **Confirm-or-close: do conversation bubbles render the list snippet instead of the full message body?** UAT **F-5** (LOW) — **UNPROVEN. This row may legitimately close with no code change.** In the `32665` conversation a bubble ended in a literal `...` matching the truncated list preview, suggesting the conversation view renders the snippet rather than the full body. **Plausible mechanism, explicitly not proven:** RotaryPhone derives per-thread messages by **filtering the whole SMS folder list** (`GvSmsClient.ListMessagesAsync`) rather than calling a per-thread Google endpoint, and folder-list entries carry **snippets**, not full bodies. **If that is the cause it is their data, not our rendering, and this row converts into a cross-repo note rather than a Radio Console change.** **First task is confirmation, not repair:** one `curl` for a known long message, compared against the same message in Google Voice. **Two disciplines the confirming call must respect:** (1) run it inside a **healthy window** — see the 20-minute blackout note in § Dependency / ordering notes — or a 502 will masquerade as a result; (2) use a **non-group** thread (e.g. `t.32665`, the original repro) until RotaryPhone's `%2F` decode lands, since group threads return 0 messages for an entirely unrelated reason. _Provenance: the diagnosis recommended waiting for their decode fix before testing. We are **not** hard-blocking on it, because the reproducing thread is not a group thread and is curl-able today — recording the deviation rather than silently departing from the source doc._ | 📋 | _plan TBD (investigate first; scope depends on the answer)_ | [UAT F-5](uat/2026-07-31-gv-live-data/REPORT.md) · [root-cause § Scope-affecting notes](uat/2026-07-31-gv-live-data/F-1-DIAGNOSIS.md) | **GV-3** (texts surface). _Lowest priority of the three UAT rows; do not schedule ahead of GV-8._ | `fix/gv-texts-bubble-full-body` |
| OPS-1 | **Build stamp on `radio-web` + deploy verification for both services.** **Most of this already exists — scope is the gap, not a greenfield build.** `Directory.Build.props` already stamps the git SHA into `AssemblyInformationalVersion` (`_StampGitCommitHash`), `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1` already passes `-p:SourceRevisionId` to **both** publishes and already **fails loudly (`exit 1`) on an API SHA mismatch**, and `Radio.API` already serves `GET /api/health/version` → `VersionInfoDto { GitSha, GitShaShort, InformationalVersion, AssemblyVersion, BuildTimestampUtc, AssemblyName }`. **Two gaps remain, and together they are exactly the 2026-07-30 incident:** (1) **`Radio.Web` exposes no version endpoint at all** — its assembly already carries the correct SHA, it is simply unreadable at runtime; (2) **deploy verifies `radio-api` only** — for `radio-web` it checks `systemctl is-active` and nothing more, so a stale web binary passes verification silently. Add a minimal-API `GET /api/health/version` to `Radio.Web` (matching the existing `app.MapGet("/api/albumart/…")` convention — Radio.Web has no Controllers dir), returning the same shape; extend the deploy verify block to poll `:5002` and fail identically on mismatch. Also document that `BuildTimestampUtc` is file-mtime, i.e. *landed-on-disk* time, not compile time — the SHA is the authoritative signal. **Interim stopgap, proven in the GV-8 UAT — reference it in the plan and keep it documented until the endpoint ships:** grep the deployed web binary for a symbol that exists only on the branch under test — `grep -ac RetryOpenThreadAsync /opt/radio-console/web/Radio.Web` (non-zero → the new binary is live). The Tester used `OpenThreadLoading`, `RetryOpenThreadAsync` and `GvCallOutcome` to confirm freshness by hand, precisely because `systemctl is-active` had reported a healthy service while proving nothing about *which* binary was running. Now also recorded in `CLAUDE.md` § Deployment. **(c) FOLDED IN 2026-07-31 — the script's runtime default does not match the box (GV-8 UAT `L-2`, LOW, but sharp).** `deploy/Deploy-ToLinux.ps1:51` declares `[ValidateSet("linux-arm64","linux-x64")] [string]$Runtime = "linux-arm64"`, while `uname -m` on `192.168.86.50` is **`x86_64`** — so the **literal documented invocation** `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1 -TargetHost 192.168.86.50` publishes and ships **ARM64 binaries to an x64 host**. This nearly cost the GV-8 UAT its validity; the correct call is `-Runtime linux-x64` and the report records it as the rollback recipe. **Fix direction, not prescribed:** probe `uname -m` over the SSH session the script already opens and **fail before publishing** on mismatch (cheapest, and it cannot go stale), and/or derive the default from the target host instead of hardcoding one. **Note the sibling default has the identical shape** — `$TargetHost` defaults to **`piradio`**, which does not resolve from WSL (§ Dependency / ordering notes) — so the real defect is *"the script's defaults describe a box that no longer exists,"* and a `uname -m` guard fixes the half that silently produces a wrong-architecture deploy rather than an obvious connection failure. **Why this is folded here rather than standing alone:** it is the **same file** and the **same failure class** as gap (2) above — *the deploy reports success without proving what it did* — so one Builder cycle should own deploy integrity end to end; a separate row would put two PRs on one script for a ~5-line change. _Splittable back out if OPS-1 slips: it has no dependency on the version endpoint and could ship on its own in an afternoon._ | ✅ **PR [#485](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/485)** | _plan TBD (small; scope is fully specified in this row)_ | [ADR-024 §3.3 amendment](../design/decisions/2026-06-20-gv-mark-read-durable-readstate.md) (context: the incident that surfaced it) · [GV-8 UAT `L-2`](uat/2026-07-31-gv8-error-state/REPORT.md) | — | `feat/ops-web-build-stamp` |
| UX-1 | **Skeleton shimmer amplitude — is a 6/255 gradient delta enough on the dark theme?** **Design-led: get a Designer answer before a plan is written, and accept that this row may legitimately close as "no change."** From [GV-8 UAT `L-1`](uat/2026-07-31-gv8-error-state/REPORT.md). **The shimmer is NOT broken, and this row must not be re-filed as "the skeleton is broken"** — it demonstrably animates, on three independent kinds of evidence: `animationPlayState: running` with `Animation.currentTime` advancing **0 ms → 117 ms**; `backgroundPosition` moving `-200%` → `-174.531%`; and a CDP screencast in which **14.5 % of pixels changed** in the skeleton region between two frames **116 ms** apart, against a **0 %** change in a static control region of the same frame pair (which is what rules out compression noise and global repaint). `prefers-reduced-motion` was `false`, so the `animation: none` override was not in play. **What is marginal is the amplitude.** `.skeleton-loading` (`src/Radio.Web/wwwroot/css/design-system.css:1075-1083`) is `linear-gradient(90deg, var(--surface-raised) 0%, var(--surface-overlay) 50%, var(--surface-raised) 100%)` at `background-size: 200% 100%`, and on this theme those tokens are `#141416` and `#1A1A1D` (`design-system.css:65`/`:67`) — `rgb(20,20,22)` → `rgb(26,26,29)`, a **6/255 stop-to-stop delta**, with **measured peak frame-to-frame change of 3/255**. Side by side the two captured frames read as identical to the eye. **Scope is the whole app, not the texts pane — this is the point of the row.** `.skeleton-loading` is the shimmer primitive every skeleton composes with: `Skeleton.razor`'s seven shapes (NowPlaying, Radio, ListRow, DeviceRow, MetricTile, Visualizer, …) are mounted at **27 call sites across 6 pages** (`DeviceManagementPage`, `PlayHistoryPage`, `QueueHistoryPanel`, `RadioControlPanel`, `RadioPage`, `VisualizerPanel`), plus **38 raw `.skeleton-loading` nodes** in the phone panels. **A one-line token change lands on all of them at once — which is precisely why this is a design-token decision and not a bug fix, and why it was correctly kept out of GV-8.** **Questions for the Designer, none pre-decided here:** should the shimmer highlight get **its own token** rather than borrowing `--surface-overlay`, which exists to be a *surface* and is also used with `backdrop-filter: blur(20px)` (`design-system.css:184`) — i.e. it is not free to move? Is a wider delta still tasteful on a **wall-mounted kiosk in a dark room**, or does it start reading as a flashing band? Does the answer differ per shape — a full-bleed `Visualizer` block sweeping is a much larger moving area than a 16px `skeleton-text` bar. **Constraint any fix must honour:** the `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` override at `design-system.css:1683` sets `animation: none`, so reduced-motion users see the **static** gradient — widening the amplitude must not make that state worse. **Judge it on the kiosk panel, not a laptop LCD:** the numbers above came from frames rendered on the box, and a 6/255 delta is exactly the range where two displays will not agree. | 📋 | _plan TBD — **do not write one until the Designer has answered**; scope depends entirely on whether the answer is "new token," "retune the existing pair," or "leave it"_ | [GV-8 UAT `L-1`](uat/2026-07-31-gv8-error-state/REPORT.md) · evidence: `uat/2026-07-31-gv8-error-state/screenshots/03-c2-frame-a-108ms.png` vs `04-c2-frame-b-224ms.png` | — _(no code dependency; it is gated on a design answer, not on a row)_ | `feat/ux-skeleton-shimmer-amplitude` |
| KIOSK-1 | **One kiosk launch line, and it works under Wayland.** **This row exists because every deploy currently leaves the screen dead, and the script itself documents why.** `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1:376` relaunches the kiosk with `DISPLAY=:0 nohup google-chrome …` on a box that runs **Wayland**, which its own comment at `:369-374` labels a *"KNOWN DEFECT, deliberately not fixed here."* The owner hit it live on 2026-08-18. **The replacement is verified by hand, not proposed:** `systemd-run --user --collect google-chrome --kiosk … --ozone-platform=wayland …`, which starts the browser from the graphical session's *own* service manager and therefore inherits that session's `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` / `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS` instead of an SSH shell's. **Confirmed by 2 established connections to `:5002` afterwards — and that is the meaningful check, not process existence: during the 2026-08-02 outage Chrome was running and `radio-web` returned 200 for ~33 hours while the count was 0.** Scope: a new `deploy/debian-x64/kiosk/bin/radio-kiosk-launch` that becomes the **single definition** of the kiosk command line (three callers converge on it — autostart, deploy, and the desktop icon — which today carry three different flag sets); a dedicated `--user-data-dir=~/.config/radio-kiosk-chrome`, which **pays for itself three times**: it lets `KIOSK-2`'s Exit spare the GV bridge, it **restores CDP on `:9223`** (Chrome ≥136 silently ignores `--remote-debugging-port` on the default profile), and it makes `--password-store=basic` safe **by construction** on a profile that was `basic` from first run — `CLAUDE.md` measured that flag destroying 45 `v11` → 16 `v10` cookies on an existing profile; **`setup-kiosk.sh` fixed to `install -m 755`, NOT `chmod +x`** (with the default umask `+x` yields **775**, and GNOME silently refuses to launch a group-writable `.desktop` — that mode bit is the entire reason the box's `GV-Bridge.desktop` was unlaunchable); the **missing repo→`~/Desktop` installer**, which is the root cause of the whole drift problem (the in-tree `radio-console.desktop` has carried `--password-store=basic` since Aug 11 and the live copy still does not, because nothing has ever installed it — **three separate instances of this drift turned up in one day**); and dropping **`onboard`** from `deploy/provision/packages.sh:88` + the autostart entry, which `docs/uat/2026-08-03-osk-wayland-viability/REPORT.md` already recommended (Chrome 151 on Wayland issues **zero** `zwp_text_input_v3.enable()` calls, so the OS keyboard cannot type into the kiosk at all). **⚠ Two `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1` details that will silently misfire if skipped:** the stop block's cache and `Singleton*` paths point at `~/.cache/google-chrome` and `~/.config/google-chrome`, which belong to the **default** profile — a `--user-data-dir` profile keeps both **inside itself**, so leaving them alone clears the wrong cache and leaves the kiosk's own lock in place; and its comment at `:146-154` describes exactly those paths, so it becomes a **false comment** unless rewritten with the diff (this repo's `## Pre-Merge Review` rule). **⚠ Merge-order: `KIOSK-1` BEFORE `KIOSK-2`, and it is not a preference** — every piece of `KIOSK-2` (scoped exit, single-instance guard, launcher) needs the dedicated profile to already exist; landing `KIOSK-2` first would mean matching the kiosk on the *absence* of a flag, which the design explicitly rejects as fragile. Plan Tasks **A0–A7**; A0 is a **box-probe task that pins four unknowns** (zenity's GTK major, `--timeout`/`--icon-name`/`--default-cancel` support, whether `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` is in `systemctl --user show-environment`, the `rotary-phone` unit name) and **must run first** — several later choices branch on its output. **⚠ PROMOTED above `TEST-1` on 2026-08-18 with the owner's explicit approval, and the reason is the blast radius rather than the size of the fix: this defect fires on **every single deploy** and costs a dead kiosk screen each time, so it taxes every other row in this queue — including `TEST-1`'s own verification, which needs a live panel to confirm anything on the box. `TEST-1` remains the highest-priority row of the 2026-08-10 tranche; this is one row jumping it, not a re-ordering of that tranche.** | ✅ | [`plans/.../kiosk-desktop-launcher.md` § Part A](superpowers/plans/2026-08-18-kiosk-desktop-launcher.md) | [handoff](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-kiosk-desktop-launcher.md) | [#479](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/479) | `fix/kiosk-launch-contract` |
| TEST-1 | **Stop the suite producing false CI signals — four flaky tests, and the ambient-`localhost:5000` defect underneath them.** **Highest priority of the 2026-08-10 tranche.** Four distinct tests failed under timing/load pressure on 2026-08-10 and passed on retry, during a debugging session that was chasing a real audio bug — **the flake was read as a regression and produced a wrong diagnosis before it was recognised as noise.** That is the cost this row exists to remove: a suite that lies intermittently makes every other row's verification untrustworthy. **Three parts, and (c) is the actual fix.** **(a) A bUnit assertion raced across a real `await`.** `ModePicker_ClickingPhase_ActivatesPhaseSegment` (`tests/Radio.Web.Tests/Components/Shared/VisualizerPanelTests.cs:184-193`) clicks the Phase segment and asserts `is-active` / `aria-selected` **immediately**, with no wait for the re-render. `phase.Click()` dispatches `VisualizerPanel.SelectMode` (`src/Radio.Web/Components/Shared/VisualizerPanel.razor:207-229`), which assigns `_currentMode` **only after** `await UnsubscribeFromCurrentMode()` — and that await **completes synchronously when the SignalR hub is disconnected but makes a real network round-trip when it is connected**, which is exactly why this passes locally and flakes on the runner. Fix: wrap the assertions in `cut.WaitForAssertion(...)`. **⚠ This is NOT loosening the assertion — it is the same condition, waited for rather than raced.** Do not re-file it as "the test was made permissive." **Five files in this project already use the idiom — copy one rather than inventing a wait:** `Components/Pages/PhonePageThreadLoadErrorTests.cs`, `Components/Pages/PlayHistoryPageTests.cs`, `Components/Shared/CastDeviceDropdownTests.cs`, `Components/Shared/RadioControlPanelTests.cs`, `Components/Shared/PhoneStatusHeroTests.cs`. **(b) ⚠ MEASURED AND RE-FILED 2026-09-01 — this part was misdiagnosed and is NOT part of this row.** The record described three `Radio.API.Tests` timing out at 30–46 s. Measured: **16–18 tests inflate and none times out** (325 passed / 0 failed both under load and alone), worst case **14.2 s**. Every inflated test is the **first-executed test of a class holding `IClassFixture<CustomWebApplicationFactory<Program>>`** — 17 such classes, 17 entries — so what is measured is host boot, which xUnit bills to the collection's first test. **`Radio.API.Tests` cannot reach the network at all**: every `HttpClient` comes from `_factory.CreateClient()` over an in-memory `TestServer`. Control run of the project alone against 40 CPU-burner processes with `:5000` closed reproduced it exactly (1.05 s → 3.43 s per first-of-class). Cause is **CPU oversubscription** — 11 assemblies as concurrent processes, each defaulting xUnit to all 32 cores. The *"22/22 in ~5 s"* also fails checking: **no class here has 22 tests**, and ~5 s is the whole project's isolation wall time. **Split out as `TEST-3` (P2, bound test parallelism).** Nothing to do in this row. **(c) The underlying defect, and the real fix: unit tests can reach an ambient `localhost:5000`.** A unit test whose result depends on whether `radio-api` happens to be running on the self-hosted runner is the root problem — **(a) and (b) are both symptoms of a test rig that is not hermetic**, and (a)'s whole flake mechanism is "the hub was actually connected." Stub the hub/config clients in the test rig so no unit test can open a socket to the box. **Ordering within the row: do (c) first, or at least alongside.** Shipping only (a) and (b) fixes two instances and leaves the class open — the next timing-sensitive test added will rediscover it. | ✅ **SHIPPED [#483](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/483)** | _(a) done — and widened from 1 test to 3: `Fall` and `Ring` had the identical click-then-assert shape and raced the same await; only `Phase` had lost. (b) measured and **re-filed as `TEST-3`** — not this defect. (c) done: transport seam on all four hub services + an `IHttpMessageHandlerBuilderFilter` covering every `AddHttpClient<T>()`. **Verified with a listener bound to the port during full runs: 74 connections → 0 on :5000, 0 on :5004**, 877/877 Web and 325/325 API. UAT on the live app: all four hub services connected in production config with events flowing. Pre-merge review caught a comment of mine that overclaimed safety — `GetCoverArtUrlAsync` builds its own `HttpClient`, so the stub covered one of two transports; fixed with a second seam plus a regression test proven able to fail._ | _no spec doc — the diagnosis is in this row_ · provenance: the 2026-08-10 "no audio from the soundbar" debugging session (PRs #467 / #468 / #469) | — _(no code dependency; claimable now, and **should be claimed before the other seven** — every one of them is verified by this suite)_ | `fix/test-rig-hermetic-and-flaky-waits` |
| AUD-1 | **Split `UseShazamForAllSources` into the two independent decisions it currently conflates.** **⚠ READ THIS BEFORE TOUCHING THE FLAG: do NOT "fix" the overwrite behaviour by setting `UseShazamForAllSources` to `false`. That turns off BT album art entirely.** One boolean (`src/Radio.Fingerprinting/FingerprintingOptions.cs:19`, surfaced at `src/Radio.API/appsettings.json:91` and `src/Radio.Web/Models/ApiModels.cs:788`) governs two things that have nothing to do with each other. **Decision 1 — "always fingerprint BT" — is NECESSARY and must not change.** The 2026-08-10 investigation established that **AVRCP cannot supply album art on this box**: BlueZ 5.72 ships **no BIP / cover-art implementation**, and 7 days of fingerprint-DB data show **0 AVRCP-sourced art against 2,560 SongRec-sourced**. SongRec is not an enhancement here, it is the only source. Mechanically: the flag feeds the gate at `src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/Sources/Primary/BluetoothAudioSource.cs:837` (`NeedsFingerprintingLookup = hasIncompleteMetadata \|\| FpOptions.UseShazamForAllSources`), and a false gate makes `src/Radio.Fingerprinting/Services/BackgroundIdentificationService.cs:260` (`if (!audioTap.NeedsFingerprintingLookup) return;`) **hard-return** — so whenever AVRCP supplies a title *and* artist, nothing is ever fingerprinted and no art is ever found. **Decision 2 — "let SongRec overwrite AVRCP title/artist/album" — is NOT necessary and is actively wrong.** It rewrites `Enter Sandman (Remastered)` to `Enter Sandman`; AVRCP is the authoritative name for what the phone is actually playing, and SongRec is guessing from audio. **The wanted behaviour already exists in the same method, on the branch that never runs when the flag is on.** `BluetoothAudioSource.OnTrackIdentified` (`:845`) forks at `:867`: **`:867-891` is the overwrite branch** (assigns Title/Artist/Album unconditionally, caches art, logs *"Shazam metadata replaced AVRCP for BT"*, `return`s) and **`:893-905` is the preserve branch** (computes `hasArt` from existing metadata and calls `CacheAndSetCoverArtAsync` **only when art is absent**, never touching title/artist/album). **The fix is to reach `:893-905`'s art-fill behaviour while keeping `:837`'s always-fingerprint gate** — i.e. two flags, not one. **⚠ Caveat that probably forces a per-source split rather than a rename:** the same flag is read by `src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/Sources/Primary/FilePlayerAudioSource.cs:1988` (the gate) and `:2112` (the overwrite, additionally guarded by `needsLookup`), where **ID3 tags are authoritative in a different way** and the current behaviour may well be correct as-is. A single global rename will not serve both sources — expect `FingerprintingOptions` to gain two properties and the config/DTO surfaces above to follow. **Decide deliberately whether FilePlayer's overwrite behaviour changes at all**, and say which way in the plan; silently dragging it along with BT is the failure mode here. _All line numbers verified against `main` @ `c129e0d`; **PR #469 moved this file by ~28 lines**, so pre-merge citations are stale._ **⚠ RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-11 against `main` @ `8b1ce0a` (post-#468), and TWO ANCHORS IN THIS ROW WERE STALE AND ARE NOW CORRECTED: `FilePlayerAudioSource.cs:1968 → :1988` and `:2092 → :2112`.** #468 touched that file (`StopCoreAsync` / `DisposeAsync` resume-position handling, nothing to do with fingerprinting) and pushed everything below `:872` down **+20 lines**. **Everything else in this row re-verified byte-exact and unchanged:** `FingerprintingOptions.cs:19`, `appsettings.json:91` (**checked specifically — #468 added a line to that file and it landed below `:91`, so the citation survives**), `ApiModels.cs:788`, `BluetoothAudioSource.cs:837`/`:845`/`:867-891`/`:893-905` (#468 did not touch that file at all), and `BackgroundIdentificationService.cs:260`. | 📋 | _plan TBD (small-to-medium; the behaviour already exists — the work is splitting the switch and deciding FilePlayer's side)_ | _no spec doc — the diagnosis is in this row_ · provenance: 2026-08-10 debugging session; PR #469 is the adjacent merged fix | — _(no row dependency. **Touches the same file and method region as PR #469** (`BluetoothAudioSource.OnTrackIdentified`), which is merged — rebase, don't re-derive. **Also touches `FilePlayerAudioSource.cs`, which #468 changed on 2026-08-11** — the anchors above are already re-sited, but rebase rather than trusting any earlier copy.)_ | `fix/split-shazam-fingerprint-vs-overwrite` |
| AUD-6 | **Output device identity is an enumeration ordinal, so a persisted output preference silently changes which physical device it means.** **⚠ The diagnosis is complete — do NOT re-brainstorm it. And read the falsification note before anyone "reopens" the 2026-08-10 question; this row is a different claim.** **The mechanism, verified end-to-end against `main` @ `0b2ebcb`.** `SoundFlowDeviceManager` mints output ids straight off the MiniAudio enumeration index — `Id = $"playback-{i}"` (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/SoundFlow/SoundFlowDeviceManager.cs:517`, and the sibling at `:695`), where `i` is the raw loop counter over `playbackDevices` (`:498`). **That string is what reaches storage.** It is persisted to `AudioPreferences:CurrentOutput` from two places — `SoundFlowDeviceManager.SetOutputDeviceAsync` (`:234-238`) and `SoundFlowAudioEngine.PersistActiveOutputAsync` (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/SoundFlow/SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs:425`) — and read back at startup by `AudioEngineInitializationService` (`src/Radio.API/Services/AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:210`). **And resolution back to hardware is pure string parsing, not a lookup:** `SoundFlowAudioEngine.GetDeviceIndexById` (`:1030-1043`) checks the `playback-` prefix and returns `int.TryParse(deviceId.AsSpan("playback-".Length))` — **there is no name comparison anywhere on that path.** So the persistent key *is* the ordinal, and any change in enumeration order, or a device appearing or disappearing, silently repoints the preference at different hardware. **The measurement, on the box across today's deploy restart.** Aug 10 16:17 — `Playback device [0]: "AB13X USB Audio Analog Stereo"`, `[1]: "Built-in Audio Analog Stereo" (IsDefault=True)`. Aug 11 15:04 — `[0]: "Built-in Audio Analog Stereo" (IsDefault=True)`, `[1]: "AB13X USB Audio Analog Stereo"`. The store held **`playback-1`** across both. **Aug 10 that meant the Soundbar; Aug 11 it means USB Audio Out. Nothing in the store changed — the meaning did.** **⚠ It is intermittent by nature and will NOT reproduce on demand.** It surfaced only because today's restart happened to reorder enumeration; a plan that budgets for a deterministic repro will not get one. Reordering can be *forced* for a test (unplug/replug the USB DAC, or restart PipeWire) but that is a hardware exercise, not a unit test. **⚠ THE CODEBASE ALREADY KNOWS ABOUT THIS, AND THE GUARD IT WROTE FOR IT CANNOT FIRE — read this before writing a fix, because the obvious reaction is "there is already a check."** `AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:294-296` carries the comment *"After PipeWire restarts, MiniAudio device indices may shift, causing the wrong device to be selected even though the ID matches,"* followed by a verification block at `:297-330`. **It is a tautology.** Its "expected" value, `preferredDeviceName`, is derived from the ordinal itself (`:258-262`, `outputDevices.FirstOrDefault(d => d.Id == preferredOutputId)`), and its "actual" value is `currentDevices.FirstOrDefault(d => d.Id == selectedId)` (`:300-303`) where `selectedId` is the same id just written at `:286`. **Both sides are the same lookup, by the same key, against the same `SoundFlowDeviceManager` cache** — `outputDevices` is `GetOutputDevicesAsync()` from `:142`, and `currentDevices` is the same call again at `:299` — **so `activeDevice.Name` and `preferredDeviceName` are the same string, and the mismatch branch at `:304` does not fire.** If the ordinal has gone stale, the check confirms the *wrong* device against itself and passes. _Stated precisely, because this row must not overclaim the way the comments it is criticising do: the branch is not unreachable in the strict sense — the hot-plug refresh can rewrite `_cachedOutputDevices` between those two reads, and a **reorder inside that window** would make the two sides differ. That is a narrow, unrelated race, not the restart-scale reordering this row is about, and **it is the only path on which the guard can fire at all.**_ Its repair path is dead for a second, independent reason: it calls `_deviceManager.SetOutputDeviceAsync(correctDevice.Id)` (`:317`), which performs no native switch (see `AUD-7`). **THE STABLE KEY ALREADY EXISTS, IS ALREADY COMPUTED, AND IS ALREADY TRUSTED — this row is largely about promoting it, not inventing one.** `AudioOutput:DeviceDisplay:DeviceFriendlyNames` (`src/Radio.Core/Configuration/AudioOutputOptions.cs:70`, written by `DevicesController.SetDeviceFriendlyName:1016-1021`) is a `Dictionary<string,string>` keyed by **device name**, consumed by `SoundFlowDeviceManager.ApplyFriendlyName` (`:432-451`) on `rawName`. **That mapping survived the reordering correctly** — `"Built-in Audio Analog Stereo": "Soundbar"` and `"AB13X USB Audio Analog Stereo": "USB Audio Out"` resolved to the same physical devices on both days, which is precisely why the friendly names in the live payload are right while the id underneath them is wrong. The raw name is already carried through the domain model as `AudioDeviceInfo.RawName` (`src/Radio.Core/Interfaces/Audio/IAudioDeviceManager.cs:144`, populated at `SoundFlowDeviceManager.cs:519`). **Fix direction — persist a stable identifier (the device name, or something more hardware-derived if the implementer can find one that survives a PipeWire restart), and keep `playback-N` as a transient view-model concern that never reaches storage.** The exact identifier is the implementer's call and should be argued in the plan; name-keying is the cheapest option that is already proven on this box, but it is not obviously the best one — device names are not guaranteed unique, and two identical USB DACs would collide where ordinals would not. **Say which way and why.** **⚠ THREE CONSTRAINTS ON THE FIX, all cheap to miss.** **(1) The key is polymorphic.** `AudioPreferences:CurrentOutput` holds either a device id **or** one of the virtual-output sentinels `google-cast` / `http-stream` (`SoundFlowAudioEngine.SetActiveOutputAsync:204-206`; the startup branch at `AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:226`). A format change must leave those two values untouched and still distinguishable. **(2) There are TWO read paths for this preference, not one, and they do not read the same store.** The startup service reads the **config store** (`:210`) and falls back to `IOptionsMonitor` (`:223`), while `SoundFlowDeviceManager`'s constructor restores from `IOptionsMonitor` **only** (`:115-128`, i.e. `appsettings.json:170`, which ships empty). The asymmetry is already commented at `AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:201-203`. **Both paths must handle a legacy value or the migration is half-applied.** **(3) The API DTO does not carry the raw name** — `DevicesController.MapToDeviceDto` (`:1486-1501`) projects `Id` and the friendly `Name` and drops `RawName` — so surfacing a stable id to the UI is a DTO change plus a `Radio.Web` change, not an infrastructure-only edit. **⚠ MIGRATION NOTE — REQUIRED, and it is the part with real risk.** Every existing store holds an ordinal. **A fix must be able to read a legacy `playback-N` without repointing anyone's output on upgrade** — and note the cruel detail: **on the very deploy that ships the fix, the legacy ordinal may already mean the wrong device**, so "resolve the old ordinal and rewrite it as a name" silently canonises whatever the enumeration happens to be at that moment. Decide deliberately between resolving-and-rewriting, and treating an unmigratable legacy value as *no preference* and falling back to the system default; **the second is more honest and the more defensible default, but it will move one user's output once, so say so in the plan rather than discovering it in UAT.** **⚠ RECORDED SO IT IS NOT REOPENED: this exact fragility was raised as a hypothesis on 2026-08-10 and CORRECTLY FALSIFIED as the cause of the soundbar silence.** The app had opened the right device; the cause was the Cast startup mute, shipped in **#468**. **That falsification stands — do not let this row be read as overturning it.** This row makes a strictly different claim: not *"this caused that outage"* but *"this fragility has now been observed firing,"* which the 2026-08-10 evidence neither established nor excluded. **Test gap, stated because the one test that exists gives false comfort:** `tests/Radio.Infrastructure.Tests/Audio/SoundFlowDeviceManagerTests.cs:188-207` (`SetOutputDeviceAsync_SetsSelectedDevice`) asserts only that `GetSelectedOutputDeviceId()` returns the string that was just passed in — **a bookkeeping round-trip that would pass unchanged even if no audio device were ever opened** — and it silently `return`s (i.e. passes) when the runner has no audio hardware (`:194-198`), which is the same non-hermetic pattern `TEST-1` owns. | ✅ **PR [#499](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/499)** | _plan TBD (medium — the identifier change is small, the **migration** and the two read paths are where the work and the risk are)_ | _no spec doc — the diagnosis is in this row_ · provenance: the 2026-08-11 production deploy; enumeration captured from the `radio-api` journal on both days · the pre-existing name-keyed precedent is `AudioOutput:DeviceDisplay:DeviceFriendlyNames` ([`AudioOutputOptions.cs:70`](../src/Radio.Core/Configuration/AudioOutputOptions.cs)) | — _(no row dependency; claimable now. **⚠ CLAIM THIS BEFORE `AUD-7` — the ordering is load-bearing, not a preference:** `AUD-7` makes startup **act** on this preference, and acting on an unstable key is worse than ignoring it. See § Dependency / ordering notes. **Same files as `AUD-7`** (`SoundFlowDeviceManager.cs`, `AudioEngineInitializationService.cs`, `SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs`, `DevicesController.cs`) — expect anchors to move if both are in flight. **Same root class as `AUD-2`/`AUD-4`** (an unstable identifier used as a persistent key) but **no file overlap with either**, so it can run alongside them.)_ | `fix/stable-output-device-identity` |
| AUD-7 | **The reported active output diverges from where audio physically goes — the output selector cannot be trusted.** **⚠ Diagnosis complete; do NOT re-brainstorm. This is the half that makes the UI lie, and any fix for `AUD-6` that does not also close this leaves a selector that is still wrong.** **The observation, live on the box at `0b2ebcb`.** `/api/devices/output` returned `{"id":"playback-0","name":"Soundbar","isActive":false}` and `{"id":"playback-1","name":"USB Audio Out","isDefault":true,"isActive":true}` — while PipeWire had the application's stream on **sink 57, `alsa_output.pci-0000_00_1f.3.analog-stereo`** (the built-in analog, i.e. the Soundbar) and the USB sink (**17449**) `SUSPENDED`. **Audio was physically reaching the Soundbar while the UI claimed USB Audio Out.** **⚠ THE MECHANISM IS SHARPER THAN "the two concepts are not reconciled at startup" — read this, because it changes the fix.** It is not that two notions drift apart; it is that **the native device switch is reachable only from the interactive HTTP path.** `SoundFlowAudioEngine.SwitchPlaybackDevice` (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/SoundFlow/SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs:838`) is the **only** code anywhere that stops, disposes and re-initializes `_playbackDevice` (`:873-883`), and it has **exactly one caller in the solution**: `DevicesController.SetOutputDevice`, fire-and-forget on the thread pool (`src/Radio.API/Controllers/DevicesController.cs:250-254`; grep-verified — the only other reference to `GetDeviceIndexById` is its own declaration). **The startup path never calls it.** What startup calls instead does nothing native. `AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:286` calls `_deviceManager.SetOutputDeviceAsync(outputToUse)`, and `SoundFlowDeviceManager.SetOutputDeviceAsync` (`:213-244`) **validates the id against the device cache, assigns the `_selectedOutputDeviceId` string, and persists the preference — and touches no audio device.** Then `:337` calls `_audioEngine.SetActiveOutputAsync(outputToUse)`, which is explicit that the swap is somebody else's job: *"The local-device switch (native MiniAudio swap) is the caller's responsibility via `IAudioDeviceManager.SetOutputDeviceAsync` — this gate only owns mute state, virtual-output lifecycle, and persistence"* (`SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs:222-224`). **That comment assigns a responsibility that the named method does not discharge.** It is the same failure class as the three worked examples in `CLAUDE.md` § Pre-Merge Review, and fixing the wording belongs in whichever PR fixes the behaviour. **So the device that is actually open is whatever `InitializeAsync` picked** — the system default, selected at `SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs:504-517` and logged as *"Selected system default playback device at index {Index}"*, which is exactly the line the startup journal shows immediately before `SetActiveOutputAsync: "<none>" -> "playback-1"` and `Local output "unmuted"`. **⚠ CONSEQUENCE THAT SEPARATES THIS ROW FROM `AUD-6`: this defect is DETERMINISTIC, not intermittent.** It fires on **every** restart in which the persisted preference is not the system default — **no reordering required**. `AUD-6` needs the enumeration to shuffle; this one does not. Do not let the two be collapsed into "the same intermittent thing." **Why the UI reports what it reports.** `isActive` is a string comparison and nothing more: `DevicesController.cs:74-81` computes `isActive = d.Id == _deviceManager.GetSelectedOutputDeviceId()`, i.e. against the same `_selectedOutputDeviceId` field the bookkeeping just wrote. (Note `isDefault` on that payload is *also* derived from the user selection when one exists, `:79-80` — which is why the live capture shows `isDefault:true` on `playback-1` while MiniAudio reports the **built-in** device as the system default. Two different meanings of "default" on one payload; worth tidying here, but it is not the defect.) **And the truth is not merely unreconciled — it is UNREACHABLE.** `_currentDeviceIndex` (`SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs:44`, assigned at `:543`, `:809`, `:883`) is the only field that reflects the device actually open, and it is **private with no accessor of any kind**, so the API layer could not report the real answer today even if it wanted to. Exposing it (or something derived from `_playbackDevice`) is likely the smallest honest fix for the reporting half. **FOUR SEPARATE NOTIONS OF "THE OUTPUT DEVICE" EXIST, AND ONLY ONE OF THEM IS REAL** — enumerate them in the plan rather than rediscovering them: **(1)** `SoundFlowAudioEngine._currentDeviceIndex` / `_playbackDevice` — where audio actually goes; set by init and by `SwitchPlaybackDevice`; **private, unexposed**. **(2)** `SoundFlowDeviceManager._selectedOutputDeviceId` (`:52`) — what `/api/devices/output` reports and what gets persisted; set by startup and by the controller. **(3)** `SoundFlowAudioEngine._activeOutputId` — the gate's local/Cast/HTTP notion, persisted to the **same** config key as (2). **(4)** `LocalAudioOutput`'s device id (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/Outputs/LocalAudioOutput.cs:166`, `:192`) — updated **only** from `DevicesController.cs:229`/`:242`, i.e. the interactive path, so it too is stale after every restart. **A THIRD SITE SILENTLY REPOINTS WITHOUT TELLING ANYONE, and it is not in the owner's report:** the engine's recovery path re-initializes `playbackDevices[0]` unconditionally and sets `_currentDeviceIndex = 0` (`SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs:805-809`) — so after a device-loss recovery, audio moves to whatever is at index 0 while (2), (3) and (4) all keep reporting the old preference. **Fold this into the fix or state explicitly that it is out of scope; do not leave it undiscovered.** **Scope, deliberately not prescribed beyond the two halves:** **(a)** make the startup path actually route to the persisted preference — the obvious shape is to call the same native switch the controller calls, but note it is currently fire-and-forget on a thread pool thread from an HTTP handler and startup wants something ordered and awaited; **(b)** make the reported active output derive from what is actually open rather than from a bookkeeping string, so the two cannot silently disagree again. **⚠ (a) WITHOUT `AUD-6` IS A REGRESSION, NOT A PARTIAL FIX — this is the single most important sentence on this row.** Making startup honour the persisted ordinal converts a **mis-report** into a **mis-route**: today's store holds `playback-1`, which now resolves to USB Audio Out, so the owner's saved intent of "Soundbar" would be honestly and confidently routed to the wrong hardware. **Claim `AUD-6` first, or ship (b) alone and hold (a) until the key is stable.** **⚠ A guard already exists for the neighbouring problem and CANNOT FIRE — do not mistake it for coverage.** `AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:294-330` compares "expected" against "actual" where both are the same lookup by the same key against the same cache; its repair path then calls `SetOutputDeviceAsync`, which does no native switch either. Full reasoning on `AUD-6`; stated here because whoever claims this row will find it first and may conclude the startup path is already verified. **It is not.** **Test gap, and it is the fifth instance of this repo's comment-accuracy failure class:** `tests/Radio.API.Tests/Services/AudioEngineInitializationServiceStartupTests.cs:79-100` (`StartAsync_PersistedLocalDevice_RoutesThroughGate`) verifies **only** `SetActiveOutputAsync("playback-2")` and asserts nothing about which device ends up open — and its own comment (`:81-83`) says the persisted device must go through the gate *"not just the device-manager swap,"* **naming a swap that does not exist.** A test whose comment describes behaviour the production code never had is worse than no test: it makes the gap look covered. Any fix here must add an assertion about the **native** target, and correct that comment. **⚠ OWNER / UAT NOTE — the evidence above is a snapshot as of `0b2ebcb`, and the owner is about to change it on purpose.** Hands-on UAT will re-select "Soundbar" by hand, which writes `playback-0` and makes the UI truthful again for the current enumeration. **Do NOT read a later truthful `/api/devices/output` as evidence this row is fixed or was never real.** The only condition under which it fires is a **restart** with a persisted preference that is not the system default — so verification means: set a non-default output, restart `radio-api`, and confirm with `pactl list sink-inputs` that the app's stream is on the sink the UI names. **Do not deploy or restart anything on the box to write this row's plan.** | ✅ **PR [#500](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/500)** | _plan TBD (medium — half (b) is small and shippable alone; half (a) is small in diff and sharp in blast radius, and is gated on `AUD-6`)_ | _no spec doc — the diagnosis is in this row_ · provenance: the 2026-08-11 production deploy — `/api/devices/output` compared against `pactl` sink state, plus the `radio-api` startup journal | — _(no **row** dependency in the blocking sense, but **`AUD-6` SHOULD BE CLAIMED FIRST** and half (a) must not ship before it; see the ⚠ above and § Dependency / ordering notes. **Same files as `AUD-6`** — rebase rather than re-deriving anchors. **Also touches `DevicesController.cs`, which `AUD-5` does not**, and `SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs`, where `AUD-5`'s work is confined to `GoogleCastOutput.cs` — so no conflict with `AUD-5`.)_ | `fix/reconcile-reported-and-actual-output-device` |
| AUD-5 | **A Cast connection that is no longer current can persist its volume as the system master volume.** **User-visible, silently persistent, and cheap to fix — but ⚠ the obvious fix is the wrong one, so read the "do not widen the lock" note before writing a line.** **The mechanism is verified end-to-end against `main` @ `0870410`; unlike `AUD-2` this row needs no investigation phase, and unlike most of the tranche it must not be re-brainstormed.** `GoogleCastOutput.SyncInitialVolumeAsync` (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/Outputs/GoogleCastOutput.cs:1543`) is a **read** of the Cast device's status, not a command — but its **success path fires `CastVolumeChanged`** (`:1568-1573`). That event has **exactly one subscriber in the whole solution**: `AudioStateUpdateService.OnCastVolumeChanged` (`src/Radio.API/Services/AudioStateUpdateService.cs:819`; subscribed `:118`, unsubscribed `:935` — grep-verified, no other handler anywhere in `src/` or `tests/`), which **unconditionally sets `AudioManager.MasterVolume`** (`:831`) and `IsMuted` (`:839`). **And that setter PERSISTS.** `AudioManager.MasterVolume`'s setter (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/Services/AudioManager.cs:74-82`) calls `_preferencePersistence?.ScheduleVolumePersist()` at `:80`, which debounces 500 ms and writes to the config store (`AudioPreferencePersistence.cs:46-62`). **So a stale event does not merely move a slider — it rewrites saved state that survives a restart.** **`IsInitialSync` exists on the event args (`GoogleCastOutput.cs:1829`) and is NEVER BRANCHED ON:** its only two uses in the entire codebase are string interpolation into log messages (`AudioStateUpdateService.cs:834`, `:842`). An initial *status read* is therefore treated identically to the user turning the knob on the Chromecast. **The window.** `SyncInitialVolumeAsync` has a **single** call site — `ConnectAsync:634` — and it runs **after** `TryPublishConnectionAsync` (`:614`) has already validated the connection generation, so at the moment of the call the connection genuinely *is* current. The exposure opens across the `await receiverChannel.GetChromecastStatusAsync()` network round-trip at `:1555`: **nothing re-checks `_connectionGeneration` between the status response arriving and the event fire at `:1568`.** Four sites can bump the generation inside that window — a newer connect's claim (`:539`), `DisconnectAsync` (`:712`), `InitializeAsync` (`:248`) and `DisposeAsync` (`:1720`) — after which the volume of a connection that **has already been superseded** is written to master volume and persisted. **⚠ THE OBVIOUS FIX IS BOTH WRONG AND RISKY — stated first because someone will otherwise reach for it. Do NOT widen `_lifecycleLock` to cover `SyncInitialVolumeAsync`.** The exposure is the **event fire**, not the field read, so widening the lock does not close it — and it would hold the lock across a SharpCaster network call, which is exactly the hang `AUD-3`'s fix was built to avoid. The file says so in as many words at `GoogleCastOutput.cs:49-53`: a connect can park for tens of seconds inside calls that do not observe cancellation, and a teardown queueing behind it converts a data race into a **multi-second hang on the output picker**. **The correct shape is a generation re-check immediately before the fire** — the same check `TryPublishConnectionAsync` already performs at `:675`, applied one step later. **It is cheap to plumb: `myGeneration` is already in scope at the call site** (captured at `ConnectAsync:539`, still live at `:634`), so this is a parameter plus a short await-free critical section, not new machinery. **Second half, and arguably the better one: branch on `IsInitialSync` so a status read is not treated as a user command at all.** The flag was built for precisely this distinction and has never been used for anything but logging. **Decide deliberately and say which way in the plan** — "re-check the generation," "never sync master volume from an initial read," or both; they are separable, and only the first is strictly required to close the race. **⚠ THIS PREDATES PR #468. It is NOT a regression from the Cast synchronization work and must NOT be "fixed" by unpicking it.** All three links — the event fire in `SyncInitialVolumeAsync`, the `IsInitialSync` flag, and `OnCastVolumeChanged`'s unconditional `MasterVolume` write — landed **together** in commit **`b420edc`, 2026-02-11** (*"feat: Fix Cast audio streaming and audio pipeline optimizations"*, whose own message reads *"Add bidirectional volume sync between app and Cast device"*) — **six months before #468.** Verified two ways: `git log -S "IsInitialSync" -- src/` returns `b420edc` and nothing else, and `git merge-base --is-ancestor b420edc 8b1ce0a` confirms the ordering. **#468 created the generation counter this fix will use; it did not create the hole.** **⚠ RECORDED SO IT IS NOT RE-INVESTIGATED: the three unsynchronized sibling reads in this file are NOT this bug, and were already assessed.** `SyncInitialVolumeAsync` (`:1545`), `SetCastVolumeAsync` (`:1632`) and `SetCastMuteAsync` (`:1657`) each null-check `_client` and then dereference it on a second, separate read. **Assessed and found safe** on three grounds: `_client` is **never set back to null** once assigned (exactly two assignment sites, `InitializeAsync` and `TryPublishConnectionAsync`, both non-null; `DisconnectAsync` and `DisposeAsync` deliberately leave it set), every dereference sits inside a `try`/`catch`, and the client-identity race is **self-neutralizing** because `ConnectAsync` disconnects any old client. **PR #473 wrote that reasoning into the file itself** — `GoogleCastOutput.cs:32-104`, specifically the PRECONDITION block at `:63-81` — so it is on the record and needs no re-deriving. **This row's defect is DOWNSTREAM of those reads, not in them:** the read yields a perfectly valid client; what goes wrong is that its *result* is published as a user-authored volume change after the connection stopped being current. **🔵 A HYPOTHESIS TO CHECK — deliberately NOT stated as a diagnosis, and the row does not depend on it.** The owner has been observing volume behaving oddly on this system, and the 2026-08-10 journal carries master-volume values ratcheting down through `0.03` / `0.02` / `0.01` / `6.85E-09`. **Those particular values are attributable to a slider drag during the crash investigation and are NOT evidence for this row — do not cite them as a repro.** But a superseded Cast connection that can write *and persist* master volume is a plausible source of *"my volume changed by itself"* reports in general, and that is worth **checking rather than assuming**. **The forensic signal already exists and costs nothing to look for:** `AudioStateUpdateService.cs:832-834` logs `"Synced volume from Cast device: {Volume:P0} (initial: {IsInitial})"` on every write. Grep journald for that line with `initial: True`, correlate against Cast connect/teardown churn and against play/volume history, and the hypothesis confirms or dies in one pass. **⚠ Do NOT let a negative journald result close this row** — the mechanism is real whether or not it has yet fired in production, and the fix is worth shipping either way. **Test gap, stated because it is total:** `CastVolumeChanged` and `SyncInitialVolumeAsync` have **zero test coverage anywhere in the solution** (grep-verified across `tests/`). `GoogleCastOutputConcurrencyTests.cs` covers the publish race only — its two tests (`TeardownDuringConnect_DoesNotCorruptTheInFlightConnect:35`, `ConnectThatSucceedsButLostTheRace_DiscardsItselfAndStaysUsable:97`) stop **before** the volume sync. **Note the existing seam does not reach this window:** `ConnectRaceHookForTests` (`:120`) fires at receiver resolution, which is **too early** — a test for this must interleave a teardown during the *status read*, i.e. after publish. Whether that justifies a **fourth** `internal` seam is a real question and it is the one `TEST-2` now owns as a pattern — **check `TEST-2` first, and if you do add a seam, record it there.** | 📋 | _plan TBD (small — a generation parameter, one await-free re-check, and an explicit decision on `IsInitialSync`; **the diagnosis is complete and must not be re-brainstormed**)_ | _no spec doc — the diagnosis is in this row_ · **provenance: PR #473's pre-merge review**, which found it while checking whether #473's own comment was true · the mechanism is now documented in-tree at [`GoogleCastOutput.cs:95-104`](../src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/Outputs/GoogleCastOutput.cs) · origin commit `b420edc` (2026-02-11) | — _(no row dependency; claimable now. **⚠ Touches `GoogleCastOutput.cs`, which PR #473 (`0870410`) reflowed: its header comment grew by exactly +66 lines, so EVERY anchor below `:32` in that file moved +66.** The citations in **this** row are already post-#473 and verified; any citation copied from the **`AUD-3`** row is pre-#473 and is not. Also touches `AudioStateUpdateService.cs`, which no other row claims. **No file overlap with `AUD-1`, `AUD-2` or `AUD-4`**, so it can run alongside any of them.)_ | `fix/cast-initial-volume-sync-generation-check` |
| AUD-2 | **Confirm-or-close: is SDR gain/ducking silently dead because the source is registered under one key and addressed by another?** **⚠ INVESTIGATE FIRST. This is an unverified inference from a code read, not an observation, and the row may legitimately close with no code change.** **The suspected mismatch.** `SDRRadioAudioSource` mints its own playback key — `_playbackId = $"sdr-radio-{Guid.NewGuid():N}"` (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/Sources/Primary/SDRRadioAudioSource.cs:908`) — and registers the component under it via `PlayComponentAsync(_playbackId, …)` (`:956-960`), which lands in `SoundFlowPlaybackService._activeComponents[sourceId]` (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/SoundFlow/SoundFlowPlaybackService.cs:423`, dictionary declared at `:25`). But `AudioManager` addresses the same source by `IAudioSource.Id`, which `AudioSourceBase` derives as `$"{Type}-{Guid.NewGuid():N}"` (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/Sources/AudioSourceBase.cs:28`) — and `SDRRadioAudioSource.Type` is `AudioSourceType.Radio` (`:204`), i.e. **`Radio-<guid>`**. **Two different keys, with two different GUIDs, for one source.** **What no-ops if it holds:** `_playbackService.SetGainOffset(source.Id, gain)` (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/Services/AudioManager.cs:292`, and the sibling at `:121`), `SetDuckingMultiplier(source.Id, multiplier)` (`:247`), `SetDuckingMultiplier(_activeSource.Id, multiplier)` (`:479`), and `ClearDuckingMultiplier(_activeSource.Id)` (`:508`, and `:240` on source switch). **Why it would fail SILENTLY, which is the part that makes this worth a row:** none of those methods checks membership. `SetGainOffset` (`SoundFlowPlaybackService.cs:620`) and `SetDuckingMultiplier` (`:643`) write into `_gainOffsets` / `_duckingMultipliers` unconditionally and then call `ApplyEffectiveVolume(sourceId)`, whose two `TryGetValue` lookups (`_activePlayers`, `_activeComponents`) simply **miss** — no exception, no warning. `SetGainOffset` then logs `"Applied gain offset {Gain:F2} to source (SourceId={SourceId})"` at Debug: **the log line asserts a success it never verified.** Same class of trap as AUD-4's mixer log, and worth fixing in the same spirit even if the key mismatch turns out not to exist. **Consequence if confirmed: TTS and event audio never duck the radio source**, and per-source gain never applies to it. Audio ducking is a documented core pattern in `CLAUDE.md` § Architecture ("Audio ducking with priority system (1-10 scale)"), so this would be a significant hole, not a cosmetic one. **First task is confirmation, not repair.** Cheapest check: play SDR radio, fire a TTS event, and observe whether radio volume actually drops; corroborate by dumping the live keys (`SoundFlowPlaybackService` already exposes `_activePlayers.Keys` / component counts around `:734` and `:752`) and comparing them against the `IAudioSource.Id` the `AudioManager` is holding. **⚠ Trap for the fix, if confirmed: do not paper over it by making `AudioManager` translate to `sdr-radio-*`.** The right answer is **one key per source, agreed by both layers** — the same conclusion AUD-4 reaches from the teardown side. Coordinate the two. _**Every anchor in this row re-verified 2026-08-11 against `main` @ `8b1ce0a` and all are byte-exact and unchanged:** `SDRRadioAudioSource.cs:204`/`:908`/`:956-960`, `SoundFlowPlaybackService.cs:25`/`:423`/`:620`/`:643`/`:734`/`:752`, `AudioManager.cs:121`/`:240`/`:247`/`:292`/`:479`/`:508`, and `AudioSourceBase.cs:28` — the last checked specifically because #468 touched that file (its hunk starts at `:97`). **The inference this row is built on is therefore intact post-#468 and still unconfirmed** — nothing in #467/#468/#469 tested it either way._ | 📋 | _plan TBD (**investigate first**; scope depends entirely on the answer)_ | _no spec doc — the diagnosis is in this row_ · `CLAUDE.md` § Architecture (ducking as a core pattern) | — _(no row dependency. **Same root as AUD-4, seen from the ducking side rather than the teardown side — prefer claiming AUD-2 FIRST**: it is investigation-cheap and it decides the key identity that AUD-4's sweep depends on.)_ | `fix/sdr-playback-id-ducking-gain` |

Change only the three files under `src/`. **Verification for this row is a clean restore + full `dotnet test`**, not a visual diff — the point is proving the pinned graph is the graph that was already building. | 📋 | _plan TBD (small; scope is fully enumerated in this row)_ | _no spec doc — the diagnosis is in this row_ | — _(no dependency; claimable now. | OPS-3 | **`BindsTo=` for `radio-web` — make joint failure actually joint.** **⚠ This is a real change to production service coupling: own PR, owner review, and it is the one row in this tranche that must NOT auto-merge on green gates.** PR #467 made the systemd restart limiter functional — `StartLimitIntervalSec=300` / `StartLimitBurst=5`, correctly placed in `[Unit]` — on both units. It stopped deliberately short of this. **The gap:** `deploy/common/radio-web.service:4` declares `Requires=radio-api.service`, which **does not couple the two lifecycles the way it appears to**. systemd propagates only an **explicit** stop/restart of `radio-api` across `Requires=`; a unit that deactivates **on its own** — exactly what `radio-api` does when it exhausts `StartLimitBurst` and lands in `failed` — is **not** propagated (`systemd.unit(5)`; `BindsTo=` is the dependency type that gives joint failure). **So when `radio-api` trips its limiter, `radio-web` keeps running and serves a UI whose backend is gone.** **The reasoning is already written down in-tree and does not need re-deriving — read `deploy/common/radio-web.service:12-22` first.** #467 states the gap, names `BindsTo=` as the fix, and records why it was scoped out. This row is the follow-through, not a rediscovery. **The tradeoff being accepted is explicit:** the console going dark is a *worse-looking* failure than a UI with a dead backend, and a *more honest* one — that judgement is the owner's to confirm on the PR, which is why this does not auto-merge. **Recovery command worth recording in the runbook**, because it becomes reachable exactly when the limiter starts latching and `Restart=always` will **not** clear a tripped start limiter on its own: `sudo systemctl reset-failed radio-api.service && sudo systemctl start radio-api.service`. **Also in scope:** leave `After=` as-is, and check whether any deploy path assumes the two services restart independently — `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1` restarts them separately today, and `BindsTo=` changes what a `radio-api` failure does to an in-flight web deploy. **Do not deploy or restart anything on the box as part of writing this row's plan;** the unit files are the deliverable, and rollout is the owner's call. | 📋 | _plan TBD (small in diff, large in blast radius — the plan should be mostly test/rollout, not code)_ | [`deploy/common/radio-web.service:12-22`](../deploy/common/radio-web.service) (the gap, already documented by #467) · [`deploy/common/radio-api.service`](../deploy/common/radio-api.service) (limiter rationale) | — _(no row dependency; **PR #467 is merged**, so both `StartLimit*` blocks are on `main` to build on.)_ | `fix/systemd-bindsto-radio-web` |
| AUD-3 | **Close the Cast connect/teardown data race — not just its observable outcome.** **✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-11 in PR #468, merge commit `8b1ce0a` (commits `47da897` "close the Cast connect/teardown data race" + `1fded0c`).** _This row was written as 🔒-on-#468 while #468 was open and scoped to the observable outcome only. The owner then directed the race be closed **before** #468 merged, and it was — so #468's final merged diff is materially larger than the one this row was written against. **The diagnosis below is preserved verbatim as the record of what was wrong; the ✅ VERDICT block after it records what actually landed and what did not.**_ **The race.** The Cast connect path calls `_castOutput.InitializeAsync` / `ConnectAsync` / `SetStreamUrl` / `StartAsync` **without holding `SoundFlowAudioEngine._activeOutputLock`** (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/SoundFlow/SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs:58`), while the teardown path `TearDownCastOutputAsync` (`:303-330`) manipulates the **same** `GoogleCastOutput` instance. `GoogleCastOutput` has **no synchronisation over its connection state**: `_client` and `_connectedReceiver` (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/Outputs/GoogleCastOutput.cs:29-30`) are reassigned from at least three places (`:142-150`, `:437-444`, `:514-520`), and the type's only lock is `_debounceLock` (`:48`), which guards something else entirely. Connect and teardown can therefore interleave on an unsynchronised client handle. **⚠ Citation caveat — the method name in the session record only exists on #468's branch.** `TryConnectCastAsync` is `src/Radio.API/Services/AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:499` on `fix/cast-startup-mute-rollback-and-source-teardown`; on `main` @ `c129e0d` the same path is **still inline** at `AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:405-452`. This row is written against the post-#468 shape because that is where the fix lands — **re-site the anchors when you claim it** rather than trusting either set. _**Resolved 2026-08-11:** #468 merged, so the branch-only shape **is** `main` now — `TryConnectCastAsync` is at `AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:499` on `8b1ce0a`, confirmed. The caveat is retained because it is the reason the citation was correct in the first place._ **What #468 does and does not do, stated so its merge is not misread as closing this:** it adds a post-`StartAsync` teardown guard, which closes the **observable outcome** (the engine no longer leaves local output muted behind a Cast connect that did not survive) — **the underlying race is untouched.** **Why this is its own cycle rather than a fix-up on #468:** doing it properly means the connect path runs under the same lock the teardown path uses (or `GoogleCastOutput` grows its own state lock), which **changes the engine's output-coordination API** and ripples into `AttachOutputCoordination` (`SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs:171`) and every caller. **And it wants hardware UAT, not a desk fix** — a real Chromecast, deliberate connect/disconnect churn during startup, and the `/stream/audio/mp3` path exercised while teardown runs. Budget for time on the box. **— END OF THE 2026-08-10 DIAGNOSIS. What follows is the 2026-08-11 verdict, read off the merged diff at `8b1ce0a` rather than off the merge report. —** **✅ VERDICT: fully satisfied on the code.
