Recorded here so nobody re-derives it.** The fix took the **second** of the two options this row itself named — *"or `GoogleCastOutput` grows its own state lock"* — precisely because the first (running the connect under `_activeOutputLock`) would convert the race into a multi-second hang on the output picker: a Cast connect can park for tens of seconds inside SharpCaster calls that do not observe cancellation. **No lock is held across any network call.** **(1) `GoogleCastOutput` gained `_lifecycleLock` (`:39`) + `_connectionGeneration` (`:45`).** All three reassignment sites this row cited are now inside it — `InitializeAsync` (`:179-190`), the new `TryPublishConnectionAsync` (`:599-623`), and `DisconnectAsync` (`:643-655`) — plus `DisposeAsync` (`:1651-1663`) and a snapshot read in `StopAsync` (`:930-937`). **Six critical sections, all await-free**, all plain field/auto-property swaps (`Name` and `ConnectedDevice` are auto-properties with no side effects — checked, because a setter that re-entered the engine would break the stated lock order). **(2) The NRE mechanism this row named is structurally eliminated, not merely guarded.** The old code stored the resolved receiver in `_connectedReceiver` and dereferenced it after a teardown had nulled it; `ConnectAsync` now resolves into a **local** `ChromecastReceiver? receiver` and snapshots the client, so the long network work operates on values a concurrent teardown cannot touch. Teardown **preempts rather than queues**: `DisconnectAsync` bumps the generation and takes the state out from under an in-flight connect *first*, then does its network work on the snapshot. A connect that lost re-validates at publish, disconnects what it built, and hands the state machine back. **(3) The engine API change this row predicted landed** — `BeginCastConnectAsync` (`SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs:361`) / `TryCommitCastConnectAsync` (`:387`) over `_castConnectEpoch` (`:65`), bumped inside `SetActiveOutputAsync` under the existing `_activeOutputLock` (`:58`, unmoved). The commit **re-checks the epoch and tears Cast down under a single acquisition**, replacing the check-then-act (a `_localFallbackApplied` flag read, then an action) that had a window between the two steps. It did **not** ripple into `AttachOutputCoordination` (`:173`) as this row guessed it would — the epoch handshake sits beside that API rather than through it. **(4) One real defect was found in the race fix itself and fixed before merge (`1fded0c`), and it is worth knowing about because it was worse than the race:** `ConnectAsync` sets `State = Connecting` on entry and the new discard-on-supersede branch returned without resetting it — and `Connecting` is a dead end for this class (`ConnectAsync` requires Ready/Stopped, `ValidateCanInitialize` accepts only Created/Error), so the **first** teardown-during-connect would have made Cast permanently unusable until process restart. Losing that race is the *normal* outcome of the startup auto-connect rolling back, not an edge case. The branch now sets `State = Ready`. **⚠ THREE THINGS THIS ROW ASKED FOR DID NOT SHIP. Do not record them as done.** **(a) The hardware UAT this row explicitly demanded never happened.** #468 added **zero** files under `docs/` — no UAT report, no ADR — and its verification is desk-only: a deterministic `internal` hook plus a real loopback listener, and a substituted transport. No real Chromecast, no connect/disconnect churn on the box, no `/stream/audio/mp3` exercised during teardown. **This is the GV-8 `M-1` situation again** (see that row's ⚠ and § Dependency / ordering notes) — shipped, unit-pinned, **live-unverified**. It is cheap to close opportunistically and needs no cycle: switch the output picker away from Cast while a startup auto-connect is mid-flight and confirm Cast neither wedges at `Connecting` nor streams alongside the soundbar. **(b) The service-level commit call is untested.** `AudioEngineInitializationServiceStartupTests` builds the service with `new Mock<IAudioEngine>()` (`:381`), while **both** epoch call sites gate on the concrete type — `_audioEngine is SoundFlowAudioEngine` at `AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:522` (capture) and `:569` (commit). So `castEpoch` is `null` in **every** test in that file and the commit branch is unreachable there. The engine methods have four direct tests in `SoundFlowAudioEngineActiveOutputTests`; it is the **wiring between them that is unpinned**, and a refactor that dropped either `is` check would pass the whole suite. **(c) Closing the test gap cost two `internal` seams** — `ConnectRaceHookForTests` (`GoogleCastOutput.cs:54`) and `ConnectTransportOverrideForTests` (`:64`), the second added only because the first could not reach the succeeded-then-lost path offline. Genuine design debt, and the **second** time this codebase has bought coverage with a seam — folded into `TEST-2` as a pattern rather than left as an incident. **Two smaller observations, recorded because they are the kind of thing that decays into a bug:** the class comment at `GoogleCastOutput.cs:32` claims `_lifecycleLock` *"guards every read/write of `_client` / `_connectedReceiver` / `ConnectedDevice`"* — **it does not**; roughly twenty reads remain outside it (`:698`, `:710`, `:776`, `:781`, `:800`, `:1044`, `:1173`, `:1230`, `:1479`, `:1566`, `:1591`, `:1622`, …). The design is still sound *only because* `_client` is deliberately never nulled after first assignment — `DisposeAsync` says so in as many words at `:1656-1659` — so the `_client!` dereferences cannot NRE. **But the comment asserts an invariant the code does not hold, and the next person to null `_client` anywhere will believe the lock protects them.** That is the same trap as `AUD-4`'s *"Removed audio source … from mixer"* log. Second: `ConnectAsync` threads the client **snapshot** into `SubscribeToReceiverStatus(client)` — a parameter added by this very fix, documented at `:1436-1441` — and then immediately calls `SyncInitialVolumeAsync()` with no argument, which reads the `_client` **field** at `:1479`. One of three sibling call sites was left on the field. Low severity (post-publish the two agree unless something superseded again) and a one-line change; **noted, not queued.** _**⚠ BOTH of those two closing observations have since resolved — updated 2026-08-11 (second pass), and this is the only edit made to this ✅ row.**_ _**(1) The over-claiming class comment is FIXED.** PR #473 (`0870410`) rewrote it: it now states that the lock serializes the connection-state **swaps** and explicitly says an earlier revision claiming it guarded every read *"never did, and believing it makes the unlocked dereferences look safer than they actually are."* It goes further than this row asked, writing the **precondition** that licenses the unlocked reads (`_client` is never set back to null) directly into the file. **Worth knowing how it was caught, because the lesson is not the one it looks like:** #473's own **first draft of the corrected comment over-claimed in turn** — it said "never a null one," when the field is in fact null before the first `InitializeAsync` — and only a reviewer briefed to actively **falsify** the comment caught that. Reviewing a comment for plausibility is not the same as checking it against the code. **That is now a standing checklist item in `CLAUDE.md` § Pre-Merge Review**, where this row's `AUD-4` cross-reference is cited as one of its three worked examples._ _**(2) The `SyncInitialVolumeAsync()` field-read observation has been superseded by a real defect one step downstream — now `AUD-5`, and the honest note is that this row under-read it.** This row called it "low severity, a one-line change, noted not queued," and **for the field read itself that verdict still holds** — #473's precondition block establishes why the unlocked read is safe. **But the assessment stopped at the read.** What #473's review then found is that the same method's **success path fires `CastVolumeChanged`**, whose sole subscriber sets *and persists* `AudioManager.MasterVolume`, with **no generation re-check between the status response and the event fire** — so a teardown landing in that window persists the volume of a superseded connection. **That is a user-visible, persistent-state defect, not a cosmetic inconsistency, and it PREDATES #468** (`b420edc`, 2026-02-11) — so it is neither this row's residue nor a regression from this row's fix. **See `AUD-5`.** ⚠ **Note the anchor:** the `:1479` cited above is pre-#473; that read is now at `:1545`, along with every other anchor in this row's `GoogleCastOutput.cs` citations, which shifted **+66** uniformly. Those citations have been left as written because they are the historical record of what #468 fixed — **re-site them against `main` before reusing any of them.**_ | ✅ **PR #468** (`8b1ce0a`) | _no plan was written — and deliberately so: the fix landed inside #468 at the owner's direction rather than as its own cycle_ | _no spec doc — the diagnosis is in this row_ · merged evidence: `47da897`, `1fded0c`, `tests/Radio.Infrastructure.Tests/Audio/Outputs/GoogleCastOutputConcurrencyTests.cs`, `tests/Radio.Infrastructure.Tests/Audio/SoundFlowAudioEngineActiveOutputTests.cs` | **PR #468 — ✅ merged `8b1ce0a`.** _The 🔒-on-a-PR deviation this row carried is now closed; it never had to resolve either way, because the blocker and the fix turned out to be the same PR._ | `fix/cast-connect-teardown-race` _(never used — shipped on `fix/cast-startup-mute-rollback-and-source-teardown`)_ |
| AUD-4 | **Unify the two source-removal layers, and rename `SoundFlowMasterMixer` — it is not a mixer.** Two cleanups on one mechanism, deliberately in one cycle because the rename is what stops the bug from recurring. **The misnomer, and the damage it already did.** `src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/SoundFlow/SoundFlowMasterMixer.cs:13` is `private readonly List<IAudioSource> _sources = []` — a **registry of source objects**, not the audio mixer. The real audio mixer is SoundFlow's `playbackDevice.MasterMixer`, and the real detach is `SoundFlowPlaybackService.StopAsync` (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/SoundFlow/SoundFlowPlaybackService.cs:494`) → `MasterMixer.RemoveComponent` (`:526` / `:548`). But `SoundFlowMasterMixer.RemoveSource` (`:109-121`) logs **`"Removed audio source {SourceId} ({SourceName}) from mixer"`** (`:118`), which reads as an audio detach and is not one. **Commit `03a6fea`** ("fix: Source exclusivity, PipeWire BT auto-link cleanup, and default device selection", 2026-02-26) was applied **one layer too high** — at `AudioManager.SwitchSourceAsync`'s `mixer.RemoveSource(oldSource)` (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/Services/AudioManager.cs:214-217`, which logs the equally misleading `"Removed old source {SourceName} from mixer"`) — **and silently did nothing for months**, because the audio stayed attached one layer down. **The rename is therefore the durable half of this row, not the cosmetic half.** **⚠ ADDED 2026-08-11 — PR #468 changed the ground under this row, and it is the one thing in this tranche nobody anticipated. Read this before writing a plan.** #468 rewrote `AudioSourceBase.StopAsync` (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/Sources/AudioSourceBase.cs:100-124`): teardown is no longer gated on `State` being `Playing`/`Paused`, only `Created`/`Disposed` are skipped. The reason it gives is **exactly this row's failure class, at a layer this row does not name** — *"a source can hold an attached, audible sound component while its state reads Ready/Stopped/Error … gating teardown on the state flag meant those sources kept producing audio after a switch-away, because `StopCoreAsync` — the only code that detaches the component — was skipped."* **Two consequences.** **(1) There are THREE layers here, not two.** This row names `SoundFlowMasterMixer.RemoveSource` (registry) and `SoundFlowPlaybackService.StopAsync` → `MasterMixer.RemoveComponent` (audio); the third is `AudioSourceBase.StopAsync` → `StopCoreAsync`, whose **entry condition just changed**. Any unification written against the two-layer picture will be unifying two of three while the third has moved underneath it — which is, precisely, how `03a6fea` went wrong. **(2) #468's comment and this row make competing "the only detach" claims and they cannot both be the whole truth.** #468 says `StopCoreAsync` is *the only code that detaches the component*; this row says the real detach is `SoundFlowPlaybackService.StopAsync` → `RemoveComponent` (`:526`/`:548`). Most likely both are locally right about different sources (per-source `StopCoreAsync` implementations vs. the shared playback service), which would mean the detach is **doubly** non-unified — but that is an inference, and **resolving it is the first task of this row, before any code moves.** Do not silently pick one. **Related, and cheap to confirm at the same time:** #468's state-independent teardown may already have closed part of what this row's "no caller left able to do half the job" was aiming at. Say explicitly in the plan what remains rather than assuming the whole scope survived. **Scope:** (a) unify the layers so one call detaches both the registry entry and the audio component, with no caller left able to do half the job; (b) rename `SoundFlowMasterMixer` (and consider `IMasterMixer`, `:10`) to something that says *registry* — the exact name is the implementer's call, but **it must not contain "Mixer"**; (c) fix both log strings to state what actually happened. **⚠ TRAP FOR THE IMPLEMENTER — stated explicitly because it is the exact mistake this row exists to stop repeating: do NOT sweep by `oldSource.Id`.** SDR registers its component under `sdr-radio-<guid>` (`SDRRadioAudioSource.cs:908`) while `AudioManager` knows that same source as `Radio-<guid>` (`AudioSourceBase.cs:28`) — an `Id`-keyed sweep would miss the SDR source and, once again, log success. **Sweep `SoundFlowPlaybackService._activeComponents` (`:25`) instead**, which is keyed by whatever each source actually registered. **Coordinate with AUD-2 — it is the same key-identity problem seen from the ducking side.** If AUD-2 confirms and lands first, adopt its single-key answer here rather than coding around the mismatch a second time. **Budget note:** renaming the interface touches DI wiring and any test doubles, so this is a larger diff than the behaviour change suggests. _**Anchors re-verified 2026-08-11 against `main` @ `8b1ce0a`** and all are byte-exact and unchanged: `SoundFlowMasterMixer.cs:10`/`:13`/`:109-121`/`:118`, `SoundFlowPlaybackService.cs:25`/`:494`/`:526`/`:548`, `AudioManager.cs:214-217`, `SDRRadioAudioSource.cs:908`, and `AudioSourceBase.cs:28` — that last one checked specifically because #468 **did** touch the file, but its hunk starts at `:97`, well below the `Id` derivation._ | 📋 | _plan TBD (medium; the rename dominates the diff, the layer-unification dominates the risk — **and the first task is now the three-layer reconciliation above, not code**)_ | _no spec doc — the diagnosis is in this row_ · commit `03a6fea` is the provenance for the "one layer too high" trap · **PR #468 (`8b1ce0a`) is the provenance for the third layer** | — _(no hard row dependency. **Prefer AUD-2 first** — it decides the key identity this row's sweep must use, and doing them in the other order risks building the sweep around a mismatch that AUD-2 then removes. **Also rebase past #468**, which changed `AudioSourceBase.StopAsync`'s teardown gate — see the ⚠ above.)_ | `refactor/unify-source-removal-and-rename-mixer` |
| TEST-2 | **Close the deferred-capture branch-dispatch coverage gap left by PR #469 — if a native test harness ever becomes feasible.** **This row records a known limit so it is not mistaken for coverage. It may legitimately close as "still infeasible," and that is an acceptable outcome.** PR #469's deferred-capture test reaches the acquisition through the **internal `ApplyDeferredCaptureState()` seam** (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/Audio/Sources/Primary/BluetoothAudioSource.cs:454`, driven from `tests/Radio.Infrastructure.Tests/Audio/BluetoothAudioSourceTests.cs:917`, `:930`, `:954`) rather than by driving the real `capture is AudioCaptureDevice` / `capture is SoundComponent` branches end-to-end. **Why, and it is already recorded in the code:** constructing either type needs a **native SoundFlow `AudioEngine`**, which the method's own doc comment states at `BluetoothAudioSource.cs:449-452` — that constraint is exactly why the seam is `internal` + `InternalsVisibleTo` in the first place. **What IS pinned by #469:** the state decision (a source already `Playing` stays `Playing`; only a not-yet-playing source lands in `Ready`) and its tap consequence — demoting to `Ready` would silently kill fingerprinting while audio keeps flowing. **What is NOT pinned:** the branch dispatch around it — `TryAcquireAudioCaptureAsync` (`:462`) at `:483` / `:494`, plus the two sibling dispatch sites at `:159` / `:166` and `:684` / `:689`. Three places make the same `capture is …` decision and none is covered end-to-end. **⚠ Do not "close" this with a mock that asserts the seam again from a different angle** — that adds a test without adding coverage, and would make the gap harder to see than leaving it open. The only real close is a harness that can produce a native `AudioEngine` (or a genuine integration test on the box). **Lowest priority in the 2026-08-10 tranche** — nothing is broken; the risk is that a future refactor of the dispatch goes unnoticed. **⚠ ADDED 2026-08-11 — this stopped being a one-off, which raises the row's value without widening its scope.** PR #468 (`8b1ce0a`) closed *its* test gap the same way, and needed **two** `internal` seams to do it: `GoogleCastOutput.ConnectRaceHookForTests` (`:54`) to interleave a teardown at the exact point between receiver resolution and the network connect, and then `ConnectTransportOverrideForTests` (`:64`) because the first could not reach the succeeded-then-lost branch offline — a fake socket cannot complete a Cast handshake, so every connect diverted into the error handler instead. **So the codebase now has three `internal`+`InternalsVisibleTo` seams that exist solely because a native/hardware dependency makes the real path untestable** (`ApplyDeferredCaptureState` here, plus #468's two). **What this changes about this row: the feasibility check should ask the general question, not just the Bluetooth one.** If the honest answer is *"native `AudioEngine` construction in tests is not practical,"* then the durable output of this row is **a stated, written convention for when a seam is acceptable and how it must be labelled** — which would also retire `AUD-3`'s residue (c) — rather than a Bluetooth-specific test. That is a legitimate close for this row and a better one than another mock. **Still forbidden, unchanged:** do not close this with a mock that re-asserts the seam from a different angle. _Anchors re-verified 2026-08-11 against `main` @ `8b1ce0a` — all byte-exact and unchanged (#468 did not touch `BluetoothAudioSource.cs`): `:159`/`:166`, `:449-452`, `:454`, `:462`, `:483`/`:494`, `:684`/`:689`, and `BluetoothAudioSourceTests.cs:917`/`:930`/`:954`._ | 📋 | _plan TBD — **feasibility check first**: if a native SoundFlow test harness is not practical, close the row and say so — **and if so, prefer closing it with the seam convention described above rather than with nothing**_ | _no spec doc — the diagnosis is in this row_ · PR #469 (merged) is where the seam and its tests landed · **PR #468 (`8b1ce0a`) is where the second and third seams landed** | — _(no dependency. **Touches the same file as AUD-1** (`BluetoothAudioSource.cs`); if both are in flight, expect line anchors to move.)_ | `test/bt-capture-branch-dispatch-coverage` |
| KIOSK-2 | **Three desktop icons, one repair-and-open action.** Four hand-drifted icons become three, and **Radio Console** becomes the single *make-everything-right* tap: probe `radio-api` / `radio-web` / `rotary-phone` / the GV bridge in parallel (≤2s), start whatever is down in dependency order, then open the kiosk. **⚠ TWO RULES INVERT THE WHOLE DESIGN IF GOT WRONG, and both are the reason this can ever be silent.** (1) **The kiosk browser is the SUBJECT of the action, not a reported component.** The desktop is only visible *when the kiosk is closed*, so the kiosk is **always** down when the icon is tapped — if starting it counted as a repair, the dialog would fire on **every single tap** and the silent path would never happen. (2) **Google Voice auth is dead ~9 minutes in every 20 by design, and that is deliberately NEVER reported.** `available` / `degraded` / `cookiesValid` on `/api/gvbridge/status` are **liars** (captured reading healthy while both SMS endpoints returned hard 502s); `psidtsAgeSeconds` is the only honest field and is a live blackout clock — `<660` healthy · `660–1200` **normal trough, silent** · `>1200` or absent = the refresh cycle itself stopped. A naive "is Google OK?" check answers *no* on ~45% of taps, and **a status surface that cries wolf is worse than no status surface**. Owner approved the **stateless `>1200` probe** (§11 Q3, no Architect escalation) and confirmed the field live: `psidtsAgeSeconds: 310`. When something *did* need fixing, one zenity dialog reports **all four** components using the corpus's existing `[label][value][pill]` grammar and `--signal-*` hex values, in three tiers mapped onto `--info` / `--warning` / `--error` — **no new severity vocabulary is invented** (two new pill *labels* are: `Starting` and `Needs sign-in`, both justified in handoff §9). Started-for-you auto-closes in 10s; degraded waits for a tap; hard failure **holds the kiosk back** and offers `Try again` / `Open anyway`. **Two ordering rules that are easy to get backwards:** on the success path the kiosk launches **before** the dialog (Wayland stacking is decided by launch order, so a dialog raised first is swallowed by the fullscreen kiosk), and on the error path the kiosk is **held back** (the error dialog carries the only explanation plus the retry). Also: `Exit Browser` → **`Exit to Desktop`** (§11 Q1 approved), scoped to the kiosk profile so the GV bridge survives — today's `pkill -f chrome` kills it and nothing restarts it on demand; **`Shutdown System` gains a confirm that never auto-proceeds** (§11 Q2 approved, overriding the earlier "leave as-is") — GNOME's own confirm carries a **60-second countdown and then powers off by itself**, so an accidental tap plus walking away powers the box off anyway; and the **Anderson Console mark finally ships** (`branding/favicon.svg`, unwired until now) to `~/.local/share/icons/radio-console/` referenced by **absolute path** — a theme name is precisely what failed here (`Icon=audio-radio` exists in no installed theme and silently resolved to a generic document glyph). **⚠ The in-app Blazor favicon (`App.razor:9`) is explicitly OUT OF SCOPE** — the owner deferred it to its own pass because `--kiosk` hides the tab strip entirely, so it is never visible on the appliance. Do not "finish the job" by changing it here. **⚠ Cross-repo boundary: `gv-bridge-ensure.sh`, its watchdog and its nightly timer are ROTARYPHONE-OWNED and are being brought under version control in that repo RIGHT NOW.** This row **invokes and probes only** — it must never own, reimplement, edit, install or `pkill` bridge startup; the script is resolved from a candidate list at runtime because the canonical path may move. **Three prerequisites, not polish:** 56px GTK buttons (GTK's ~34px default is under the 48px touch floor — **if the override cannot be made to work, say so rather than shipping 34px**), the `chmod 755` fix (`KIOSK-1`), and re-running `setup-kiosk.sh` **on the box** — merging alone changes nothing. **Testability is designed in because the box cannot be screenshotted** (Shell screenshot API + `GetWindows` are `AccessDenied` on GNOME 46; `gnome-screenshot`/`grim`/`scrot` absent): `radio-console-open` ships `--print-status`, `--dry-run` and `--dialog-demo` so every tier decision is verifiable over SSH, leaving only two checks that genuinely need the owner's eyes. Plan Tasks **B1–B8**. | ✅ | [`plans/.../kiosk-desktop-launcher.md` § Part B](superpowers/plans/2026-08-18-kiosk-desktop-launcher.md) | [handoff](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-kiosk-desktop-launcher.md) | [#480](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/480) | `feat/kiosk-desktop-launcher` |
| ENC-4 | **`EncoderHud` — every knob visible within 100 ms, on every route.** 🔴 **P0, and the defect the whole encoder design handoff exists to fix.** Two of the four knobs currently produce **no visible evidence that anything happened**, and one changes the machine's entire behaviour from an invisible internal counter — a knob that acts silently is worse than a knob that does nothing, because the user's response to silence is to turn it further. Scope: one component, **two hosts** (`EncoderHud.razor` in `MainLayout` for normal routes, again inside `Sleep.razor` with `Variant="Sleep"`, because `/sleep` is on `EmptyLayout` and `MainLayout` is not in that tree). **Geometry is the whole trick** — the HUD divides 1920 px into quarters (centres 240 / 720 / 1200 / 1680) and renders the active knob's card in its own quarter, so something lights up above the knob that was turned. Built from three pieces already in the tree: the **unused** `.snackbar-enter` / `.snackbar-exit` primitives (`design-system.css:1218-1219`), the `GainPopoverService` layout-root hosting pattern, and `SourceBubble`. **No new design tokens** (handoff §6.9 — no `--hud-*` anything). Plus **host-side long-press synthesis** at 600 ms with a ring from 300→600 ms (the protocol has no long-press gesture; this is synthesised from press/release edges), which also moves the short press **from press to release**; and **`ENC-4b`** — turning the volume knob while muted unmutes and applies the delta in the same frame, which the Designer calls "the most important small rule in this document". **`ENC-4a` (the persistent topbar `MUTED` chip) already shipped** as [#493](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/493) and is the pattern to follow for cross-route persistence, **not work to redo**. ⛔ **Does NOT remap the router's index→handler table** (still `0=Volume 1=Tuning 2=Source 3=Visualization` against a cabinet reading VOLUME / SOURCE / PRESETS / TUNING) — index 0 is VOLUME under both, so the dangerous knob is already right, and the remap belongs with `ENC-5`/`ENC-7` which introduce the handlers it would point at. ⛔ Does not build the selector overlays, the three-state wake model, blanking, or the `MEMORY`→`PRESETS` rename. 17 tasks, 6 phases, ~49 new unit/bUnit tests, and a browser Test Plan Tester can drive. | ✅ | [`design/plans/ENC-4-encoder-hud.md`](../design/plans/ENC-4-encoder-hud.md) | [punch list §3.0 `ENC-4`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) · [encoder handoff Rev 3 §6](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-rotary-encoder-mapping.md) | **✅ met** — `ENC-1` [#498](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/498) and `ENC-3` [#511](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/511) both merged | `fix/enc-4-review-findings` |
| ENC-4c | **The `EncoderHud` geometry rotation — `ENC-4` shipped it on the wrong axis.** ⚠ **Not a redesign and not a re-plan: every design question was closed before this row was claimed** (handoff Rev 4 §6.2 corrected the geometry against the owner's as-built drawing; Rev 5 closed the collisions, the centring, the keyframe exception and the phase contract). `ENC-4` divided the **1920 px width** into quarters at `left: 240 + index * 480`, which is right for four knobs in a **row beneath** the display. The drawing puts them in a **vertical column to the LEFT of the LCD**, uniform 29.63 mm pitch, 15 mm diameter, on a 406.4 × 152.4 mm panel — so the axis was wrong and all four constants with it. Cards now anchor to `left: 24px` and band down the **720 px** axis at **90 / 270 / 450 / 630**, the measured projections (93.05 / 271.02 / 448.98 / 626.95) deliberately rounded: 3.05 px of worst-case deviation is 0.508 mm on the panel against a nearest wrong band 178 px away, and *“93.05 reads as a measurement and invites the next reader to re-measure or round it; 90 reads as a layout decision.”* **The bands, the engraved names and the index→knob mapping have ONE definition** — `Radio.Core.Configuration.FrontPanelGeometry`, citing `design/hardware/front-panel-layout_4.svg` — because §6.2 names four surfaces that need them and this repo has already paid once for a value defined in three places. Three mechanics changed with the axis: `margin-left: -180px` has **no vertical twin** (card height varies — measured on the box, 178.5 px volume vs 92.5 px frequency and 113.5 px track), so the card is vertically centred on its band and **clamped ≥ 8 px inside the viewport** — load-bearing, since the measured 178.5 px card centred on band 90 would span y 0.75–179.25, i.e. **off the top of the viewport**, and on band 630 would come within 0.75 px of the bottom; the clamp engages on exactly those two bands and nowhere else, moving the card 7.25 px — expressed on the independent `translate` property so the entrance animation cannot drop it (verified mid-animation on the appliance); `snackbarSlideIn`'s `translateY(100%)` points the wrong way for a left-anchored card, so §6.1's **declared, narrow** mirrored keyframe pair is implemented as declared (⚠ **a Polisher must read `.encoder-hud-enter` as declared, not as drift**); and §6.10's phase contract is **four explicit arms, not three** — an unrecognised phase is *not holding* (it renders nothing, and a true `IsHolding` suspends the dismissal timer and strands a card forever), while **`"Value"` keeps preserving `IsHolding`** or a turn mid-hold collapses the progress ring. ⛔ **Occlusion was decided, not redesigned:** transient occlusion accepted on every band, no position / width / z-order change — per-route suppression and z-order yielding were rejected because they violate the handoff's own principle 2. ⛔ Does not remap the router's index→handler table; that is still `ENC-5` / `ENC-7`. ✅ **SHIPPED** — unit suite green, UAT on the box at 1920×720, pre-merge review clean. | ✅ | [handoff §6 Rev 5](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-rotary-encoder-mapping.md) · [`design/plans/ENC-4-encoder-hud.md`](../design/plans/ENC-4-encoder-hud.md) (geometry sections superseded) | [encoder handoff Rev 5 §6.1, §6.2, §6.7, §6.9, §6.10](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-rotary-encoder-mapping.md) · [`design/hardware/front-panel-layout_4.svg`](../design/hardware/front-panel-layout_4.svg) | **✅ met** — `ENC-4` shipped | `fix/hud-vertical-geometry` |
| ENC-5 | **The SOURCE overlay, the shared selector component, and the router remap.** 🔴 **P0.** The SOURCE knob today moves an invisible counter and logs at Debug; this row gives it a screen. Centred overlay, ~440 px, **preview-then-commit** — ⛔ do **not** "simplify" to live-commit-per-detent, which would tear down and stand up an audio source at every detent, straight into the capture-lifecycle and `autoSwitchOnConnect` bugs. **Press is one rule, not two:** press commits the highlight, so with the overlay closed it commits what is already playing — which changes nothing and opens the overlay showing you where you are. That is what makes a mis-grab free. All five states ship: A preview · B dimmed **with a reason** (reusing `SourceBubble`'s `" · offline"` idiom) · C a press on a dimmed row flashes amber 1.5 s and **leaves the overlay open**, never a silent no-op · **D in-flight spinner · E failure card** — D and E are not optional polish, because an overlay that dismisses into silence is how a person concludes the knob is broken and starts pressing repeatedly, which is the input pattern that provokes the capture-lifecycle bug. **D7 folded the radio bands in and that is where 2–3 d became 5–6 d:** committing a band while radio is *already active* is a **BAND CHANGE, not a source switch** (`SetBandAsync`, no teardown, no spinner, no fade, restore that band's last-tuned frequency); committing one from another source does both and *is* a real switch; the current-marker tracks the active **band**, so on AM row 2 is marked; and the knob and `RadioControlPanel`'s band pills must be one state. ⭐ **This row also carries the router remap** — see the ordering note below; it ends at `0 = VOLUME · 1 = SOURCE · 2 = Visualization (seat-warmer) · 3 = TUNING`. **Five deviations are declared in the plan's §0.4** and every one is a case where the handoff assumed something the tree does not have — most importantly **no tuner reports which bands it supports**, so the plan adds `IRadioControl.SupportedBands` with a default interface implementation. **Two findings correct the row's own text:** `RadioControlPanel` **already** subscribes to `RadioStateChanged`, so the one-state rule is met today and the task is a regression guard rather than a rebuild; and **`ENC-9a` did not remove `VisualizerPanel`'s local `_currentMode`** — it added the missing subscription — so the template is "one authoritative owner + typed broadcast + a component that re-syncs without echoing", not "no component may hold a copy". **Builds the shared component `ENC-7` consumes** — row model, preview machine, overlay, CSS, wire format, and the seven-row window SOURCE does not need. ✅ **SHIPPED** — [#536](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/536). End state `0 = Volume · 1 = SOURCE · 2 = Visualization · 3 = Tuning`; index 2 is `ENC-7`'s. ⚠ **`ENC-7` inherits three things:** the shared `EncoderSelectorOverlay` is consumed unchanged (its only parameter is the payload — adding a `ListKind` would undo "one component, two lists"); the plan's **44 px preset-row modifier was deliberately not built**, because nothing could apply it without that forbidden branch, so add it beside the caller that needs it; and the Settings page's cabinet-mismatch warning is now computed per knob, so `ENC-7` should not need to touch it. ⚠ **Pre-merge review found two HIGH defects** (the State D spinner dismissed itself at 1500 ms mid-switch; the no-tuner band list was cached permanently), both fixed with regression tests. ⚠ **UAT H2 passed — zero new xruns across 1,664 tuner calls — but measured ~52 ms per tuner step**, so an 8-step detent is ~416 ms and a hard flick tunes for ~6 s after the hand stops; logged in `design/FUTURE-WORK.md`, not changed here. | ✅ | [`ENC-5-source-overlay.md`](../design/plans/ENC-5-source-overlay.md) | [rotary-encoder-mapping §4.4 · §6.6 · §6.2](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-rotary-encoder-mapping.md) | **`ENC-4`** (hard — mounts into its seam and edits its files) | — |
| ENC-7 | **The PRESETS knob: recall and save on the existing bank, and the last remap.** 🔴 **P0.** Turn moves a highlight through the saved-station bank and **nothing plays**; press = **Recall** (switch source *and band* if needed, tune, play — **not** scoped to the active source, which is what keeps the knob alive from Bluetooth or Phono); long-press 600 ms = **Save to the next free slot, never overwrites**. Empty state is instructional — `NO STATIONS SAVED` / `hold this knob to save what's playing` — so the knob teaches its own use. ⚠ **The bank is not the shape the punch list describes, and this is the biggest finding: there are no 7 slots.** `RadioPresetService.MaxPresets` is **50** globally; `RadioPreset` has **no** `SlotNumber` and neither does the SQLite table — the slot is a per-band ordinal re-derived per request in `RadioController.GetPresets:585-589`; there is **no next-free-slot logic** because gaps cannot exist (deleting slot 2 of 3 renumbers the survivor). The design survives intact and is simpler — "never overwrites" is free because no overwrite path exists, and `PRESETS FULL` is kept verbatim while being honestly noted as a message that will essentially never fire. ⭐ **But one case the handoff does not cover fires constantly:** holding on a station already saved throws a duplicate error, and silence there is the same defect this arc exists to fix — the plan adds **`ALREADY SAVED · slot NN`** and flags it as an addition to the spec, not an interpretation. ⚠ **Recall cannot use `POST /api/radio/presets/{id}/load`** — it returns 400 `"Radio is not the active source"`, which is exactly the case the knob exists to serve; recall is implemented server-side instead. ⚠ **`IRadioPresetService` is scoped and the router is a singleton** — reach it through `IServiceScopeFactory`, never a constructor injection. ⭐ **The on-screen bank is renamed `MEMORY · n saved` → `PRESETS · n saved`** because the cabinet is engraved PRESETS. **This is a DELIBERATE, DECLARED deviation** from [`HANDOFF-saved-station-display.md`](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-saved-station-display.md), recorded in Rev 3's header, in Designer §4.4 and in punch-list §6 precisely so a later consistency pass does not revert it — **Builder must put it in the PR body and Polisher must not flag it as drift.** One functional line (`RadioControlPanel.razor:249`), one test (`RadioControlPanelTests.cs:835`) and ten stale comments; scope the verification grep with `--glob '!**/bin/**' --glob '!**/obj/**' --glob '!.claude/worktrees/**'` or it reports ~90 false positives from generated XML docs and stale agent worktrees. **Ends the remap at `0 = VOLUME · 1 = SOURCE · 2 = PRESETS · 3 = TUNING`** — matching the escutcheon and what `ENC-11` has pushed to the device since #509. **Consumes `ENC-5`'s component unchanged; the Web side of this row is one string.** | ✅ | [`ENC-7-presets-knob.md`](../design/plans/ENC-7-presets-knob.md) | [rotary-encoder-mapping §4.3 · §4.4 · §6.6](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-rotary-encoder-mapping.md) | **`ENC-5`** (hard — consumes its component and remaps the index it parks) | [#541](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/541) |
| ENC-8 | **The encoder Settings surface, and the stale docs behind it.** 🔴 **P0. `ENC-11`'s tiered fault model works and is verified on hardware — this is the half that lets anyone diagnose it.** Four cards under System Config → Integrations → Rotary Encoders: **Status** (connection, config tier, last verified, last saved-to-device with a staleness comparison); **Device configuration** (read-only, every field × 4 encoders, labelled by cabinet name `VOLUME · SOURCE · PRESETS · TUNING` and **never by index**, each row showing whether the device agrees); **Direction** (four `Reverse` toggles — the one editable thing; toggling pushes immediately and marks the flashed copy stale); **Actions** (`Save to device`, `Re-apply settings`, `Reset counters`). ⛔ **Factory reset is deliberately absent and a test pins it absent** — it would put the device on defaults where one volume detent spans the full range. ⚠ **The `Save to device` copy is load-bearing and a reviewer should treat a mismatch as a defect:** flash holds exactly the operating configuration, i.e. exactly what the screen shows — enforced structurally (screen, push and flash read one object from one call) and pinned by a test, not left to care. Underneath it: an `IRotaryEncoderProvisioning` facet on the same `HidRotaryEncoderService` instance, retained sent/read-back state and timestamps, **the first code in this repo that ever sends `SaveConfig` (0x01)**, staleness decided by comparing a stored SHA-256 of the flashed bytes rather than inferred from a timestamp, and a maintenance channel that writes under a lock while the read loop stays the only reader. The mapping table is served **from the router** so it cannot drift again — one array that both dispatch and display read, which makes `ENC-5`/`ENC-7`'s remap a one-line edit. Plus the doc fixes: `INTEGRATIONS.md:22-26` (wrong 8-byte report format, superseded by `ENC-1`), `:125` (swap-the-A/B-pins advice, superseded by `reverse`), `:80`/`:95` (`TuningStepKHz` — deleted from code by #490, still documented), `:88` (`Enabled` documented as defaulting `false`; it is `true` since `ENC-0`), `:5` (“all integrations are disabled by default”), and `CLAUDE.md`'s MudBlazor claim. `VolumeStepPercent`'s duplicate **editable** numeric is deleted; the value itself is kept (the router reads it every detent) and relocated read-only into card 2. **20 tasks, 5 phases, a hardware gate at Task 4, and a 16-step browser Test Plan at 1920×720.** | ✅ | [`design/plans/ENC-8-encoder-settings-surface.md`](../design/plans/ENC-8-encoder-settings-surface.md) | [punch list §3.5 `ENC-8`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) · [encoder handoff Rev 3 §7.7–§7.8](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-rotary-encoder-mapping.md) | **✅ met** — `ENC-1`, `ENC-2` and `ENC-11` all merged (O10). Not blocked by `ENC-4`, but shares three files with it (`RotaryEncoderActionRouter.cs`, `AudioServiceExtensions.cs`, the `ApiModels.cs` tail) — expect to rebase. | [#527](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/527) |
| ENC-12 | **Tiered config-fault surfacing — giving `ENC-11`'s safety response a voice.** 🔴 **P0, and the row that closes the encoder arc's worst silence.** When a safety field does not read back, the host **already** drops the volume knob's per-event clamp from 6 units to 2 and holds it there until a push verifies. That response is real, correct, verified on hardware — and completely invisible. The owner experiences it as a volume knob that has quietly gone sluggish inside sealed furniture. Two surfaces: **(1)** a cross-route fault badge on the **Settings** nav pill, reusing the bell-failure §3.7 pattern verbatim (same corner, same 12 px glyph) — amber `warning` for Degraded, `--signal-red` `error` for a hard fault, amber `link_off` for absent. **Three glyphs, not one glyph in two colours:** Designer separates the tiers by colour alone, and colour alone fails WCAG 1.4.1 and this project's own §8.3 rule. **(2) One** Radzen notification, **once per session, on escalation only** — *“Knob settings couldn't be applied. The knobs still work, but they may feel wrong.”* / *“Knob safety settings couldn't be applied. Volume is limited until this is fixed.”* The latch is monotonic and **never resets**, so `Degraded → Configured → Degraded` speaks **once**; ten literal tests prove every row of the table. **A healthy boot stays completely silent** — no toast, no splash, no banner. Also adds the `ConfigStatusChanged` event and its SignalR broadcast (**the tier only** — the field detail stays on `ENC-8`'s page, which is the same `UI-2` load argument answering a different question), **resets the tier to `Unknown` on disconnect** (today an unplugged device still reports `Configured`, and the same value drives the volume clamp), and finally consumes `ENC-0`'s `WasEverConnected` flag, which nothing has used since it shipped. ⛔ **Adds no markup to the Settings page and no endpoint to `IntegrationsController`** — the punch list attributes the `Sent` vs `Read back` table and its buttons to this row; they are `ENC-8`'s, decided once in the joint planning pass. **11 tasks, 5 phases, 15-step browser Test Plan.** | ✅ **PR [#535](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/535)** | [`design/plans/ENC-12-config-fault-surfacing.md`](../design/plans/ENC-12-config-fault-surfacing.md) | [punch list §3.5 `ENC-12`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) · [encoder handoff Rev 3 §7.4/§7.6](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-rotary-encoder-mapping.md) · [bell-failure handoff §3.7](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-bell-failure-surfacing.md) | **✅ met** — `ENC-8` [#527](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/527) and `ENC-4` / `ENC-4c` all merged. | — |
| PHN-1a | **ADR-029 PR 1 — the event-playback contracts.** 🔴 **P0, `O6`, and the head of the seven-PR phone arc.** A new **`IEventPlaybackService` in `Radio.Core`, sitting BESIDE `IAnnouncementService` rather than inside it** — the announcement service is fire-and-forget *by signature* (bare `Task`, no identity, one global `StopAsync`) and has two callers that depend on exactly that; the split it encodes is **attended vs unattended**, which is what PR 4 and PR 5 branch on. Plus the **closed discriminated `EventPlaybackRequest` with asymmetric arms** — speech carries literal text, voicemail carries a `(kind, id, duration)` **reference** — and the `EventPlaybackSnapshot` anchor (position + broadcast time + state; **no position tick**, ADR §8.2). Plus `IEventAudioSource` gaining `Position` / `IsSeekable` / `SeekAsync` / `PauseAsync` / `ResumeAsync`, copied verbatim from `IPrimaryAudioSource` and implemented on `AudioFileEventSource` and `TTSEventSource`. ⚠ **NEVER a caller-supplied URL** — that is an SSRF primitive, and "it's a LAN kiosk" is not a defence. Pinned two ways: `Validate` rejects a URL-bearing `MediaId` with a named reason, and a reflection test asserts the request type declares **no** URL-shaped property at all. ⚠ **PLANNING FOUND THE ADR WRONG ON ITS OWN LOW-RISK CLAIM, and it adds a task.** ADR §8.3 says D4 is "a lift" because `FilePlayerAudioSource` "already implements seeking through `SoundFlowPlaybackService`". **`SoundFlowPlaybackService` has no seek method at all**, and `FilePlayerAudioSource.SeekCoreAsync` (`:910-922`) assigns `_position = position` and returns — it moves a field, not any audio, under an `IsSeekable => true` at `:119`. That is a **fourth** instance of the `CLAUDE.md` § Pre-Merge Review comment-accuracy class; a **fifth** is `SoundFlowPlaybackService.GetPosition` returning `TimeSpan.Zero` behind the comment *"Position tracking not available in current SoundFlow API"* — false: `ISoundPlayer.Time` exists in the restored SoundFlow 1.4.1, along with `bool Seek(TimeSpan, SeekOrigin)`. **So PR 1 builds the primitive (Task 4) instead of lifting one; `GetPosition` has zero callers, so the fix carries no blast radius.** `FilePlayerAudioSource` is **NOT** fixed here — live primary path, own UAT, logged to `FUTURE-WORK` instead. ⚠ **Two completion traps the ADR half-missed:** a seek OR a pause makes `AudioFileEventSource`'s wall-clock `Task.Delay(_duration)` (`:205`) fire at the wrong time (ADR §14 Q4 caught only the seek half), and pausing a `TTSEventSource` would make its monitor loop read `!IsPlaying(Id)` as **natural completion** (`:169-175`) — no document mentions this one. Both fixed by re-arming on transport events; **no poll and no timer is added** (ADR §1.3). ⛔ **Do NOT use `POST /api/sources/events/{tts,file}` as a template** — re-verified live at the cited lines: `_duckingService` taken at `:44` and never read, `mixer.AddSource` at `:651` never removed or disposed, `PlayFileEvent` double-plays via `:719` then `:732`. 🎁 **Ships a gift to PR 4:** `DuckingServiceCharacterizationTests` pins that ducking is **binary and transition-only today**, so PR 4's change to it lands as an edited test in PR 4's own diff rather than as a silent shift inside a shared audio service. | ✅ | [`design/plans/PHN-1a-event-playback-seam-contracts.md`](../design/plans/PHN-1a-event-playback-seam-contracts.md) | [ADR-029](../design/decisions/2026-08-03-gv-audio-through-engine.md) D1/D2/D4 · [PHN arc PR breakdown](../design/plans/PHN-arc-pr-breakdown.md) PR 1 · [punch list §3.5 `PHN-1`, §2 `O6`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) · [phone/audio handoff](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-phone-console-audio-and-canned-replies.md) | [#528](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/528) · **None — was the head of the arc.** `O6` makes it the predecessor of `PHN-2`; PRs 2-7 of the breakdown all sit behind it and are **not yet planned**. | `feat/phn-1a-event-playback` |
| OPS-5 | **Stale static assets on the kiosk — `Cache-Control` was absent entirely.** **This is the hole `OPS-1` left in deploy verification, and it silently invalidated any UAT of a styling change.** `Radio.Web`'s `UseStaticFiles()` sent `ETag` and `Last-Modified` but **no `Cache-Control` at all**, so browsers applied heuristic freshness (RFC 9111 §4.2.2) and reused `css/`, `js/`, fonts and the Radzen theme **without revalidating** — the server offered a cheap way to check and never told the client to use it. Measured 2026-09-02 while UAT'ing `ENC-4c`: the kiosk painted a `design-system.css` of 775 rules missing the whole `ENC-4` block, i.e. a copy predating a deploy earlier the same day, and those measurements only became valid after a forced reload. **A CSS-only change could land, pass the SHA check on both services, and still not be on the panel** — `OPS-1` verifies the git SHA stamped into each *assembly*, a true statement about which binary runs and a silent one about which bytes a browser paints. The kiosk relaunch does not help either: Chrome's HTTP cache lives in the profile directory and outlives the process. **Fix:** `Cache-Control: no-cache` on everything `UseStaticFiles` serves, via a testable `StaticAssetCaching.CreateOptions()` (7 unit tests). That is *revalidate before reuse*, **not** *don't store* — the existing `ETag` turns it into a `304` with **0 bytes** (measured; vs 193,069 on a full GET). **Applied uniformly on purpose:** nothing this app links is content-hashed — it is Blazor *Server*, so `_framework/` carries `blazor.web.js` at a fixed path rather than a fingerprinted set, and `_content/Radzen.Blazor/*`, `Radio.Web.styles.css`, `css/*`, `js/*`, `fonts/*` are all stable names (`js/virtual-keyboard.js?v=2` is a *hand-maintained* buster — evidence of someone patching this by hand once). A long `max-age` on any of them is this same bug with a longer fuse; caching hard needs fingerprinting first (`MapStaticAssets` + `@Assets[...]`), a larger change deliberately not taken. `Radio.API` serves no static files and needed nothing. `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1` already deleted the kiosk profile's cache for this reason (since 2026-08-18) — one profile on one box, nothing for a LAN client, nothing under `-NoRestart`; left in place, but no longer what correctness rests on. **Verified on the appliance:** header present on all 6 asset classes; `If-None-Match` → `304` **carrying the header** (so `OnPrepareResponse` runs on the revalidation path — confirmed, not assumed); and the bug itself closed — a CSS probe reached the kiosk's *computed style* over CDP after a plain `location.reload()` **with no cache clear, no restart and no relaunch**, isolating the header as the sole mechanism. | ✅ | _no plan — small operational fix, scoped in this row_ | `CLAUDE.md` § Deployment ("Verifying a deploy actually landed") | — | `fix/static-asset-cache-control` |
| PHN-1b | **ADR-029 PR 2 — `GvMediaClient`, the bounded cache, and API-side auth.** 🔴 **P0, `O6`, second of the seven-PR phone arc.** A typed `GvMediaClient` in `Radio.Infrastructure/External/` that fetches a voicemail recording **server-side** into a size-bounded LRU disk cache at `./data/gvmedia/`, a `GvMediaAuthHandler` that can carry `X-RotaryPhone-Auth` where a browser `<audio>` element never could, and Radio.API's own `GvMedia` config block. **No endpoint, no `EventPlaybackService`, nothing that plays a sound** — PR 3 is the first PR a user can reach. ⚠ **The cache is the load-bearing part and must not be reviewed as an optimisation.** GV auth is dead ~9 minutes in every 20 (`XR-3`) and their `/api/gvbridge/status` reports healthy *during* the blackout, so a replay 30 s later has ~45% odds of 502ing without it. Its correctness properties are: a hit never touches the network, eviction **actually deletes**, and `CacheMaxMegabytes = 0` is a **no-cache** path rather than an infinitely-evicting one (ADR ⟨A1·2⟩). ⚠ **PLANNING FOUND THE ADR WRONG ON THE CLAIM THAT SET THIS ROW'S RISK.** ADR §5.1/§10.1 say Radio.API has *"no `AddHttpClient`, no `IHttpClientFactory`, and no `DelegatingHandler` infrastructure at all"* and the breakdown concludes the handler infrastructure is *"genuinely net-new"*. The grep is right about the **directory** and wrong about the **container**: `Program.cs:102` → `AddSoundFlowAudio` → `AudioServiceExtensions.cs:257` `AddPhoneIntegration` (unconditional) → `:469` `AddHttpClient<PhoneContactLookupService>()`, and `Program.cs:107` → `AddRadioWeather` → two more named clients. `IHttpClientFactory` resolves in Radio.API **today**, and `Microsoft.Extensions.Http` is already a package reference. Genuinely net-new is only the handler **type**, which has a 31-line template plus tests in `Radio.Web`. ⚠ **ADR §5's registration instruction walks into the DI hazard this row exists to close** — *"beside its sibling in `AudioServiceExtensions.cs`"*, but that sibling lives inside `AddPhoneIntegration`, reachable only through `AddSoundFlowAudio`, so registering there buries the feature in the audio graph and any test resolving it initialises real audio hardware. That is exactly why `ActiveSourceAccessorRegistrationTests` can only inspect descriptors, and therefore exactly why **nothing in this repo would catch a missing registration today** — `ValidateOnBuild`/`ValidateScopes` appear **nowhere** in `src/` or `tests/`. **Scoping correction:** a standalone `AddGvMedia` following the `AddRadioWeather` precedent, which is what makes this row's **first real build-and-resolve guard** possible. ⚠ **ADR §5.1 tells this row to copy `PhoneContactLookupService`'s masking, and that file leaks on four lines against one** — raw number at `:62` (Information, **with the contact's full name**), `:78`, `:96-97`, and `:102` (Warning, so it still reaches the journal after `LOG-11`); the one masked line still logs `contact.Name` in full. **A fifth leak nobody had named: exception messages**, which every caller's catch logs. Rule here is *no raw id in a log message, a log argument, or an exception message*, masked as a **hash prefix** (`gvm:1a2b3c4d`) rather than `***last4` — nobody recognises a voicemail id, so a suffix leaks characters for zero operator benefit — pinned by a test that captures every line at every level across success/404/502/timeout/cache-hit. ⚠ **Four decisions PR 1 handed forward are settled here, so they cannot drift again:** `MaxSpeechChars` is **rejection, not truncation** (ADR §4.2 overridden — its own rule is that utterance composition belongs to `Radio.Web`, and a server that speaks less than it was asked while returning 200 is the untruth PR 1 refused when it made `SeekAsync` throw); `IEventAudioSource.SeekAsync` **stays `Task`** (widening breaks D4's verbatim-lift and drags in `FilePlayerAudioSource`, a live primary path — a refused seek is still visible as an anchor that does not move); `Label` gets `MaxLabelChars = 128` in `Validate`, **implemented by PR 3** because it would be unreachable here; and the masking rule above. ⛔ **Do NOT use `POST /api/sources/events/{tts,file}` as a template** — re-verified live at `c830fb8`: `_duckingService` taken at `:44`, never read; `mixer.AddSource` at `:651` never removed or disposed; `PlayFileEvent` double-plays via `:719` then `:732`. ⛔ **Do not touch `DuckingServiceCharacterizationTests`** — PR 4's tripwire, all four green. ⚠ **Deploy trap the ADR's D8 table depends on and does not mention:** `src/Radio.API/appsettings.Production.json` **does not exist**, the per-machine file ships from `deploy/<target>/`, and `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1:217` excludes it from rsync while `:222` seeds it **only when absent** — so a repo edit to it will **not** reach a box that already has one. Defaults therefore go in `src/Radio.API/appsettings.json` (which *is* overwritten), and flipping `AuthKey` on a live box is a documented hand edit plus a restart. | ✅ | [`design/plans/PHN-1b-gvmedia-client-cache-and-auth.md`](../design/plans/PHN-1b-gvmedia-client-cache-and-auth.md) | [ADR-029](../design/decisions/2026-08-03-gv-audio-through-engine.md) D3/D8 · [PHN arc PR breakdown](../design/plans/PHN-arc-pr-breakdown.md) PR 2 · [punch list §3.5 `PHN-1`, §2 `O6`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) · [`PHN-1a` plan](../design/plans/PHN-1a-event-playback-seam-contracts.md) §0.4/§5 — **authority over the ADR wherever they disagree** | **`PHN-1a`** ✅ [#528](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/528) — merged, so this row is claimable now. Blocks PR 3 (`EventPlaybackService` + `POST /api/audio/events`), which consumes `GvMediaClient` and `GvMediaOptions`. `O6` still governs the arc. | ✅ [#534](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/534) — merged |
| ENC-6 | **Sleep, wake, and the three-state model — the non-blanking half.** 🔴 **P0, the last unplanned one, and the row that closes the overnight failure mode.** ⚠ **THE BLANKING HALF IS WITHDRAWN AND MUST NOT BE REINSTATED.** `ENC-15` was its hard predecessor and **its gate FAILED** on 2026-09-02 ([report](uat/2026-09-02-enc15-touch-wake-gate/REPORT.md)): the touchscreen is powered by the panel and **leaves the USB bus when it blanks**, so touch cannot wake it — there is no device left to generate the event — and `cafe:4005` has **zero evdev nodes**, so a knob cannot wake the compositor either. That is one *application-mediated* wake path where two were required, and the investigation **reproduced the brick** (a ~13 s on/off oscillation the documented recovery line could not break). **So this row builds NO DPMS, no `Ambient-Dark`, no `Standby-Dark`, no re-blank timers and no encoder-presence coupling rules.** Designer Rev 5 §8.5 and §15's first and last *Sleep, wake, blanking* checkboxes are out of scope. If a later session reads “ENC-6” and reaches for blanking, `design/INTEGRATIONS.md` §1 and `design/FUTURE-WORK.md` §7 (Sleep Mode) both carry the reason it stays off. <br><br>**What ships — and the five-state model does still cohere as three.** Rule 1 (“if the panel is dark the first input lights it”) governed *only* the two dark states, so it drops out whole; Rule 2 governs all three survivors and §8.3 already specifies both remaining columns completely. **(1) The headline defect, and it is current behaviour:** idle-at-30-min navigates to `/sleep` **without calling `SleepService`**, so `IsSleeping` stays `false` and the API never learns the clock is up. Fixed by **`Sleep.razor` reporting itself** through a new `POST /api/system/sleep-screen` — which also collapses the finding a Builder confirmed today while shipping `ENC-4`, that **`/sleep` reached by idle and `/sleep` reached by the Sleep pill are different states**. ⚠ **Do NOT “fix” this by making `idle-dimmer.js` call `SetSleepAsync(true)`** — Ambient is *defined* by audio still playing, so that converts it into Standby. **(2) The wake latch.** `TryWakeFromSleep` calls `WakeAsync` fire-and-forget and returns `true`, so a 10 ms poll discards every following event; a synchronous `TryClaimWake()` makes the state read `Awake` from the claim instant. Under the new model the window is **worse** than the handoff assumed — leaving Ambient needs a *browser round trip*, not just an await. *A fast spin must lose one detent, not twelve.* **(3) D22, settled scope, not an open question:** from Standby a **turn** lights nothing new and **does not resume audio**; a **press** (any button) or a **screen tap** does. Owner approved Designer's narrowing verbatim — *“that D8 narrowing is fine, keep it.”* **(4) Rule 2:** VOLUME **acts in place** on the lit clock and does not change screens; everything else wakes to the full UI and is consumed. **(5) The refinement that turns a loss into an answer:** a consumed input still renders that knob's **current value** — *the first detent tells you where you are, the second one moves it* — which is also what stops three knobs looking broken for a whole standby, since D22 makes every turn there consumed. Plus the Standby hint, closing the `TODO(ENC-6)` `Sleep.razor:113-116` has carried since `ENC-4`. <br><br>⚠ **Three things a reviewer should check rather than assume.** **(a)** This row **does not depend on `ENC-5`/`ENC-7` and is not blocked by them, in either order** — the gate runs *before* the dispatch tables and the only index it compares is `0`, which is VOLUME under both mappings (already pinned by `EncoderIndexZero_IsVolume_UnderBothTheOldAndTheNewPhysicalOrder`); the consumed-value readout is a **fourth parallel array beside the two `ENC-5` Task 7 already reorders**, index threaded in so no literal is left behind. If `ENC-5` lands first, re-read the router — its handlers gain a leading `int index` and **the code wins**. **(b)** Two shipped assertions **invert and must be updated, not deleted**: `WakeConsumesThePressEdge_…`'s `Assert.Empty(h.Hud.Published)` (a consumed input now publishes, by design) and `SleepService`'s class doc, which claims DPMS control the code has never performed — a **fourth** comment/code mismatch of the class `CLAUDE.md`'s pre-merge rule exists for. **(c)** `dotnet test` **cannot** cover the new HTTP round trip: `AddHermeticTestRig` fails every outbound call by design, so a null result is the *expected* state — exactly how `ENC-8` shipped a page that could not deserialize its own response with a green suite. **UAT scenario B is the only gate on it, and the plan's Test Plan exercises both `/sleep` entry paths separately** because a run through the pill produces a false pass for the idle path. <br><br>📋 **PLANNED 2026-09-02** — [`design/plans/ENC-6-sleep-wake-state-model.md`](../design/plans/ENC-6-sleep-wake-state-model.md), **10 tasks** across 4 phases, **2 declared deviations** (§0.4) and **3 mechanism decisions** the handoff does not make (§0.3).<br><br> ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-09-02** — [#539](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/539). Three states derived from `IsSleeping` + a new `IsSleepScreenVisible` the `/sleep` page reports about itself; the sleep gate, the `TryClaimWake()` latch, D22, the consumed-value readout and the Standby hint all landed. **Blanking did not ship and must not be reinstated.** ⚠ **Three things the plan got wrong, all verified against the tree:** (1) its `_currentValuePublishers` literal was the **pre-`ENC-5`** order and would have put a TUNING readout on the SOURCE band — *the exact defect its own four-array test exists to catch* — and (2) **that test would not have caught it**, because it asserted only `card.EncoderIndex`, which every publisher threads through from its own parameter; it now pins the label per index. (3) `SystemControllerTests` is an HTTP integration class, so the plan's controller-construction helpers do not exist; Task 3's facts were rewritten over real HTTP and assert the **raw wire text** `"wakeState":"Ambient"`, which catches the enum-as-number regression a typed read would accept. **Also: "0 warnings" was never true here** — `main` carries 53 pre-existing `IDE0011`; the set is byte-identical before and after. ⚠ **Open follow-up, recorded in `design/FUTURE-WORK.md` §7 rather than fixed:** `IsSleepScreenVisible` is in-memory, so an API restart while the kiosk is parked on `/sleep` reinstates this row's headline defect until something re-renders — and every deploy restarts `radio-api`. `SetSleepScreenVisible` was made **edge-triggered** here so the re-report heartbeat that closes it cannot wipe an in-flight wake claim. | ✅ | [`ENC-6-sleep-wake-state-model.md`](../design/plans/ENC-6-sleep-wake-state-model.md) — 10 tasks | [punch list §3.5 `ENC-6`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) · [encoder handoff **Rev 5** §8](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-rotary-encoder-mapping.md) · [`ENC-15` gate report](uat/2026-09-02-enc15-touch-wake-gate/REPORT.md) — **withdraws the blanking half** | **✅ met** — `ENC-1` [#498](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/498) and `ENC-4` [#519](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/519) / `ENC-4c` [#526](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/526) all merged. **`ENC-15` was the hard predecessor of the blanking half only, and that half is out of scope.** Not blocked by `ENC-5`/`ENC-7` in either order, but **shares `RotaryEncoderActionRouter.cs` with both** — expect to rebase. | `feat/enc-6-sleep-wake` |
| TEST-4 | **The BT capture watchdog's flaky test — a wall-clock test window racing an independent wall-clock poll loop.** `BluetoothCaptureWatchdogTests.IntermittentStall_ResetsCounter` failed `Build and Test` on `main` at `2a81f56` and passed on `c3b62d8` with no change to the file: timing-sensitive, not a regression. ⚠ **"It passed on the retry" was not the resolution** — per `TEST-1`/`TEST-3` and criterion (d), a randomly-red suite trains everyone to re-run rather than read, and five Builders were merging into `main` the day it surfaced. **The root cause is entirely in the test; the production watchdog is correct** and was not changed — which matters, because `AUD-8`'s watchdog guards a real long-uptime capture failure and this repo has a documented long-running capture-lifecycle bug. The test sequenced three phases with `await Task.Delay(60)` while `BluetoothCaptureWatchdog` polled the snapshot source on its own independent `Task.Delay(20)` tick, **with no rendezvous between the two clocks**. Nothing guaranteed the watchdog ever observed the middle, below-threshold phase — the one whose entire job is to reset `_consecutiveStalledChecks`. **Reproduced at 13/200 under 64 concurrent CPU burners on a 32-core host**, and the poll log names the mechanism exactly: the whole phase-2 window (`t=60ms → t=125ms`) elapsed **without a single poll** — ticks stretched to 42–91 ms, the reset value was set and replaced unobserved, and phase 1's two stalled polls concatenated with phase 3's three into the five consecutive checks that then **correctly** fired. Given the polls it actually received, raising was the right behaviour. **Fixed test-only.** The fake `ICaptureStreamSnapshotSource` is now a **rendezvous gate**: the watchdog parks on entry to every `GetCaptureStreamSnapshot()` until the test grants that poll, so a test states how many polls observe each value and every assertion runs while the watchdog is frozen before its next read. Polls are counted, not timed; `WatchdogTickIntervalMs` now affects only how fast the tests run, never whether they pass. **Verified 200/200 at the same 64-burner load that produced the 13/200 failure rate** — the apples-to-apples comparison — plus 200/200 and 360/360 (all six tests) at 40 burners: **760 executions under load, zero failures**, against load severe enough to slow each iteration ~12x. ⚠ **No box UAT is owed and none was run** — this is test infrastructure with no user-visible surface; `build.yml` is the gate. **Sibling tests:** three shared the shape but only in the *safe* direction (starvation could only remove polls, never flip an assertion red); they are now exact rather than "at least" assertions. **Two further defects found and fixed while there.** (1) `NullSnapshotSource_ExitsImmediatelyWithoutRaising` documented that it *"verifies the watchdog idles (never enters its main loop)"* while asserting only `ConsecutiveStalledChecksForTest == 0` — which **reads 0 on both paths**, because a null snapshot resets the counter every tick. The doc claimed a verification the assertion did not perform: a sixth instance of the `CLAUDE.md` comment-overclaim class. It now asserts `ExecuteTask` reaches `RanToCompletion`, which is what actually distinguishes the early return from the loop. (2) `AboveThreshold_RaisesAfterConsecutiveChecks` asserted `RaiseCount >= 1`; it now pins the exact count, the one-short boundary, and the post-fire reset. **The pattern is recorded in `CLAUDE.md` → "Test Timing — never race a wall clock against a wall clock"**, because it is not a one-off: the same shape is live in `Radio.Web.Tests` (see the `NowPlayingPanel` fast-follow below). | ✅ | — _(Builder-discovered; no Planner spec — the diagnosis is in this row and in the test file's class-level `<remarks>`)_ | `CLAUDE.md` → **Test Timing** · `tests/Radio.Infrastructure.Tests/Audio/Services/BluetoothCaptureWatchdogTests.cs` `<remarks>` | — _(no dependency; test-only, touches no production file)_ | `fix/flaky-bt-capture-watchdog-test` |
| PHN-5 | **`PhoneContactLookupService` logs raw phone numbers on four lines and the contact's real name on the one line it calls masked.** ⚠ **This is a PII-in-logs defect, not a style issue** — filed by `PHN-1b` rather than fixed there, because it is a different subsystem with its own callers and deserves its own reviewable change. Verified against the tree, `src/Radio.Infrastructure/External/PhoneContactLookupService.cs`: **`:62`** `LogInformation("PBAP contact found: {Number} → {Name}", phoneNumber, contact.DisplayName)` — raw number **and the contact's real name**, at **Information**, which since `LOG-11` persists to the file sink at `/opt/radio-console/logs/radio-*.txt`; **`:78`** `LogDebug("Looking up contact for {PhoneNumber}", phoneNumber)` — raw; **`:96-97`** `LogDebug("Contact lookup returned {StatusCode} for {PhoneNumber}", …, phoneNumber)` — raw; **`:102`** `LogWarning(ex, "Contact lookup failed for {PhoneNumber}", phoneNumber)` — raw, at **Warning**, so it is one of the few that still reaches `journalctl` after `LOG-11`, **and it logs `ex`**, so any exception whose `Message` carries the number leaks a second way. The single line the file treats as masked, **`:90`**, masks the number to `***1234` but still logs **`contact.Name` in full** — and ADR-029 §5.1 asks for *"voicemail ids **and caller identity**"* to be masked. So the count is **four raw-number sites against one masked, and the masked one leaks the name anyway.** ⚠ **ADR-029 §5.1 cites this exact file as the masking *example* to copy**, which is how the pattern propagates; `PHN-1b` refused the citation and masked as a hash prefix on every line instead, pinned by a test. **Exposure, stated honestly rather than inflated:** the only caller of `FindCallerNameAsync` is `Radio.API/Services/PhoneCallIntegrationService.cs:121`, whose `ExecuteAsync` returns at `:44` when `PhoneIntegration:Enabled` is false — and it is false and has never been true. So this is **latent today and becomes live the moment that flag flips**, which is a one-line config change with no code review between it and the leak. ⚠ **A second, smaller instance for the same Planner pass to scope in:** `src/Radio.Web/Services/ContactResolutionService.cs:173` logs a raw number at **Debug** and is **not gated at all** — it is injected into `PhoneMessagesPanel.razor`, the live `/phone` texts surface. Debug is normally below the configured minimum level, so it likely does not emit today; that is a level away from leaking, not a design that prevents it. **Fix shape (~15 lines):** compute the masked form once at method entry and use only it; mask `contact.Name`/`DisplayName` the same way; do not pass `ex` where its message can carry the number. | 📋 | _none — needs a Planner pass_ | [ADR-029](../design/decisions/2026-08-03-gv-audio-through-engine.md) §5.1 (the citation that propagates it) · [`PHN-1b` plan](../design/plans/PHN-1b-gvmedia-client-cache-and-auth.md) §0.3 ⓸ (the count, and the masking rule that replaced it) · `design/FUTURE-WORK.md` | **None.** Independent of the ADR-029 arc — `PHN-1b` only *found* it. Best done before `PhoneIntegration:Enabled` is ever flipped. | — |
| PHN-1c | **ADR-029 PR 3 — `EventPlaybackService` and the `/api/audio/events` route family.** 🔴 **P0, `O6`, third of the seven-PR phone arc, and the first PR a user can reach.** Implements `IEventPlaybackService`: one in-flight attended playback, a server-minted `playbackId`, a lifecycle that ends exactly once, and ADR §3.3's six routes. Both arms land here — `RemoteMedia` fetches through `GvMediaClient` into the bounded cache and plays the local file as an ordinary file event; `Speech` synthesises through `ITTSFactory` with the engine resolved explicitly. **No `Radio.Web` change, no `/hubs/audio` broadcast, no ducking change, no config key.** ⚠ **THE SHAPE IS `202` + BACKGROUND ACQUISITION, AND FOUR DECISIONS FOLLOW FROM IT.** PR 1's shipped `StartAsync` doc says it returns at `Preparing` and ADR §3.3 says `202`, so acquisition outlives the response: (1) the acquisition token **must not** be `HttpContext.RequestAborted`; (2) a fetch failure is a `FailureReason` on a `Failed` snapshot, not a status code; (3) `Preparing` must be bounded; (4) no exception may escape the background task. ⚠ **`GvMediaCache.WriteAsync`'s remark says PR 3 passes `HttpContext.RequestAborted`. It must not, and the remark is corrected in this PR (Task 11).** `RequestAborted` is request-scoped on a pooled, reset context — binding acquisition to it would cancel **every** fetch the instant it was accepted, so the RemoteMedia arm would fail 100% of the time looking like a network fault. PR 2's staging fix is still right and still load-bearing; only its stated *trigger* was wrong. The cancellation that actually exists is `StopAsync`, a replacing `StartAsync`, and disposal — all three land mid-`WriteAsync`. ⚠ **PLANNING SETTLED PR 2'S DEFERRED GVBRIDGE QUESTION BY READING THEIR SOURCE, AND FOUND A BUG THAT INVERTS A HANDOFF INSTRUCTION.** `D:\prj\RotaryPhone\...\GvVoicemailController.cs`: the audio route is `PhysicalFileResult("audio/mpeg")` with `Content-Length` present on the whole-body GET this client issues, and answers `200`/`404`/`502`, plus `401` once `GvBridgeAuthMiddleware`'s default-off gate is configured — so PR 2's size bound and auth handler are both correct. **But `GetAudio` resolves through `FindNodeAsync`, which never checks `ListVoicemailsAsync`'s `Succeeded` flag** — unlike the sibling `GetList`, which guards it and returns 502 with a comment saying why. A failed authenticated list returns an **empty** item set, so `FirstOrDefault` yields null and **the route answers `404 "has no recording"` during a GV auth blackout** — ~9 minutes in every 20. Both handoffs say map `NotFound` → 404 *"retrying will not help"*, and `IsPermanent` says the same: **wrong ~45% of the time**, telling a user a voicemail is permanently gone when it will play in a minute. That is the `GV-6`/`GV-8` class arriving through an unwatched door. **Fix: `IsPermanent` becomes `Disabled`-only** (Task 10), the name `MediaNotFound` survives so nothing is collapsed, and the cross-repo ask is written out for the owner rather than filed here. ⚠ **`TTSOptions.GenerationTimeoutSeconds` has never had a reader** — declaration, a Web DTO twin and a `RadzenNumeric` the owner can set, and nothing in `src/Radio.Infrastructure`. So synthesis is unbounded and a hang would park the seam in `Preparing` forever; PR 3 gives the knob its first reader by bounding its own acquisition, without touching `TTSFactory`. ⚠ **The `TTSParameters` trap was four fields wide when this row was written; `TTS-9` closed two of them, and it is now TWO.** `Engine` and `Voice` are nullable as of [#548](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/548) and fall back to configuration correctly; `Speed` and `Pitch` are still non-nullable with `1.0f` initializers, so any non-null `TTSParameters` silently pins them to the type's default rather than to `TTS:DefaultSpeed` / `DefaultPitch`. All four are filled explicitly anyway — passing `null` would be correct only until a `VoiceId` is set, which is the trap re-armed. ⚠ **The pin only has teeth because its fixture configures NON-default speed and pitch:** the shipped `1.0f` defaults are identical to the type's own initializers, so the plan's original fixture would have passed against the exact bug it exists to catch. ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-09-04 by the `PHN-1c` Builder cycle — the security item this row carried is DISCHARGED, and the instruction it gave is withdrawn.** This row previously said `TTSFactory.GenerateESpeakAsync` interpolates the caller's voice into a process argument line, that `espeak-ng -w <path>` is an arbitrary file write, and that PR 3 should **file the live one in `design/FUTURE-WORK.md`**. **All of that predates `TTS-9`** ([#548](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/548)), which removed eSpeak entirely: `GenerateESpeakAsync` no longer exists, and punch-list `SEC-4` is ✅ closed. **Nothing was filed and nothing should be** — filing a hole that does not exist is how a future session spends a cycle looking for it. ⚠ **The `VoiceId` allow-list still shipped, on different and still-valid grounds:** it bounds this seam's own new caller-supplied string to `[A-Za-z0-9._~+-]`, 64 chars, which is a strict subset of any known-voice set, so it pre-empts nothing. The surviving **class** question — allow-listing against the engines' known voices — is punch-list **`SEC-5`** and belongs to Planner. ⚠ **The `TTSParameters` trap is TWO fields wide, not four:** `TTS-9` made `Engine` and `Voice` nullable, so only `Speed` and `Pitch` still pin to the type's `1.0f` initializers. Recorded in `design/FUTURE-WORK.md` § *TTS seam* item 1. ⚠ **A stop after natural completion raises `PlaybackCompleted` TWICE** — both sources raise from their monitor *and* from `StopCoreAsync`, and `AudioSourceBase.StopAsync` short-circuits only on `Created`/`Disposed`. `AnnouncementService` is immune by accident (`TrySetResult`); a state machine is not, so terminal transition is once-only via `Interlocked`. ⚠ **`AudioFileEventSourceFactory.CreateFromFileAsync` cannot be used for a cached recording** — it re-roots relative paths against `FilePlayer:RootDirectory` (`/mnt/nas/music` on the box) and estimates the duration ADR §4.1 says to pass through. One new overload; that is also what keeps `SoundFlowPlaybackService` out of the service's constructor and makes the DI guard buildable. ⚠ **`ADR §14 Q10` answered: the speech pre-flight is DROPPED**, synthesis is the only gate — on the deployed config an unsubstituted `${secret:` tag advertises Google as *available* anyway, so the pre-flight would miss the actual misconfiguration while blocking a fixed engine until restart. ⚠ **A `Speech` snapshot's position is always zero** (`TTSEventSource` never overrides `Position`) — pinned as-is, not faked, handed to PR 5. ⛔ **Do NOT use `POST /api/sources/events/{tts,file}` as a template** — all three defects still live at `d1226da`. **`AnnouncementService` is the template, and the one line not to copy is that it awaits playback inside the call.** ⛔ **No `mixer.AddSource`** — it does not route audio, and never removing it is where `SourcesController` leaks. ⛔ **Do not touch `DuckingServiceCharacterizationTests`** (PR 4's tripwire) or `INTEGRATIONS.md:566` (still true today; PR 4 makes it false). **Verification that can actually fail:** a `CustomWebApplicationFactory` test that turns `GvMedia:Enabled` on, points `BaseUrl` at `127.0.0.1:1`, and asserts the playback reaches `Failed`/`MediaTransport` through `GET /current` — route → validate → 202 → background fetch → auth handler → taxonomy → snapshot, end to end, with no network and no GV clock. It also closes PR 2's carried *"does `radio-api` still start"*. **`./data/gvmedia` writability is RE-CARRIED to PR 6**, not claimed: `GvMedia:Enabled` ships `false` and PR 3 does not flip it, so the first real fetch is still PR 6's. | ✅ [#556](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/556) | [`design/plans/PHN-1c-event-playback-service-and-route.md`](../design/plans/PHN-1c-event-playback-service-and-route.md) | [ADR-029](../design/decisions/2026-08-03-gv-audio-through-engine.md) D1/D2/D3 + §14 Q10 · [PHN arc PR breakdown](../design/plans/PHN-arc-pr-breakdown.md) PR 3 · [punch list §3.5 `PHN-1`, §2 `O6`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) · [`PHN-1a` plan](../design/plans/PHN-1a-event-playback-seam-contracts.md) §0.4/§5 and [`PHN-1b` plan](../design/plans/PHN-1b-gvmedia-client-cache-and-auth.md) §0.3/§0.4/§5 — **authority over the ADR wherever they disagree; this plan's §0.4 C-21…C-33 is authority over both wherever it contradicts them, and it does in three places** | **`PHN-1a`** ✅ [#528](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/528) and **`PHN-1b`** ✅ [#534](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/534) — both merged, so this row is claimable now. Blocks PR 4 (priority becomes load-bearing), PR 5 (server-owned state + the three stop conditions) and PR 6 (`PHN-2`, retire the `<audio>` element). `O6` still governs the arc. | `feat/phn-1c-event-playback-service` |
| TTS-9 | **Deprecate and remove eSpeak entirely.** Owner-approved 2026-09-03 **over** remediating `SEC-4`, because removal is comparable effort and closes three rows instead of guarding one.
