Deletes `GenerateESpeakAsync` (the `$"-v {voice} ..."` interpolation that is `SEC-4`), `GetESpeakVoicesAsync`, `IsESpeakAvailable`, the `TTSEngine.ESpeak` enum member, `TTSOptions.ESpeakPath`, and every ESpeak default. **Closes `SEC-4` by deleting the vulnerable path, closes `TTS-7` (nothing left to install), and removes the root of `TTS-3`.** `O11` is thereby **discharged, not deleted.** ⚠ Accepted trade-off: ESpeak was the only `IsOffline` engine, so there is no TTS at all without network. | ✅ **PR [#548](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/548)** | plan-as-handoff (owner brief) | [`SEC-4`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) · [`TTS-3`/`TTS-7`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) | — | `feat/tts-9-remove-espeak` |
| TEST-7 | **A `TimeProvider` seam for `NowPlayingPanel`'s two hardcoded debounce timers.** 📌 **Promoted from a note in § Documented fast-follows on that note's own instruction** (*"Planner: this is a row, not a note."*), 2026-09-03. Three tests in `tests/Radio.Web.Tests/Components/Shared/NowPlayingPanelVolumeDebounceTests.cs` — `VolumeDrag_PersistsThePreferenceOnce` (`:129`), `VolumeDrag_PersistsTheFinalValue` (`:165`) and `SeparatedVolumeChanges_EachPersist` (`:192`) — sequence `await Task.Delay(1500)` against `NowPlayingPanel`'s own **300 ms** `System.Threading.Timer` (`src/Radio.Web/Components/Shared/NowPlayingPanel.razor:1054-1078`) with **no rendezvous**. ⚠ **Be precise about which assertion is at risk.** `Assert.Equal(88d, pending)` is **safe** — `_pendingVolumePreference` is assigned synchronously inside `QueueVolumePreferenceSave` before the awaited call returns, so starvation cannot break it. It is **`Assert.Equal(1, CountConfigWrites(handler))`** that is unsafe, and it fails in **both** directions: **undershoot to 0** if the timer callback plus the *two* HTTP hops it makes (`ConfigurationApiService.cs:83-97` GETs before it POSTs) do not all drain inside 1500 ms, and **overshoot to 2** if a stall inserts >300 ms between the un-slept setup invokes. **Same shape as `TEST-4`, lower flake rate** — a 5x margin (1500/300) rather than 3x on a 20 ms poll — and the overshoot mode is the one `TEST-4` did not have. **This one needs production code to change, which is why it was not folded into `TEST-4`:** `VolumePreferenceDebounce` is `private static readonly` and the timer is a raw `System.Threading.Timer`, so `NowPlayingPanel` must take an injectable `TimeProvider` first. **The house idiom already exists in `Radio.Web`** (`EncoderHudService.cs:32,45-48`) and `FakeTimeProvider` is already used in this same test project (`EncoderHudServiceTests`, `SleepTests`). ⚠ **Advancing a fake clock is NOT sufficient on its own** — the callback is `async void` over two awaited HTTP hops, so the fix still needs a completion rendezvous before asserting on the write count. **Also pull in the panel's second hardcoded timer** (`_gainDebounceTimer`, 200 ms, `:891-909`) — same exposure, same seam. Sibling `VolumeDrag_StillAppliesEveryTickToTheAudioEngine` (`:150`) is genuinely safe and needs no change. | 📋 | — | [punch list §4.6 `TEST-7`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) | — | — |
| ENC-20 | **Three knob-feel defects the owner found with his own hand on the panel.** ⭐ **The first findings from real knob use, and the reason they exist is that `ENC-17` proves *mechanism* while *feel* is only reachable by turning the thing.** Shipped as one PR because they are one user-facing complaint ("the knobs don't feel right yet"). **(1) One detent moved volume 4 points; it now moves 1.** VOLUME ran `step_size 2` while the host applied `VolumeStepPercent 2`, so the device's *units* were multiplied into *points* twice over. Now `step_size 1` / `VolumeStepPercent 1`, tiers re-cut `(150ms x2), (80ms x4)`, `VolumeClamp` 6 -> 4. &lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;⚠ **And the safety argument's arithmetic was wrong, in the file whose entire job is to justify a safety decision.** `RotaryEncoderConfigDefaults` claimed *"a maximum slew of 6 points per 80 ms"* and *"silence to full takes at least 1.33 seconds"*. The real figures were **12 points** and **0.67 s** — and 0.33 s at the fastest rate tier 2 actually fires at, because **a tier threshold is a maximum interval, not the rate the user turns at**. The claim was not a typo: handoff §5.4's table computes points as `multiplier x 2`, correct only at `step_size 1`, and **`ENC-11` ([#509](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/509)) shipped `step: 2` while inheriting that justification**. Corrected in the code, the handoff (Rev 8), the punch list, `INTEGRATIONS.md` and the harness. &lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;**(2) Knobs did not wake the dimmed screen.** `idle-dimmer.js` undims on `pointerdown`/`keydown`/`wheel`/`pointermove`; encoder input is a **SignalR server push and never becomes a DOM event**, so a knob turn neither undimmed nor reset the idle timers. `MainLayout` now wakes on `EncoderHudService.StateChanged`. ⚠ **Not an `ENC-6` regression** — that row shipped the `/sleep` three-state model at the 30-minute boundary; this is the separate 5-minute dim. The input is **not consumed** (unlike ENC-6 Rule 1), and the two stay separate **by construction**: `Sleep.razor` is on `EmptyLayout`, so `MainLayout` is not mounted on `/sleep`. &lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;**(3) The change tile vanished too fast.** `HudHoldMs` 1500 -> 2500 — the tail after the hand stops, not the lifetime, since the timer re-arms on every detent. &lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;⭐ **`ENC-17`'s first real job, and it paid for itself:** the harness measured **4 points/detent and a 12-point clamp before, 1 and 4 after** — the gap this whole arc was trying to close, measured rather than reasoned about. It also caught that the harness's own `turn()` docstring claimed *"the designed config uses step_size 1"* while VOLUME ran 2, which is **why the harness had under-reported the real knob all along**. &lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;📋 **QUEUED 2026-09-03** — owner-reported; specified in the Builder dispatch rather than by a Planner plan. &lt;br&gt;&lt;br&gt;✅ **SHIPPED 2026-09-03** — [#555](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/555). Measured 4 points/detent → 1, clamp 12 points → 4, HUD dwell 2500 ms, and all four knobs wake the dimmed panel. | ✅ | _(no plan — owner-reported defects, specified in the dispatch)_ | [encoder handoff **Rev 8** §5.2 · §5.4 · §7.6 · §15](design-handoffs/HANDOFF-rotary-encoder-mapping.md) · [punch list `ENC-3`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) | **✅ met** — `ENC-11` [#509](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/509), `ENC-16`, `ENC-4` [#519](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/519) and `ENC-17` [#553](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/553) all merged | `fix/enc-20-knob-feel` |
| PHN-1d | **ADR-029 PR 4 — priority becomes load-bearing.** 🔴 **P0, `O6`, fourth of the seven-PR phone arc, and the one to review hardest.** `DuckingService.StartDuckingAsync` starts raising `DuckingStateChanged` for **every source that joins the ducking set**, not only the one that causes the fade; `EventPlaybackService` subscribes and **stops attended playback when a source starts at or above `GvMedia:PreemptAtPriority` (8)** — the key's first reader in its life. **This is the first load-bearing use of priority in this system**: `GetPriority` has been read in exactly one place, inside `GetActiveEventsByPriority`, which has had zero non-test callers since it was written. This PR gives it its first. **No `Radio.Web` change, no route change, no broadcast, no duration cap, no config key** — `PreemptAtPriority` already ships in `GvMediaOptions.cs` and in `src/Radio.API/appsettings.json`. ⚠ **FIVE THINGS THAT MAKE THIS SHARP, ALL AUDIBLE RATHER THAN TEST-VISIBLE.** (1) **`GetPriority` answers `DefaultEventPriority` (8) for any event source it has not been told about, and `StopDuckingAsync` deletes what it was told** — so a priority resolved one moment too late reads 8, and every *ending* announcement would stop the voicemail. The handler ignores `IsDucking:false` and reads the priority **synchronously on the raising thread**. (2) **The raise is synchronous and the handler runs inside `AnnouncementService`'s call**, mid-announcement, from `POST /api/notifications/announce` — and `StopAsync` takes a gate `EventPlaybackService` already holds whenever the raise came out of `TearDownAsync`. The stop is **dispatched**, exactly as `OnSourceCompleted` is, for exactly the same reason. (3) ⚠ **The worst outcome available in this PR: a teardown landing between `StartDuckingAsync` and `PlayAsync` produces audio with no `playbackId`** — no route, no chip and no later preemption can address it, so it plays to the end over the announcement that preempted it. The acquisition tail now runs **under `_gate`**, which `TearDownAsync` also holds, making the two mutually exclusive; and a cancelled acquisition **disposes what it acquired**, which PR 3 leaked. (4) **Self-preemption:** `EventPlaybackRequest.Priority` accepts 1-10 and the attended source's own start now raises — so an identity check by reference is what stops a playback posted at `Priority` 8 from stopping itself the instant it starts. (5) ⭐ **OWNER DECISION 2026-09-04 — the mirror direction is deferred, and deliberately.** D5 rule 2 is symmetric: pressing play while a ≥ 8 source is *already* sounding should not add a second voice either. Three options went to the owner; he rejected mixing, **rejected refusing** (*"press play, get an error, nothing happens"* is the punch list's tier (b) shape) and chose **queueing** — wait for the blocker, then play. **That lands in PR 5, not here**, on his own reasoning turned on the queue: a waiting state nobody can see is the same complaint for longer, and visibility is PR 5's broadcast plus PR 6's chip. Nothing reaches a user in between — `GvMedia:Enabled` is `false` until PR 6 — and what it falls back to is the mixing this system has always done. **PR 4 pins that behaviour as a characterization test** so PR 5's queue arrives as an edited assertion, exactly as `PHN-1a` did for this row. `ThrowIfAlreadyPreempted`, `PreemptedByPriority` and `EventPlaybackState.Waiting` are **not built here**. ⚠ **PR 5's row must be re-sized**: §5 carries the queue's five open questions, including the awkward one — the wake-up trigger is the `IsDucking:false` raise this PR ignores, and `StopDuckingAsync` raises it only when the ducking set *empties*, so a ≥ 8 source ending while a sub-8 one continues produces no wake. ⚠ **TWO tripwires, not one.** `DuckingServiceCharacterizationTests` is the one all three predecessor handoffs name; `DuckingServiceTests.StartDuckingAsync_MultipleEvents_DoesNotDuckAgain` asserts the same property in a file nobody was told to look at. **Both are updated, neither deleted.** ⚠ **CITE `design/INTEGRATIONS.md`'s ducking correction BY CONTENT, NEVER BY LINE NUMBER — the number has moved twice in two days, and the most recent *correction* was already wrong on the day it was written.** The claim this PR owns is the struck bullet under **§ How Audio Ducking Works**, beginning *"~~Higher priority announcements can interrupt lower priority ones~~ — **this is not true today.**"* Grep that sentence; do not trust a number. **The history, recorded so it stops being rediscovered:** three merged plans — [`PHN-1a`](../design/plans/PHN-1a-event-playback-seam-contracts.md), [`PHN-1b`](../design/plans/PHN-1b-gvmedia-client-cache-and-auth.md) and [`PHN-1c`](../design/plans/PHN-1c-event-playback-service-and-route.md) — all instruct the reader *"do not touch `INTEGRATIONS.md:566`"*. **`:566` has never held this claim and does not hold it today: it is a code fence inside the RotaryPhone hub runbook.** The claim was at `:932` before [#556](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/556); that PR's own banner in this file recorded its new position as `:980`, which is **also wrong** — `:980` is inside a Python request example. It is in fact at **`:997`** at `b77ffe12`, and it will move again the next time anything is inserted above it. **Three successive documents got this number wrong; that is a citation-format defect, not three typos**, which is why the number is dropped here rather than corrected a fourth time. **And this PR does not make the struck-through claim true as written.** Announcement-versus-announcement still **mixes** (ADR §6.2 rule 3 declines to fix it); what becomes true is exactly *"an event at ≥ 8 stops attended GV playback"*. Restoring the original sentence would replace a true statement with a false one. ⚠ **`PreemptAtPriority` is safe to LOWER and a trap to RAISE**, and only the lowering case is argued in the ADR: because `GetPriority` answers 8 for every source that never claimed a rank, a threshold of 9 or 10 silently exempts almost everything while leaving the feature apparently intact. A test pins the two shipped defaults against each other; the doc line covers the per-machine override a test cannot see. ⚠ **The live consequence, an acceptance rather than a discovery:** with `PhoneIntegration:Enabled` false, **a doorbell posted to `/api/notifications/announce` at its default priority 8 will stop a voicemail mid-play.** Intended (ADR §6.1). **Est. 2-3 d, unchanged** — C-46 removed work rather than adding it. **Determinism:** this PR adds no timer, so `TimeProvider` is the wrong seam; what is asynchronous is a `Task.Run`, and the plan adds an internal `PreemptionTail` so the **negative** assertion — *"a priority-5 source changed nothing"* — is a rendezvous rather than a sleep. ⚠ **Plan §0.3 is a re-check list, not decoration.** Nine of its twelve assertions were **verified against real `PHN-1c` code** at `56fef797` rather than against plan text — which is how the plan caught that PR 3 ships `PublishNonTerminal`, not the `Publish` its own plan showed, and that substituting one for the other would reintroduce the synchronous-`PlayAsync`-failure bug its remark describes. Three are still open because Builder was mid-file. **PR 3 was not merged when this was written** — it has since merged at `b77ffe12`, so its pre-merge review may already have moved some of it: Builder's first action is to re-grep the list against merged `main` and answer it in the PR description. | ✅ [#558](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/558) | [`design/plans/PHN-1d-ducking-priority-load-bearing.md`](../design/plans/PHN-1d-ducking-priority-load-bearing.md) | [ADR-029](../design/decisions/2026-08-03-gv-audio-through-engine.md) **D5** §6.1/§6.2/§6.3 · [PHN arc PR breakdown](../design/plans/PHN-arc-pr-breakdown.md) PR 4 · [punch list §3.5 `PHN-1`, §2 `O6`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) · [`PHN-1a`](../design/plans/PHN-1a-event-playback-seam-contracts.md) §0.4/§5, [`PHN-1b`](../design/plans/PHN-1b-gvmedia-client-cache-and-auth.md) §0.3/§0.4/§5 and [`PHN-1c`](../design/plans/PHN-1c-event-playback-service-and-route.md) §0.4/§5 — **authority over the ADR wherever they disagree; this plan's §0.4 C-34…C-46 is authority over all of them, and it is in five places — plus **C-46, the owner decision of 2026-09-04 — ✅ now FILED as [`D28`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) in punch list §7 (*Closed 2026-09-04 by the owner*), so this row no longer carries an unfiled action. | PHN-1e | **ADR-029 PR 5 — server-owned state and the stop conditions that need no client.** 🔴 **P0, `O6`, fifth of the phone arc.** `EventPlaybackService.PlaybackChanged` has been raised on every transition since PR 3 and subscribed by **nothing**; this row connects it to `/hubs/audio` as `EventPlaybackChanged`, caches it in `AudioStateStore`, and seeds that cache once from `GET /api/audio/events/current` so a browser arriving mid-playback is not told the room is silent (ADR §8.1 ⟨A1·4⟩). Plus **the max-duration cap** (D7 §7.1), **entering `/sleep` stops attended playback** (D7 §7.5, closing ADR §14 **Q8**), and **the last-circuit-closed `CircuitHandler`** — the first this application has ever had (D7 §7.3). **No user-visible change:** `GvMedia:Enabled` is `false` until PR 6, and the chip is PR 6's. **No config key** — `MaxPlaybackSeconds` already ships. ⚠ **THE QUEUE IS NOT IN THIS ROW — see `PHN-1f`, and read the plan's §0.2 before disputing it.** ⚠ **THREE THINGS THAT MAKE THIS SHARP.** (1) ⭐ **The cap is NOT `CancelAfter` on `Playback`'s token, and two merged plans say it is** (`PHN-1c` §5, `PHN-1d` §5). Nothing observes that token once acquisition returns — the last read is `source.PlayAsync(token)`, which starts playback and returns — so a cap built that way would expire silently at 300 s and change nothing: the worst available shape for something the ADR calls *"THE guarantee"*. It is a one-shot `TimeProvider` timer whose callback **dispatches** a stop (plan C-49). (2) ⭐ **SignalR does not use MVC's `JsonStringEnumConverter`.** `AddControllers().AddJsonOptions` configures the output formatter and nothing else, so handing the snapshot straight to `SendAsync` would put `"state": 1` on the hub while `GET /current` says `"state": "Playing"` — and ADR §8.1 feeds **both** into the same client field. The enums go on the wire as **strings**, which is also what lets a `Radio.Web` build that predates `PHN-1f`'s `Waiting` ignore it instead of failing to parse (plan C-47). (3) **The circuit backstop fires on the TRANSITION to zero, never on an observed zero**, and is registered **singleton** so one instance counts every circuit — a scoped registration gives each circuit its own counter reaching zero on its own close, which is precisely the owner-circuit rule ADR ⟨A1·4⟩ deleted (plan C-52). ⚠ **`AudioStateStore` "had never been constructed in its life" is STALE** — `ENC-12` itself added `MainLayout.razor:20`'s injection, so nothing needs bringing to life (plan C-48). ⚠ **Three things no unit test in this repo can prove** and which the plan's §2.2 settles on the box: that the payload survives the real `JsonHubProtocol`, that the backstop's latency is what ADR §7.3 assumes, and that a **browser refresh does not stop playback**. ⛔ **IT DOES — measured on the box 2026-09-04, twice: reloading the only browser goes 1 → 0 → 1 and stops the playback immediately.** U3 resolved AGAINST the design; see § *Blocker — `PHN-1e`*. The "four minutes late" in the previous wording was a third retention figure, neither the framework's 3 nor this app's configured 10, and the prediction was inverted. ⭐ **RESOLVED — `D30` says the stop is WANTED, so the handler shipped as built and the correction landed on §7.3's reasoning; and the review found a SECOND, independent gap — §7.5's rule never fired on the idle timer, fixed with the `SetSleepScreenVisibleAsync(true)` edge (ADR-029 §16.5).** See § *Resolved — `PHN-1e`'s blocker*. | PHN-1f | **ADR-029 PR 5b — the mirror-case queue (`D28`), and the ducking-args change that makes its wake correct.** 🔴 **P0, `O6`, new row: the arc becomes eight PRs.** ✅ **THE 🔒 CLEARED BECAUSE THE PLAN LANDED — that was the row's only lock, and its own flip condition.** This row read *"NOT CLAIMABLE WHEN `PHN-1e` MERGES — THIS ROW HAS NO PLAN"* and *"Flip 🔒 → 📋 only when a plan file exists and this cell links it."* [#563](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/563) wrote that plan on 2026-09-04, the Plan cell now links it, so the condition is met and the row went straight to 🚧. `PHN-1e`'s plan §5 was the **design input**, never the plan — and the planning pass re-derived all five of its settled questions against merged code, where **one did not survive** (C-57, below). Owner decision **`D28`**: pressing play while a source at or above `GvMedia:PreemptAtPriority` is already talking must **wait and then play** — not mix (today), not refuse (rejected as *"press play, get an error, nothing happens"*). ⭐ **The design is `PHN-1e`'s plan §5, complete, with all five of `PHN-1d` §5's open questions settled** — three leans confirmed, one overturned, one dissolved. **The shape:** a waiting playback **IS** the current playback in a new state; there is no pending slot, so replace semantics, `StopAsync`, `Current`, the re-attach path and the controller's 404/409 rule are all inherited rather than built. Audio is acquired **first** and the wait happens after, so the room's silence is not followed by a fetch — and an acquisition failure surfaces at once rather than after thirty seconds of Waiting. ⚠ **THE OVERTURNED LEAN, AND IT IS THE BULK OF THE ROW.** `PHN-1d` §5 leaned to accepting a known starvation case: `StopDuckingAsync` raises **only when the ducking set empties**, so a ≥ 8 source ending while a sub-8 source continues produces **no wake**, and the playback expires as `Failed`/`WaitExpired` — which is `D28`'s rejected option delivered thirty seconds late. **Rejected. This row adds `DuckingSourceTransition` and `TriggeringSourcePriority` to `DuckingStateChangedEventArgs`** (a `Radio.Core` change), makes `DuckingService` raise for **every** source that leaves with the true aggregate in `IsDucking`, and updates `AudioManager` to branch on the transition. ⭐ **That closes BOTH residuals `PHN-1d` handed forward as a by-product**: the fade-window race where a synchronous `GetPriority` still answers 8, and the `ActiveEventCount` guard's acknowledged hole — the guard is **deleted**, not supplemented. ⚠ **HIGH RISK, `PHN-1d`'s review posture.** Two hazards make the naive versions wrong: raising `IsDucking:false` while others remain makes `AudioManager` restore the radio to **full volume mid-announcement**, and raising `IsDucking:true` for a stop hands the handler a source whose priority entry is already deleted, turning a **stop into a preemption**. Both are why the transition field exists. **Third rewrite of the same two ducking tripwire files in three PRs** (`DuckingServiceTests`, `DuckingServiceCharacterizationTests`). ⛔ **`EventPlaybackState.Waiting` goes at the END of the enum**, and `APlaybackStartedUnderAHigherPrioritySourceStillMixes_TODAY` is **updated, never deleted**. One new key, `GvMedia:MaxQueuedWaitSeconds` (30) — ⚠ read `PHN-1b` **C-14** first. ⚠ **TWO THINGS THE PLANNING PASS FOUND THAT `PHN-1e` §5 DID NOT, both in plan §0.4.** **C-56:** `SleepService`'s stop is an **allow-list** (`SleepService.cs:356-358`) while `EventPlaybackSnapshotDto.IsLive` is a **deny-list** (`ApiModels.cs:1522`), so adding `Waiting` silently removes it from ADR §7.5's `/sleep` rule while adding it to the circuit backstop for free — a queued playback would then start audio up to 30 s after the panel goes dark, on `EmptyLayout`, with no stop control anywhere on screen. **C-57:** the wait adds two throwing exits between acquisition and `TryAdopt`, and neither `TearDownAsync` nor `FailAsync` can release an *unadopted* source — so without a `DisposeOrphanAsync` guard the `RemoteMedia` arm leaks an open `FileStream` over the cached recording. ⭐ **And one thing that got SMALLER:** `Waiting` needs **no `Radio.Web` change and no broadcast change at all** (C-64) — `.ToString()` on the hub plus `IsLive`'s deny-list carry it, which is C-47's payoff arriving. | PHN-2 | **ADR-029 PR 6 — retire the `<audio>` element (Feature A).** 🔴 **P0, `O6`, sixth of the eight-PR phone arc, the LAST OPEN P0 on the GA punch list, and the first PR of this arc a user can hear.** `VoicemailPlayer.razor:8` is an HTML5 `<audio>` pointed straight at the GV bridge, so the **browser** fetches and decodes voicemail — bypassing mute, master volume, balance, ducking and Cast routing. Owner decision **`D17`** establishes this is **live behaviour today, not a latent risk**: press play while the radio is on and two sounds run in the room at full level each. This row spends the seam PRs 1–5b built and shipped dark: the element, `wwwroot/js/voicemail-player.js`'s player half and `GvBridgeApiService.GetVoicemailAudioUrl` go **together**; the transport becomes a view of the server snapshot driven through `POST /api/audio/events`; a **console-playback chip** lands in `.topbar-primary` (handoff §Cross-3) as the global stop control that earns *"playback survives navigation"*; **`GvMedia:Enabled` flips `true`** in `src/Radio.API/appsettings.json`, which the deploy overwrites and therefore reaches every box. Also renders `PHN-1f`'s `Waiting` state — **`D28`'s queue is live on the appliance today and nothing shows it** — and adds the `TTSEventSource.Position` override `PHN-1e` §5.2 assigned here. ⭐ **THE OWNER UAT SCRIPT IS [`design/plans/PHN-2-retire-the-audio-element.md` §3](../design/plans/PHN-2-retire-the-audio-element.md#3-owner-uat--feature-a) — a fifteen-minute script written to be read on its own, at the cabinet, by someone who has not read the plan.** Part 0 = two failure modes mistakable for defects (the GV blackout probe; **Cast making the cabinet go quiet is a PASS**); Part 1 = six pre-flight steps incl. the `InterServiceAuthKey` check; Part 2 = U1–U4, the four checks that settle Feature A (ducking, mute, master volume, Cast); Part 3 = four optional; Part 4 = what to capture on a fail. ⚠ **PRE-FLIGHT BEFORE FLIPPING `GvMedia:Enabled` ON THE BOX, and it is a numbered step, not a footnote:** check whether RotaryPhone's `InterServiceAuthKey` is set **on the appliance** — the repo cannot tell you, `GVBridgeConfig.cs:81-82` instructs storing it outside source. Its gate ships default-off, but if it has been set, **every** fetch returns 401 → `MediaUnauthorized` until `GvMedia:AuthKey` matches, across **two** `appsettings.Production.json` files the deploy does not re-seed. Runbook: `design/INTEGRATIONS.md:961-973`. ⚠ **THIS ROW CARRIES THE PHONE ARC'S ENTIRE VERIFICATION DEBT — fourteen deferred items, inventoried in plan §2.2** and labelled `SOUND` (a human confirmed the room changed) or `PATH` (the code path ran), because `AudioFileEventSource.PlayCoreAsync:175-184` selects a **silent simulation** (`PlaybackLoopAsync:261-271`) when no playback service is present and reports a clean completion having produced no audio, at **Debug**, which since `LOG-11` never reaches the journal. **A green "it completed" is the least trustworthy evidence available here.** ⚠ **A `MediaNotFound` during UAT is as likely to be the GV auth blackout as a bad id** — six distinct transient conditions reach RotaryPhone's 404 because `FindNodeAsync` never checks its list's `Succeeded` flag. | UI-6 | **`AudioStateStore` notifies N subscribers and awaits one.** 🟡 **P2 — and PR 6 is NOT the deadline; see § *`UI-6` — the tiering argument, and the two counts the deferral note got wrong* below.** `AudioStateStore.NotifyAsync` (`src/Radio.Web/Services/AudioStateStore.cs:406-419`) does `await handler.Invoke()` on a multicast `Func<Task>`. `Delegate.Invoke` runs every handler but **returns only the last one's Task**, so every earlier subscriber runs to its first `await` and its continuation is never observed — the `try`/`catch` protects exactly one of N, and the other N−1 exceptions reach no log at all. **Two more sites hand-roll the identical defect and are NOT fixed by fixing `NotifyAsync`:** `OnHubRadioStateChanged` (`:223-237`) and `OnHubSleepStateChanged` (`:239-245`) — and the second has **no `try`/`catch` at all**. ⭐ **A second, sharper half the deferral note did not name:** a subscriber that throws **synchronously** — before its first `await` — propagates straight out of `Invoke`, so **every handler registered after it never runs.** That is starvation, not just a lost log line, and `DuckingService`'s own raise guard (`DuckingService.cs:481-483`) documents the same shape as a known, accepted limitation for two subscribers. **Fix:** iterate `GetInvocationList()`, await each, catch per subscriber; apply the same shape to the two hand-rolled sites. **Est. 0.5 d.** ⚠ **The `UI-6` ID was assigned by the Builder** on the plan's own `UI-` suggestion — plan §6.2 left it *"for the owner to assign"*; rename freely. | 📋 | *to be written — small enough for a plan-in-the-row if the owner prefers* | [`PHN-1f` plan §6.2](../design/plans/PHN-1f-the-wait-then-play-queue.md), and § *What the two reviewers found* below | — | `fix/audio-state-store-multicast-notify` |
| OPS-7 | **The deploy's Production-config seed can silently destroy `RotaryPhone:Gv:AuthKey`.** 🟡 **P2 — dormant on `radio`, live on the next box provisioned.** `Deploy-ToLinux.ps1:222` guards the seed on **`api/`**'s file only (`test -f $TargetPath/api/appsettings.Production.json`), while `:226` copies the seed into **both** `api/` and `web/`. A box whose api overlay is absent while its web overlay is present therefore has the web file overwritten with the api seed — and the web file is where `RotaryPhone:Gv:AuthKey` lives, which is the key `PHN-2` made load-bearing. **Fix:** guard each destination independently, or seed only the directory whose file is missing. ⚠ **Found by `PHN-2`'s planning pass and deliberately NOT folded into it** — different mechanism (PowerShell deploy sequencing, not a Blazor surface), different verification (a second box or a deliberately-removed file, not a listening test), and a change that can silently destroy a secret does not belong behind a UAT about whether a voicemail ducks the radio. Filed by `PHN-2`'s Builder cycle. **Est. 0.5 d.** ⭐ **A SECOND DEFECT IN THE SAME FOURTEEN LINES, found by this planning pass and folded in** (`C-88`): the `if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { "Remote file move failed!" }` at `:230` is positioned to guard the remote file move at `:217`, and **cannot** — `:222`'s `ssh … test -f` overwrites `$LASTEXITCODE` first, and since **both** `$configDir` values ship an `appsettings.Production.json`, `:221`'s `Test-Path` is always true, so `:222` always runs. A genuine failure of the file move is **always** masked, and the message names the wrong step. ⚠ **The verification is the reason this got its own plan file rather than a plan-in-the-row: the obvious test — run the deploy and look — is the one that can destroy the secret.** The plan's §3 uses the script's own `-TargetPath` parameter to run against a scratch install path with a sentinel file, and **runs the pre-fix mutation first** so the defect is observed rather than read. ⚠ **`-TargetPath` does NOT isolate everything** — the WirePlumber block at `:299-318` is parameterised by neither `-TargetPath` nor `-NoRestart`, and can restart WirePlumber, cycling BT/audio on the box. Plan §3.2. **Swept for the same shape elsewhere: one instance.** `Sync-WpRule` (`:268-297`) already guards per destination and is the exemplar the fix copies; the rsync at `:216-217` excludes the overlay from **both** directories and is correct; `setup.sh` has no seed; `Deploy-ToPi.ps1` is a pure wrapper that inherits the bug and needs no separate fix. | 📋 | [`OPS-7-production-config-seed-guard.md`](../design/plans/OPS-7-production-config-seed-guard.md) | [`PHN-2` plan §0.4 C-81](../design/plans/PHN-2-retire-the-audio-element.md) | — | `fix/deploy-production-config-seed-guard` |
| TTS-11 | **The console keeps a plaintext copy of everything it says out loud.** 🟠 **P1 — punch list §4.4 (`docs/HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md:1076`).** Since `LOG-11` the API's Information lines go to the **file sink**, and `src/Radio.API/appsettings.json:17-18` sets `"Override": { "Radio": "Information" }` — so an SMS body the console reads aloud is left **at rest on an appliance in a family home**, and `GET /api/system/logs` renders it in Settings → Logs with `Info` one dropdown away. ⭐ **THE ROW WAS FILED FOR THREE PATHS. THERE ARE ELEVEN, AND THE TWO WORST WERE NOT AMONG THE THREE.** `AnnouncementService.cs:40` logs the message **untruncated** — every other site clips to 47 or 50 characters, this one does not, and it is the shared entry point for `NotificationsController` and phone-call announcements. And `SoundFlowMasterMixer.cs:101-103` logs `source.Name` from **generic mixer code that has no idea it is holding speech** — `TTSEventSource` is the only `IAudioSource` whose `Name` embeds user text (`$"TTS: {first 47 chars}"`). That reframes the defect: it is not three lines that each chose to log text, it is **one property — a display name that is really user content — plus a habit of logging payloads**, so any code that logs an audio source's name is a leak, present or future. Full inventory in plan §0.2. ⚠ **Two line numbers in the punch-list row are stale and are corrected in the plan, not in the punch list** (`C-82`, `C-84`): the ducking argument is at **`AudioManager.cs:515`**, not `:501` — `:499`/`:501` are comment prose after `PHN-1f`/`PHN-2` edited the method — and the whole-string line is **`TTSEventSource.cs:109`**, not `:92`. The row hedged correctly by quoting the statement, which is what let both be re-found. **A second `AudioManager` site at `:523-525` (`C-83`) and a second mixer site at `:117-119` were never filed at all.** ⭐ **Decision — what replaces the content:** a hash prefix **plus** a length, `txt:9f2ab41c/142`, in the shape `GvMediaCache.MaskFor` already establishes; and for the four `.Name` sites, **drop the name and log `Type` + `Id` instead** (the mixer already logs `Id`, so `Name` is redundant there today). **This makes diagnosis better, not worse:** today three sites truncate the same utterance at 50, 47 and not-at-all, so an operator cannot tell they are one event — a stable token makes the chain traceable for the first time, and `Id` joins a ducking line to its mixer line. ⚠ **The helper's doc must say it is a correlation token, NOT a confidentiality boundary** — announcement text has a small candidate space and a short utterance's hash is recoverable by enumeration. **Est. 1.5 d, not the 0.5 h on the punch list** — that estimate was honest for three lines. ⚠ **`NeitherTheTextNorTheRawMediaIdReachesAnyLogLineThisSeamWrites` MUST NOT be widened** — `TTSFactory` news up its own `HttpClient`, so a real factory still cannot run offline and the harness still cannot observe the wider property. Plan §4.6. | 📋 | [`TTS-11-no-user-text-at-rest-in-the-log.md`](../design/plans/TTS-11-no-user-text-at-rest-in-the-log.md) | punch list [§4.4](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) · `design/FUTURE-WORK.md` § *TTS seam* item 5 | — | `fix/tts-11-no-user-text-in-logs` |

## ✅ Resolved — `PHN-1e`'s blocker, and what each half cost

**Filed 🚫 2026-09-04 (Builder). Cleared 2026-09-04 (Builder) — the row shipped as
[#561](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/561).** ⭐ **Kept in full rather than deleted, and
kept because the two halves resolved in OPPOSITE directions — one against the design, one against the
code — and a reader who sees only "resolved" would learn nothing from either.**

| | What was blocked | How it resolved | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| **B1** | A browser refresh stops attended playback (ADR-029 §7.3) | ⭐ **Owner decision `D30`: that is the WANTED behaviour.** The code was right; §7.3's *reasoning* was wrong | **Zero code.** The `CircuitHandler` shipped byte-for-byte as built |
| **B2** | §7.5's `/sleep` rule never fires on the idle timer, the case it was written for | **A second edge**, on the `ENC-6` seam `SetSleepScreenVisibleAsync(true)` | One method, one interface signature, 11 tests |

**The instrument that found both is the same one, and it is worth naming.** B1 came from an on-box
check the plan declared *unverifiable in a test host* and attached *"stop and re-plan"* to. B2 came
from the **comment-accuracy reviewer**, which is the second brief this cycle runs because Copilot is
out of quota. Neither is a thing a green test suite can produce.

### B1 — a browser refresh stops attended playback. ⭐ Resolved by owner decision `D30`: it is supposed to.

**What was measured, on the box, twice, at `dd8e85ec`:**

```
18:21:54.342 [INF] Last circuit closed with attended playback evp-23da… still live; stopping it (ADR-029 §7.3)
18:21:54.701 [DBG] Circuit opened; 1 live
```

`GET /api/audio/events/current` went `Playing` → `Stopped` **seven seconds into a forty-nine second
recording**, because the kiosk was reloaded. Deterministic, not a race: with two tabs the sequence is
**`2 → 1 → 2`** — the close arrives *first* — so with a single browser it is **`1 → 0 → 1`**.

**Why the plan's U3 was wrong.** It predicted `1 → 2 → 1`, the replacement circuit opening before the
old one closed. Inverted. ⚠ **And note that U3's *prediction* being wrong is not the point** — the
value was in declaring the ordering unverifiable and naming the observation that would settle it. A
plan that had asserted the same ordering with more confidence would have produced identical code and
no discovery.

**Why it is immediate rather than late.** `DisconnectedCircuitRetentionPeriod` (10 minutes here)
governs **unexpected** disconnects. A reload, a navigation or a tab close is a **graceful**
termination and Blazor disposes the circuit at once. The retention window never enters the refresh
path — so `OnCircuitClosedAsync` is **bimodal, immediate or ten minutes, with no indication of which**.

**⭐ THE RESOLUTION, AND IT WENT THE OTHER WAY FROM EVERY OPTION THIS SECTION ORIGINALLY LISTED.**
Owner decision **`D30`**, 2026-09-04: *"If the page reloads mid-voicemail, the audio should fail. If
the user wants to hear it they can replay."* So:

- **`AttendedPlaybackCircuitHandler` ships EXACTLY as built.** No grace period, no circuit-identity
  matching, no survival mechanism — ⛔ **and none to be proposed as a future row.** All three options
  this section originally offered the Planner (drop §7.3; a grace period; distinguish graceful from
  dropped) are **withdrawn**, refused on the decision rather than on their merits.
- **What fell was the ADR's prose, not the branch's behaviour.** §7.3's worked example, its
  retention-window claim, and its self-description as *"the weakest of the three defences"* are all
  struck — under `D30` the rule is **promoted** to the mechanism that implements the decision in this
  box's resting configuration, with the 300 s cap as the backstop behind **it**.
- ⚠ **One divergence recorded rather than decided:** with a second browser open a reload is
  `2 → 1 → 2` and stops nothing, so the rule implements `D30` literally only in the single-browser
  case. The only mechanism that would close it is the ownership model ⟨A1·4⟩ deleted; the divergence
  is benign, because a client still holds the transport.

**⚠ The claim this section made that did NOT survive.** It said *"as built, every reachable firing of
this backstop is a false positive."* That was correct **against §7.3's stated goal** and is **false
against `D30`'s** — the identical firing is now a true positive. It is preserved because the way it
stopped being true is the whole content of the amendment: nothing measured changed, only the
objective.

**Record:** ADR-029 §16 (Amendment 2), `design/DECISION-LOG.md`, and the `D30` register entry in
[`docs/HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md`](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md) §7 — which the ADR deliberately left for
this cycle to file, and which this cycle filed.

### B2 — ADR §7.5's `/sleep` rule did not cover the idle timer, which is the case it was written for. ✅ Fixed in this row.

Found by the comment-accuracy reviewer and confirmed. §7.5's premise is *"the console can put itself
into a layout with no stop control, **on an idle timer**, while a voicemail is playing."* The rule
shipped in `SleepService.EnterSleepAsync`, and **the idle path never reaches it**: `idle-dimmer.js`'s
`navigateToSleep('idle')` only sets `window.location.href = '/sleep'`, carrying its own comment that it
deliberately does not call `SetSleepAsync` because idle navigation must not pause playback. So
`IsSleeping` is **false** on that path and the obvious predicate is the wrong one.

**Fixed with a second edge, per ADR-029 §16.5 — and the two are not redundant:**

| Edge | Expresses | Is the ONLY stopper for |
|---|---|---|
| `SleepService.SetSleepScreenVisibleAsync(true)` | the **no-transport surface** went up | the **30-minute idle timer** and a **direct navigation** — the routes that reach `/sleep` without parking audio |
| `SleepService.EnterSleepAsync` | the **room is being parked** | the Sleep pill, the server push, and the entries with **no browser at all** — `POST /api/system/sleep`, the encoder long-press |

⚠ **Read the second column carefully, because a first draft of this row got it wrong** and the
adversarial review caught it. The screen edge does not *stop anything* on the pill or the server
push: both park audio first, so by the time `Sleep.razor` reports itself there is nothing left to
stop. Every route is covered; which edge covers it is what matters when one of them changes.
⚠ **ADR-029 §16.5's own table says this edge "covers rows 1, 2, 3, 5"**, which is true of *producing
the fact* and false of *stopping the playback*. Worth an Architect's eye on the next pass — this row
did not amend the merged ADR.

⭐ **AND THE PREDICATE IS THE REPORT, NOT A TRANSITION OF `ConsoleWakeState` — a pre-merge
correction, and the sharpest finding of the cycle.** The rule was first written as *"stop when
`WakeState` leaves `Awake`"*, with a comment arguing it *"cannot suppress a stop"*. It can:
`Sleep.razor`'s dispose report is best-effort behind a 2 s timeout and `MainLayout`'s corrective
`false` is fire-and-forget with the failure swallowed, so **losing both leaves the flag reading
`true` while the console is genuinely awake on Home** — the tree documents both halves in comments.
A voicemail then starts, the idle timer lands on `/sleep` thirty minutes later, the page reports
`true`, nothing *changed*, and a transition rule **does not fire**. That is §7.5's own failure,
produced by the rule written to prevent it. A second, independent loss: with no lock, the "after"
re-read could observe a *concurrent* report's write, which a kiosk reload while on `/sleep` produces
naturally. Deciding from the report's own argument closes both by construction.
⚠ **"Never polled" is untouched** — still driven by a client's report, no loop, no timer, and
`WakeState` is now not consulted at all, which §16.5 also asks for. Idempotence moved somewhere
stronger: the stop decides on the playback's **state**, so a repeat report finds a terminal snapshot
and does nothing — and that holds across *different* clients, which an edge on one global flag
cannot see.

⚠ **`ISleepService.SetSleepScreenVisible` became `SetSleepScreenVisibleAsync` and the stop is
AWAITED.** §16.5 left that choice open and argued for awaiting on **C-49**'s own lesson — this arc has
already paid once for a stop that nothing observed. `TheReportDoesNotAnswerUntilTheStopHasCompleted`
reds under `_ = StopAttendedPlaybackAsync()`.

⚠ **How B2 survived design review, which is the transferable half.** The false claim propagated
through **four** documents — ADR §7.5 → plan constraint **C-51** → the branch's comment in
`EnterSleepAsync` → the PR body — each restating the one before it. **Not one of them checked
`idle-dimmer.js` against the claim**, and that file contradicts it in a comment written for exactly
this reason.

### What the two reviewers found, and the one thing deliberately NOT fixed

Copilot is out of quota, so two adversarial reviewers ran with different briefs — **lifecycle,
concurrency and the wire contract**, and **comment-and-test accuracy**. Third cycle running that this
pairing has earned its cost.

**Fixed in this row** — one behavioural defect in the day's own change, three latent hazards, four
tests that could not fail, and seven comments that asserted more than the code did. The defect and the
hang are the two worth remembering:

- ⭐ **The sleep edge could be SUPPRESSED by a stale flag** — see the predicate note above. Found by
  the comment reviewer, attacking the sentence *"it cannot suppress a stop"* rather than the code.
  ⚠ **The comment was written the same day, by the Builder, in the fix for the previous cycle's
  finding.** That is the shape `CLAUDE.md` § *Pre-Merge Review* names: a comment offering a reason a
  thing is safe, where the reason is the claim to check.
- ⚠ **A wedged stop made the console UNWAKEABLE.** `EnterSleepAsync` calls the attended-playback stop
  while holding `_lock`, and `WakeAsync` takes the same `_lock` — so an unbounded
  `IEventPlaybackService.StopAsync` parks the encoder, the screen tap and the REST wake behind it at
  once, and this repo has a documented class of hang in exactly that layer. Time-boxed at 3 s.
  Falsified by removing the bound: **the test hangs rather than failing.**
- The cap's timer callback was a **process kill** (unguarded `TimerCallback`; `ILogger` rethrows
  provider exceptions), `OnCircuitOpenedAsync` could **strand the circuit count** so `D30`'s rule
  silently never fires again, and `MaxPlaybackSeconds` had **no upper clamp** — over ~49.7 days
  `CreateTimer` throws *after* `PlayAsync`, failing every attended playback under a generic reason.

⛔ **DEFERRED, deliberately, and it is a real defect: `AudioStateStore.NotifyAsync` awaits only the
LAST subscriber of a multicast `Func<Task>`.** `await handler.Invoke()` on a multicast delegate returns
one task, so earlier subscribers run to their first `await` and their continuations are never observed
— the `try`/`catch` around it protects exactly one of N. The store is a **singleton** and every
circuit's components subscribe, so N > 1 is the normal multi-client state.
**Why deferred rather than fixed here:** it is **pre-existing and shared by all eleven events on that
store**; `EventPlaybackChanged` merely inherits it. Fixing it correctly means iterating
`GetInvocationList()` and awaiting each, which changes the failure semantics of ten events this row
does not own — the kind of unrelated blast radius the `PHN-1e`/`PHN-1f` split exists to refuse.
⚠ **It is worth a Planner row, and PR 6 is the deadline that matters**, because PR 6's stop chip is the
first consumer that will hang off `EventPlaybackChanged` on every circuit at once.

### `UI-6` — the tiering argument, and the two counts the deferral note got wrong

✅ **FILED 2026-09-04 (Builder, `PHN-1f` cycle) as queue row `UI-6` and punch-list §5 `UI-6`.** The deferral note
immediately above is what proposed it. Its *defect* description is right and is carried forward unchanged; its
**tiering and its deadline are not**, and `PHN-1f`'s planning pass corrected both against merged code at `4ec0fb85`.
Recorded here rather than by editing the note, because the note is the record of what the `PHN-1e` cycle believed.

**1. "Shared by all eleven events" overstates the reach: nine of the eleven have ZERO production subscribers.**
`AudioStateStore` is injected in exactly **one** place in the whole tree — `MainLayout.razor:20` — and subscribes
exactly **two** of its events: `EncoderConfigStatusChanged` (`:402`) and `EncoderConnectionChanged` (`:403`). Its only
other consumer, `AttendedPlaybackCircuitHandler`, subscribes to nothing; it reads `EventPlayback` directly.
⚠ **The count that looks alarming comes from `AudioStateHubService`, a different type** — most `@inject`ed fields
named `AudioState` in this repo (e.g. `Sleep.razor:13`) are the **hub service**, not the store. That naming collision is
almost certainly what produced the "all eleven" reading.

**2. PR 6 is NOT the deadline.** `EventPlaybackChanged` has **zero** subscribers today, and PR 6 takes it to **one**.
A multicast defect needs **N ≥ 2** to bite: with one subscriber, `Invoke` returns that subscriber's own Task, it is
awaited, and its exceptions are caught — **no defect at all.** What crosses the threshold is a **second circuit**: the
store is a singleton and `MainLayout` is per-circuit, so two open browsers already give `EncoderConnectionChanged` two
subscribers **today** — the box's documented second-browser case (ADR-029 §16.3 P4), not a hypothetical.

**3. P2 on punch list §1's own criteria**, which tier by consequence in the cabinet:

- **(a)** wrong or dangerous on day one — no. **(d)** substrate for verification — no. **(e)** permanent at install — no.
- **(c)** unrecoverable without a laptop — **no.** `ThrowUnobservedTaskExceptions` is **not set anywhere in the tree**, so
  on .NET 10 an unobserved task exception is swallowed at finalization. There is no process-crash path and nothing wedges.
- **(b)** *"the machine reports success and does nothing; a control does nothing"* — **not met by the async half.** Every
  subscriber still runs synchronously up to its first `await`, and both live handlers reach `await InvokeAsync(…)` there,
  so the re-render **is** dispatched on every circuit regardless. What is lost is a log line on a failure, plus the fact
  that `NotifyAsync` returns early. **The synchronous-throw half COULD meet (b)** — one bad subscriber starves the rest —
  but neither shipped handler can throw synchronously: both open with a null-guarded property read
  (`MainLayout.razor:1329`, `:1345`) before their first await.

⚠ **What would move it to P1, stated so the tier can be re-argued rather than re-guessed:** a store subscriber whose
handler either (i) can throw **before** its first `await`, or (ii) does work after an await that the *caller* of
`UpdateX`/`NotifyAsync` depends on having finished. Neither exists today, and **PR 6's chip is very unlikely to be
either** — a chip handler is `await InvokeAsync(StateHasChanged)`, the same shape as the two that already exist.

**Ordering call: it can land AFTER PR 6, and it should not block it.** PR 6 does not change the reachability threshold,
and the fix touches the failure semantics of ten events PR 6 does not own — exactly the unrelated blast radius the
`PHN-1e`/`PHN-1f` split exists to refuse. ⚠ **But it should land before whatever adds a SECOND subscriber to any one
store event**, and PR 6 is the row most likely to be followed by one. **Queued now at P2, unblocked; clear it in any
cycle with slack** — including before PR 6 if one appears, since it is half a day and touches no file this arc needs.

### What was never blocked, and is verified on the box

All at `dd8e85ec`, on the appliance. Recorded so nobody re-runs them:

- **U1 SETTLED — the payload survives the real `JsonHubProtocol`.** Two `Received EventPlaybackChanged event` lines in the Web log, emitted *inside* the typed `On<EventPlaybackSnapshotDto?>` handler, so deserialization succeeded; zero errors. `GET /current` returned `"state":"Playing"` as a string on the same playback. **C-47 is correct and this is the half that makes `PHN-1f`'s new state deployable without a lockstep Web build.**
- **U2 SETTLED — a singleton `CircuitHandler` receives every circuit's callbacks.** Count went `1 → 2` across two independent circuits. No fallback to a scoped registration is needed; the plan's Task 7 shape is right.
- **The max-duration cap works on real hardware.** Cap temporarily set to 20 s against a 42 s recording: `Playing` at t+17 s, `Stopped` by t+27 s, with `[WRN] … reached GvMedia:MaxPlaybackSeconds (20s)` in journald. Config restored afterwards. ⭐ **And it is now provably load-bearing in a test host too**, which matters because Amendment 2's P6 reasoning depends on the cap firing: removing `ArmDurationCap` reds 2 tests, and keeping the timer while dropping its dispatched `StopAsync` reds the same 2 — so the tests pin the **stop**, not merely the timer.
- **The `/sleep` rule works for the path that reaches it.** `Playing` → `POST /api/system/sleep` → `Stopped`, and waking does **not** resume — C-51's surviving half.
- **U3 is not on this list, and that is the point of the list.** It is the one that resolved against the design; see B1.

### On-box UAT of the new edge, 2026-09-04 — including the latency ADR §16.5 asked for

Deployed to `radio` and driven through the real controller, real DI and real JSON; both services
SHA-verified, kiosk relaunched and live each time.

⚠ **RUN TWICE, AND THE SECOND RUN IS THE ONE THAT COUNTS.** T1–T4 were first measured at `947fb2e`,
**which carried the transition predicate the pre-merge review then replaced.** Publishing those
numbers for the shipped build would have been the "measured several commits back" mistake this queue
has had to retract before — so T1 and T3 were re-run at `01dec7f`, and **T5 exists only at `01dec7f`,
because it is the case the change was for.**

| # | What was driven | Result |
|---|---|---|
| **T1** | `POST /api/system/sleep-screen {visible:true}` with a live playback, **no browser and no `EnterSleepAsync`** — re-run at `01dec7f` | `Playing` → `Stopped`. `isSleeping` stayed **false** throughout — the idle-path state, where the old rule did nothing. The API log carries `Sleep stopped attended playback evp-52d6…` with **no preceding `Entering sleep mode`**, which is what isolates the new edge from the old one |
| **T2** | The idle timer's own mechanism — `window.location.href = '/sleep'` driven over kiosk CDP — with a live playback | Stopped **592 ms** after the navigation. ⚠ **The CIRCUIT rule won on this path**, logging `Last circuit closed with attended playback … still live`. That is ADR §16.1's predicted overlap, which it marked *"derived from the mechanism, not measured"* — **now measured.** The two rules are not redundant; they overlap on exactly this path |
| **T3** | `{visible:false}` (what `MainLayout` reports on every navigation home) with a live playback — re-run at `01dec7f` | Still `Playing`. The inverse holds |
| **T4** | ⭐ **The latency §16.5 item 2 called "plausibly seconds" and left UNMEASURED** — hard navigation → the sleep screen reporting itself | **112 ms / 113 ms / 140 ms** over three runs, verified with the page really at `/sleep` and Blazor connected. It is milliseconds, not seconds |
| **T5** | ⭐ **THE STALE-FLAG CASE, at `01dec7f`** — flag already `true`, a **new** playback starts, then a report that is **not a change** (what the idle timer sends when the flag was never cleared) | `Playing` → **`Stopped`**. ⛔ **Under the transition predicate this stops NOTHING** — it is the defect the comment reviewer found, and this is it reproduced and closed on real hardware rather than only in a unit test |

⭐ **T2 also settles a MEDIUM the lifecycle reviewer could not settle from the tree.** This row moves
`IEventPlaybackService` (and `GvMediaClient`, `AudioFileEventSourceFactory`, `ITTSFactory`,
`IDuckingService` with it) from lazy construction to **eager, at host start**, because
`AudioStateUpdateService` is a hosted service that now resolves it in its constructor — so a
construction failure changes from *"a 500 on the first voicemail"* to *"`radio-api` fails to start"*,
i.e. a restart loop on a headless appliance. `CustomWebApplicationFactory` strips every
`IHostedService`, so **no test in the suite covers it**. The deploy did: `radio-api` came up, answered
`/api/health/version` at the right SHA, and served every check above.

⚠ **T2 and T4 were not re-run at `01dec7f`, and that is defensible rather than an oversight:** T2's
result is that the **circuit** rule wins the hard-navigation path, and the circuit rule is byte-for-byte
unchanged by the review; T4 measures how fast `Sleep.razor` reports itself, which is a Blazor render
latency the predicate does not touch. Said here rather than left for a reader to assume.

⚠ **What was NOT re-run at all, and why:** `U1` (the payload survives the real `JsonHubProtocol`) and `U2`
(a singleton `CircuitHandler` sees every circuit) were settled on the box by the previous cycle and
are unchanged by this one. `U3` needed no re-run because `D30` settled it as a decision rather than a
measurement.

### Two plan defects found by the same cycle, both fixed here

- **Task 12's scope gate contradicted Task 10c.** Gate 2 grepped the diff for the queued-state literal and expected nothing; Task 10c ⭐ *required* that literal in `AttendedPlaybackCircuitHandlerTests`. Scoped.
- **Task 8's `ANaturalEndDisarmsTheDurationCap` could not fail as specified.** Its `StopCalls` assertion held whether or not the disarm existed, because `ClaimTerminal` absorbs the late stop before it reaches the source — measured, **62/62 green with the disarm deleted**. Rewritten to assert on the cap's own log line, which goes red under that mutation. The plan's stated reason (*"it would only touch the source a second time"*) was wrong.
