## Dependency / ordering notes

- **GV-1 is the foundation.** GV-2 and GV-3 both depend on GV-1 (DTOs, the extended `GvBridgeApiService`, the new `PhoneHubService` events, `GvBridgeStatusService`, `PhoneUnreadState`, and the `PhoneMessagesPanel` shell). Builder must merge GV-1 before claiming either.
- **GV-2 and GV-3 are independent of each other** and may ship in either order after GV-1. **Recommended order: GV-1 → GV-2 → GV-3.** One shared coupling: GV-2 introduces the `FeedItem` interleave projection (PR2 Task 5) that GV-3 reuses; if GV-3 is built first, its plan instructs the implementer to introduce that projection there instead.
- **⚠ CORRECTED 2026-07-31 — the read experience is NOT functional end-to-end.** This bullet previously claimed "read experience ships fully today." **That claim was wrong** and is retracted. Our **client** work (GV-1/GV-2/GV-3/GV-4) is complete and correct against the documented contract, and it renders correctly given data — but **the server has never returned any.** RotaryPhone's `PositionalGvThreadParser.ThreadsArray()` requires a JSON **object** root with a `"threads"` property, while the request is built as positional protojson (`alt=protojson`), which returns **arrays**. The guard returns `null` on every real response → **0 items** → `Succeeded: true` (which only ever meant "the JSON parsed") → a clean, empty **HTTP 200**. So **voicemail and SMS read have never returned data against live Google Voice.** Their tests pass because every fixture hand-feeds the parser the synthetic shape it assumes, and their ADR admits the positions were synthesis with a §11 live-verification checklist that was never performed. Defect is in **`rp-deploy`**; a capture-then-fix cycle is underway on their side. **Nothing to build here** — our side needs no change — but the honest status is **"read UI complete, read data blocked upstream,"** not "shipped." **✅ SUPERSEDED 2026-07-31 (later the same day) — their parser fix (`627b928`) landed and read data IS now flowing**: a [live UAT](uat/2026-07-31-gv-live-data/REPORT.md) rendered 20 real SMS threads and 20 voicemails with real transcripts. **Current honest status: the data arrives; the read *experience* has three defects layered on top of it** — UAT F-1, split into **GV-8** here plus two RotaryPhone items (§ Cross-repo handoffs #5/#6). The correction above is left in place rather than deleted: it was right when written, and the sequence *"shipped" → "never worked" → "works, with new defects"* is itself the useful record.
- **Send status.** **`POST /api/gvbridge/sms/send` has SHIPPED on RotaryPhone** (dark behind their `GVBridge:EnableSmsSend=false`), so the endpoint is not the blocker. **Send is scoped as GV-5** (ADR-028), now **🔒 blocked** pending re-derivation against the deployed tree — see the row. GV-5 makes send *correct*; flipping `RotaryPhone:Gv:SendEnabled=true` stays a separate two-side decision afterwards (theirs first, then ours).
- **GV-8 and GV-6 are the same bug class — and are deliberately NOT merged.** Both are "`GvBridgeApiService` maps every non-2xx to `null`, destroying the distinction the caller needs." **Judgement call, recorded so it can be revisited rather than rediscovered:** they are kept as two rows because they differ on everything except that root shape — different methods (the two *mark* methods vs. the thread-*read* method), different dependencies (**GV-4** vs. **GV-3**), and different severity and audience (**GV-6 is diagnostic quality for operators with no user-visible affordance by design**; **GV-8 is a HIGH, user-visible correctness bug that currently lies to the user**). Merging would couple a HIGH fix to a low-priority operator nicety and would saddle GV-8 with an unnecessary dependency on GV-4. **What they should share is the idiom, not the PR:** whoever ships first introduces the outcome/status discrimination in `GvBridgeApiService` in a **reusable** shape, and the second row adopts it instead of inventing a second mechanism. **Preferred order: GV-8 first** — it carries the higher value and establishes the pattern; GV-6 then conforms to it. _If a Builder finds the shared shape is doing all the work and the two remainders are trivial, merging them is a defensible call — but say so explicitly rather than quietly._
- **⚠ UAT discipline for the `/phone` texts surface — record wall-clock time, or the results are noise.** Until RotaryPhone's cookie-refresh fix lands (§ Cross-repo handoffs #6), GV auth is **dead for ~9 minutes out of every 20**, on a wall-clock cycle that is independent of what the tester does. **Any UAT of this surface that does not note the time will produce results that look random** — that is exactly how the 2026-07-31 pass came to hypothesise throttling, a hypothesis the logs then falsified. **Test within the first ~10 minutes after a `CDP cookie refresh` log line, and record the timestamp in the report.** Probe, kept bounded because the Intel N100 does not tolerate heavy journald reads well: `journalctl -u rotary-phone --since '-30min' | grep 'api2thread/list returned'`. The matching client-side probe is `journalctl -u radio-web --since '-30min' | grep 'Failed to get GV SMS thread'`. **Do not tail either.**
  - **✅ BETTER PROBE, twice-confirmed (2026-07-31 root-cause pass + GV-8 UAT) — prefer this over the journal reads above.** `curl -s http://192.168.86.50:5004/api/gvbridge/status` returns **`psidtsAgeSeconds`**, which is a **live blackout clock**: **`< 660` healthy · `660–1200` blackout · resets at ~1200.** One request, no journald load, and it tells you the phase *before* you spend a test case. **It is also the only trustworthy field in that payload** — `{"available": true, "degraded": false, "cookiesValid": true, "psidtsAgeSeconds": 707}` was captured while both SMS endpoints were returning hard 502s. Full write-up in `design/INTEGRATIONS.md` § Google Voice (gvbridge) Messages → Gotchas. **Also note the box is at `192.168.86.50`; `radio`/`piradio` do not resolve from WSL** (now recorded in `CLAUDE.md` § Deployment — two sessions rediscovered it the hard way).
- **⚠ OUTSTANDING VERIFICATION — GV-8's `M-1` shipped but has never been observed live. It is NOT a pass, and nothing downstream should treat it as one.** The fix is the branch ordering at `PhoneTextsPanel.razor:61` — `else if (Error && Messages == null)`, i.e. **arriving content outranks a stale error flag** — so an inbound SMS landing on an open-but-*failed* thread renders the message instead of leaving the user on `cloud_off`. **What is proven:** the condition is in the shipped binary, the four-way ordering (skeleton → error → empty → list) was observed live in all four states, and `PhoneTextsPanelTests:141` (`Conversation_ShowsMessages_WhenErrorSetButMessagesArrived`) covers it at the unit level. **What is not:** the live transition itself. It requires a **real inbound SMS to arrive while a failed thread is open** — no inbound traffic arrived during the GV-8 UAT window, and the Tester **declined to inject a synthetic message**, which was the right call: a manufactured message would have proven the render path, not the arrival path. **Closing it costs nothing and needs no Builder cycle** — with the failed thread open during a blackout (`psidtsAgeSeconds` between 660 and 1200, per the probe below), have anyone text the GV number and watch the pane flip from `cloud_off` to the bubble. **Whoever does it: record it in the GV-8 UAT report and strike this bullet.** Until then, "GV-8 passed 10/10" means **ten cases plus one that could not be run**, exactly as its report states.
- **Playwright MCP does not work in this WSL instance — deliberately NOT a queue row; recorded here so the next UAT does not re-derive the workaround.** This is environment/tooling, not product: neither candidate fix is a PR against this repo, so there is nothing for a Builder to claim. **The failure:** the plugin is configured for the `chrome` channel and resolves `/opt/google/chrome/chrome`, which does not exist here; installing it needs root and there is **no passwordless sudo** on this WSL instance. **The substitute that worked (GV-8 UAT), reusable as-is:** Chromium **151.0.7922.10** from the Playwright bundle (`npx playwright install chromium` — **no root required**), with `libnss3` / `libnspr4` extracted from Ubuntu `.deb`s into a scratch dir and picked up via `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`; driven over **CDP** from `playwright-core`. **Nothing was installed system-wide and nothing outside the scratchpad was modified.** Two things this bought beyond mere parity, worth knowing before anyone "fixes" it back to the MCP: raw CDP is what made the `MutationObserver` animation probe and the frame-level screencast possible, and those are the only reason `L-1` could be stated as a measurement (`Animation.currentTime`, 14.5 % pixel delta) rather than an impression. **Also note the viewport must be asserted in-page** (`window.innerWidth/innerHeight`), not assumed from a launch flag — the screencast run came back **1920×577**, not 1920×720. **The permanent fix is an owner decision, not a Builder task:** either install Chrome on the WSL box, or point the plugin at `--browser chromium`. **The plugin config was deliberately left untouched** — it is the user's environment, and a Tester silently editing it would be exactly the kind of unrequested config change this project's agents are told not to make.
- **GV-5 is claimable now and is independent of GV-4.** It touches `GvBridgeSendService`, `PhoneTextsPanel`, `MessageBubble`, `PhoneHubService`, and `PhonePage`. The only overlap with GV-4 is `PhoneHubService` (a sibling `.On<>` registration) and `PhonePage` (a sibling handler beside `OnGvSmsReceived`); GV-4 is merged, so there is no conflict — but a Builder rebasing an old branch should expect both files to have moved.
- **The 2026-08-10 tranche (`TEST-1`, `AUD-1…4`, `OPS-2`, `OPS-3`, `TEST-2`) has a priority order, and the order is not arbitrary.** These eight rows are the deferred remainder of one debugging session, so nothing forces them into sequence the way the GV arc is sequenced — but the ranking is deliberate and should be respected absent a reason. **`TEST-1` first, and by a wide margin.** It is not the most user-visible row; it is the row that makes every other row's verification mean something. Four tests flaked under load on 2026-08-10 and the flake was **read as a real regression before it was recognised as noise** — an unreliable suite does not merely waste a cycle, it produces confidently wrong diagnoses. Every other row in this tranche is gated on a green full-suite run, so clearing `TEST-1` first is the cheapest way to make seven downstream verdicts trustworthy. **Then the correctness rows** — `AUD-1` (an active metadata defect with a booby-trapped "obvious fix"), `AUD-2` (potentially a silently dead core pattern), `OPS-2`, `OPS-3`. **Then the deferred four** — `AUD-3`, `AUD-4`, `TEST-2` — which are real but none of which is currently lying to a user. **The owner set this ranking; do not reshuffle it without asking.** _**Updated 2026-08-11 — status change only, ranking untouched.** `AUD-3` shipped inside PR #468, so the tail of this order is now **`AUD-4`, `TEST-2`** (the "deferred four" only ever listed three — a pre-existing miscount, left as written). **`TEST-1` remains first and nothing else moved.** Both remaining tail rows gained scope-shaping notes from #468 — `AUD-4` a third teardown layer, `TEST-2` two more internal seams — which **raises what each has to reckon with without raising its rank.**_ _**Updated 2026-08-11 (second pass) — one row ADDED into the correctness band; nothing existing moved.** **`AUD-5` slots immediately after `AUD-1`**, making the order **`TEST-1` → `AUD-1` → `AUD-5` → `AUD-2` → `OPS-2` → `OPS-3` → `AUD-4` → `TEST-2``. **`TEST-1` is untouched at the top — the owner set that and this pass did not go near it**, and `AUD-1` keeps its position for the same reason. **Why `AUD-5` lands where it does, stated so it can be argued with rather than rediscovered.** It ranks **above `AUD-2`** on three counts: its mechanism is **confirmed by construction** and needs no investigation phase, where `AUD-2` is an explicitly unverified code-read inference that may close as "no defect"; it is **user-visible** in the most direct way in this tranche (the volume changes and stays changed); and it is the **only row here that can silently write wrong state to *persistent* config**, so its blast radius outlives the process. It ranks **below `AUD-1`** because `AUD-1` is an *observed, currently-firing* metadata defect with a booby-trapped obvious fix, while `AUD-5`'s mechanism is proven but its firing in production is still a hypothesis. It is also **the cheapest fix in the band** — a parameter, one await-free re-check, and one deliberate decision — which is a tie-breaker, not the reason._ _**Updated 2026-08-11 (third pass) — two rows ADDED as a pair; again, nothing existing moved.** **`AUD-6` then `AUD-7` slot immediately after `AUD-1` and above `AUD-5`**, making the order **`TEST-1` → `AUD-1` → `AUD-6` → `AUD-7` → `AUD-5` → `AUD-2` → `OPS-2` → `OPS-3` → `AUD-4` → `TEST-2`**. **`TEST-1` is untouched at the top — the owner set that and this pass did not go near it.** **Why the pair lands above `AUD-5`, stated so it can be argued with rather than rediscovered.** `AUD-5` sits where it does partly because *"its mechanism is proven but its firing in production is still a hypothesis"* — **that qualification does not apply to either of these.** Both were **measured on the box today**: `AUD-6` by comparing two days' enumeration against an unchanged stored value, `AUD-7` by comparing `/api/devices/output` against live PipeWire sink state. They share `AUD-5`'s persistent-state blast radius (`AUD-6` is *worse* on that axis — the stored value does not change, its **meaning** does, so no inspection of the store can detect it) and they exceed it on user-visibility: a volume that moves is noticeable and correctable, whereas an output selector that names the wrong device is **actively misleading about where sound is going** and gives the user no way to tell. **Why they land below `AUD-1` — and this one is discipline, not severity.** A case can be made that `AUD-7` outranks `AUD-1` outright: misrouted audio is a larger failure than a rewritten track title. **`AUD-1` keeps its position because this file's convention is that a pass adds rows and does not reshuffle the owner's ranking**, and because `AUD-1` is fully scoped and cheap, so nothing is gained by displacing it. **If the owner wants `AUD-7` above `AUD-1`, that is a defensible call and needs only a word — it was not taken unilaterally.**_
- **`AUD-2` and `AUD-4` are two symptoms of ONE root cause — claim `AUD-2` first.** The root is key identity: a source registers its audio component under a key it mints itself (`SDRRadioAudioSource.cs:908` → `sdr-radio-<guid>`) while `AudioManager` addresses it by `IAudioSource.Id` (`AudioSourceBase.cs:28` → `Radio-<guid>`). `AUD-2` sees it from the **ducking/gain** side (setters that write to a key nothing reads back); `AUD-4` sees it from the **teardown** side (a sweep that would miss the SDR component). **`AUD-2` is investigation-first and cheap, and it decides the key**, so doing it first prevents `AUD-4` from engineering a sweep around a mismatch that `AUD-2` may simply remove. _Judgement recorded so it can be revisited rather than rediscovered: they are kept as two rows because `AUD-2` may close as "no defect" after one observation, while `AUD-4`'s rename-and-unify work stands on its own merits either way. If a Builder finds the single-key fix does all the work and `AUD-4`'s remainder is just the rename, merging them is defensible — but say so explicitly rather than quietly._ **⚠ Added 2026-08-11: these two now have SIBLINGS, and the pattern is worth naming.** `AUD-6` and `AUD-7` are the same failure at the **device** layer that `AUD-2` and `AUD-4` are at the **source** layer — *an identifier that is not stable being used as if it were*, then addressed by a second layer that assumes a different key. **Four of the eleven rows in the 2026-08-10 tranche now turn on that one idea.** They are **not** being merged and share no files, but whoever claims any of them should read the other three's traps first: the recurring shape is that **the mismatch fails silently and something logs or reports success anyway** (`SetGainOffset`'s *"Applied gain offset …"*, `RemoveSource`'s *"Removed audio source … from mixer"*, and now `/api/devices/output`'s `isActive`). _Not queued as a fifth "unify all identity" row, deliberately: three of the four still need their own investigation or migration, and a grand unification written before `AUD-2` reports back would be exactly the premature abstraction `03a6fea` already demonstrated._
- **`AUD-6` and `AUD-7` are one report split into two rows, and the ORDER BETWEEN THEM IS LOAD-BEARING — claim `AUD-6` first.** One deploy produced one symptom (*the output the app names is not the output the audio uses*) with **two independent causes**, and they are queued separately on the same test this file already applies to `GV-6`/`GV-8`: different mechanism, different trigger, different fix shape, different verification. `AUD-6` is a **persisted-format** defect — the id is an enumeration ordinal, so the stored preference changes meaning when enumeration reorders; its fix is a stable key **plus a migration over every existing store**, and it is **intermittent** (it needs a reshuffle). `AUD-7` is a **missing-call** defect — the native device switch (`SoundFlowAudioEngine.SwitchPlaybackDevice:838`) has exactly one caller, the interactive HTTP path, and startup never reaches it; its fix is a call plus an honest `isActive`, and it is **deterministic** (every restart where the preference is not the system default, reordering or not). **Merging them would gate a cheap deterministic fix behind a data migration**, which is the opposite of what this file usually optimises for. **⚠ The ordering constraint, which is the reason this bullet exists rather than a one-line cross-reference: `AUD-7`'s fix makes startup ACT on the persisted preference, and acting on an unstable key is strictly worse than ignoring it.** Shipping `AUD-7`'s routing half against today's store would send audio to USB Audio Out because `playback-1` now resolves there — converting a **mis-report** into a **mis-route**. Today's mis-report is, perversely, protective: it falls back to the system default. So either land `AUD-6` first, or land only `AUD-7`'s **reporting** half (make the UI honest) and hold the routing half. **What is NOT a reason to reorder them: that `AUD-7` is the more serious of the two.** It is — and it still goes second. _If a Builder finds the stable-key change and the startup call are genuinely one edit and the split is costing more than it saves, merging them is defensible — but say so explicitly rather than quietly, and do not merge them by simply claiming `AUD-7` first._
- **⚠ A FOURTH AND FIFTH INSTANCE OF THE COMMENT-ACCURACY FAILURE CLASS, found while verifying `AUD-7`. Recorded here, not queued — the fixes belong inside `AUD-7`'s PR, not in a row of their own.** `CLAUDE.md` § Pre-Merge Review enumerates three worked examples of a comment asserting something the code does not do. `AUD-7` supplies two more, and the second is the more instructive. **(4) Production code.** `SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs:222-224` states that *"The local-device switch (native MiniAudio swap) is the caller's responsibility via `IAudioDeviceManager.SetOutputDeviceAsync`"* — **`SoundFlowDeviceManager.SetOutputDeviceAsync` (`:213-244`) performs no native swap at all**; it validates an id against a cache, assigns a string, and persists. The comment does not merely overclaim about itself, it **assigns a responsibility to a named collaborator that the collaborator does not discharge**, which is why every reader of the gate concludes the swap happens somewhere. **(5) A test.** `AudioEngineInitializationServiceStartupTests.cs:81-83` says the persisted local device must route through the gate *"not just the device-manager swap"* — **naming a swap that does not exist** — while the test body (`:97-99`) verifies only the gate call. **This is the one worth generalising: a test comment can carry the same lie as a production comment, and it is more dangerous there,** because a green test reads as evidence. The existing checklist item says to check that comments assert only what the code does; **it is worth extending to test comments and test names, which currently escape it** — `RoutesThroughGate` is an accurate name for what the test asserts, and its comment is not.
- **⚠ Both `AUD-2` and `AUD-4` exist because a log line asserted a success it never verified — treat that as the pattern, not the incident.** `SoundFlowMasterMixer.RemoveSource` logs *"Removed audio source … from mixer"* while removing only a `List<IAudioSource>` entry (`SoundFlowMasterMixer.cs:118`), which is why commit `03a6fea` landed one layer too high and **did nothing for months without anyone noticing**. `SoundFlowPlaybackService.SetGainOffset` logs *"Applied gain offset …"* (`:620`) after a dictionary write whose corresponding lookup may have matched nothing. **Neither log is wrong about what it did; both are wrong about what they imply.** Whoever fixes either should fix the log wording in the same PR — a correct fix behind a misleading log line is one refactor away from being undone again.
- **~~`AUD-3` is 🔒 on PR #468, which is a PR and not a queue row.~~ RESOLVED 2026-08-11 — #468 merged (`8b1ce0a`) and `AUD-3` is ✅ shipped inside it.** The deviation this note recorded (🔒 pointing at a PR rather than a row, because neither 🔒 nor ⛔ fits an in-repo PR pending an owner decision) never had to resolve either way: **the blocker and the fix turned out to be the same PR.** The practical rule it stated — *"if #468 merges, re-site the anchors and flip to 📋"* — was followed except for the mark, which went to ✅ rather than 📋. The re-siting was done and confirms this note's own prediction: `TryConnectCastAsync` is now `AudioEngineInitializationService.cs:499` on `main`. _Kept rather than deleted, because the reasoning behind the deviation is worth more than a tidy-looking legend — the next in-repo-PR blocker should be recorded the same way._
- **⚠ REVERSED 2026-08-11 — this file previously said "Do not read PR #468's merge as closing `AUD-3`," and once #468 was extended that instruction became FALSE. #468's merged diff DOES close `AUD-3`.** The original note was correct about #468 *as it stood on 2026-08-10*: a post-`StartAsync` teardown guard closing the observable outcome and nothing more. The owner then directed the race itself be closed before merge, and commits `47da897` + `1fded0c` did exactly that — `GoogleCastOutput` grew a lifecycle lock plus a connection generation, the resolved receiver moved from a field to a local, and the engine grew `BeginCastConnectAsync`/`TryCommitCastConnectAsync` over an epoch bumped under `_activeOutputLock`. **The replacement rule is the inverse of the old one: "the Cast race is still open" is now the wrong summary, and the right one is "the race is closed in code and has never been exercised on hardware."** The `AUD-3` row carries the evidence and the three things that did *not* ship — the missing hardware UAT above all. _Recorded loudly because a stale "do not read the merge as closing this" is worse than no note at all: it would have kept a shipped fix looking open indefinitely._
- **~~🚧 PR #471 is IN FLIGHT and deliberately has NO row.~~ ✅ MERGED 2026-08-11 as `61187bb` — and the anchor churn this note predicted DID NOT HAPPEN.** `fix/serialize-native-device-enumeration` (native `pa_mainloop` enumeration serialization + playback-device lifecycle gating) landed with `SoundFlowAudioEngine.cs`, `SoundFlowDeviceManager.cs`, `FilePlayerAudioSource.cs`, `USBAudioSourceBase.cs`, `WindowsBluetoothService.cs` and `NowPlayingPanel.razor` touched, plus new `NativeAudioDeviceGate.cs` + `SerializedMiniAudioEngine.cs` and five new test files. **The note above warned that the `8b1ce0a` re-verification on `AUD-1`/`AUD-2`/`AUD-4` was "good until #471 merges and not after." It merged, the anchors were re-checked against `0870410`, and they all hold — so the warning is discharged rather than triggered.** Specifically: **`AUD-1`'s two `FilePlayerAudioSource.cs` anchors survived #471's edits to that very file** — `:1988` is still the `needsFingerprinting` gate and `:2112` is still the overwrite branch, because #471's hunks in that file net out above and below them — and its other six (`FingerprintingOptions.cs:19`, `appsettings.json:91`, `ApiModels.cs:788`, `BluetoothAudioSource.cs:837`/`:845`/`:893`, `BackgroundIdentificationService.cs:260`) are in files #471 never opened. **`AUD-2` and `AUD-4` are entirely unaffected: #471 touched NONE of the files they cite** — `SoundFlowPlaybackService.cs`, `AudioManager.cs`, `SoundFlowMasterMixer.cs`, `SDRRadioAudioSource.cs` and `AudioSourceBase.cs` are all untouched, and every one of their ~20 anchors re-verifies byte-exact. **No row has been invented for what #471 leaves open**, which is the same discipline this note originally recorded — re-triage is deliberate work, not an assumption, and nothing in #471's diff has been read as closing or opening a row without checking. **⚠ One file DID move, and it is not one this note anticipated: `GoogleCastOutput.cs`.** PR #473 (`0870410`) grew its header comment by exactly **+66 lines**, so **every anchor below `:32` in that file shifted +66** — `_lifecycleLock` `:39 → :105`, `_connectionGeneration` `:45 → :111`, the two test seams `:54 → :120` / `:64 → :130`, `TryPublishConnectionAsync` `:599 → :665`, `DisconnectAsync`'s critical section `:643 → :709`, `StopAsync`'s `:930 → :996`, `DisposeAsync`'s `:1651 → :1717`. **`AUD-3`'s citations in that file are all pre-#473 and must be re-sited before reuse** (they are historical record on a ✅ row, so they have deliberately been left as written rather than mass-edited); **`AUD-5`'s are post-#473 and verified.**
- **⚠ #468 and #469 each shipped with ZERO documentation, and this reconciliation pass exists because of it.** Neither merge touched a single file under `docs/` or any `.md` (#467 at least updated `CLAUDE.md`). The consequence was concrete: `BUILDER_QUEUE.md` **and** `ROADMAP.md` both went on asserting *"#468 🔵 open, awaiting an owner decision"* and *"the race is untouched"* after the opposite had become true, and `AUD-3` sat 🔒 against a blocker that had already delivered the fix. **A PR that closes or invalidates a queue row must update that row in the same PR.** Stated here rather than as a row because it is a process correction, not claimable work — but it is the cheapest defect in this tranche to prevent, and it has now cost a session.
- **⚠ THE BUILDER ROLE POINTS AT TWO DOCUMENTS THAT DO NOT EXIST. Recorded here as an ordering note rather than a queue row — the decision and its reasoning are below, because this one is genuinely arguable.** PR #473's Builder found that **`docs/BUILDER_PROMPT.md` has never existed** — not deleted, not moved; there is no commit in this repository that ever created it — and that the Builder role definition additionally references a **"Handoff Protocol at the top of `docs/BUILDER_QUEUE.md`"** which **is also not there**: the top of this file is a *Last updated* banner followed by § Status legend, and no section by that name has ever existed either. **#473's Builder deliberately left both alone, and was right to** — inventing its own operating instructions mid-cycle is exactly the kind of unrequested scope this file keeps warning about. **Why this is NOT a queue row, stated so the decision can be overturned rather than rediscovered.** It would be a docs-only change to this repo, so on its face it is claimable — but the *content* of both documents is **the specification of Builder's own behaviour**, and a Builder dispatched to write it would be authoring the instructions it was dispatched under. That is circular in a way that no amount of review fixes: the reviewer would be checking the loop against the document the loop just wrote. **The precedent is already set twice in this section** — the Playwright-MCP note ("environment/tooling, not product … nothing for a Builder to claim") and the zero-documentation note directly above ("a process correction, not claimable work"). This is the same shape. **⚠ But do not read "not a row" as "not important," because the blast radius here is wider than either precedent.** Every Builder ever dispatched at this project has been pointed at a prompt file that does not exist and told to resume at "step 1 of the Handoff Protocol" that does not exist. **The reason nothing has visibly broken is that each Builder has silently reconstructed the loop from this file's shape** — the § Status legend, the 📋/🚧/✅ marks, and the one-PR-per-cycle convention in the header quote — and they have so far reconstructed it **compatibly**. That is luck holding, not a protocol working, and it is precisely the failure class this repo has now shipped four times: **a document asserting something that is not there** (`SoundFlowMasterMixer`'s log line, `BluetoothAudioSource`'s unreachable `Playing` branch, `GoogleCastOutput._lifecycleLock`'s over-claiming comment, and now the Builder role's own two citations). **The concrete deliverable, so whoever picks it up is not starting from nothing:** (1) a `## Handoff Protocol` section at the top of this file, above § Status legend, stating the claim→branch→implement→review→UAT→docs→PR→merge→loop cycle and the rule for picking the next eligible row; (2) `docs/BUILDER_PROMPT.md` as the paste-trigger that points at it. **Owner or Planner authors both; a Builder may then be pointed at them, but must not be the one to write them.** Until they exist, § Status legend plus this note *are* the protocol, and that is worth saying out loud rather than leaving implicit.
- **For the record — where the pre-merge checklist lives, and why it is in `CLAUDE.md` rather than anywhere more obvious.** PR #473's checklist item (*do comments, log messages and XML docs assert only what the code actually does?*) landed in **`CLAUDE.md` under a new `## Pre-Merge Review` section**. **The location was a deliberate choice among poor options:** this repo has **no `CONTRIBUTING.md` and no PR template**, so there is no conventional home for a standing review convention; and a checklist written into an individual plan **dies with that plan** the moment its cycle closes — which is how the three comment-accuracy defects it enumerates came to be *rediscovered* three separate times rather than caught once. `CLAUDE.md` is the one file every agent in this project reads unprompted, which makes it the only location where the checklist is actually load-bearing. **Noted here because the queue is where cross-cycle conventions get looked for**, and a reviewer hunting for the checklist should not have to guess.
- **`OPS-2` is hygiene and must not be re-labelled as a bug fix.** A floating-package-version theory was investigated on 2026-08-10 as the cause of a CI failure and was **disproved**. Pinning is worth doing for reproducibility; it fixes no known defect, and citing it as the root cause of that CI failure would be wrong.
- **`OPS-3` is the only row in this tranche exempt from the auto-merge-on-green policy.** Switching `Requires=` to `BindsTo=` changes how two production services fail together on the box. Green gates are necessary but not sufficient; the owner confirms the tradeoff (a dark console instead of a UI with a dead backend) on the PR itself.

- **`KIOSK-1` sits above `TEST-1` from 2026-08-18, by the owner's explicit call, and the ordering note above about the 2026-08-10 tranche is unchanged by it.** `TEST-1` is still the head of that tranche; `KIOSK-1` is one row jumping the whole list because it is the only queued defect that fires on **every deploy** — each one leaves the panel dark until someone relaunches the browser by hand. That makes it a tax on every other row's verification rather than a competing priority: several rows in this queue can only be confirmed at the glass, and the glass is dead after a deploy. **This is not a precedent for reshuffling the queue** — Builder read top-down as normal; the promotion was an owner decision recorded before the claim.
- **`KIOSK-1` before `KIOSK-2`, and the reason is mechanical rather than editorial.** `KIOSK-2`'s `Exit to Desktop` distinguishes the kiosk Chrome from the Google Voice bridge Chrome **by profile path**. Until `KIOSK-1` gives the kiosk its own `--user-data-dir`, the only distinguishing feature is the *absence* of the bridge's flag — a negative match the design rejects, and one that would leave the current `pkill -f chrome` behaviour (which kills the bridge, and nothing restarts it on demand — its watchdog is a 2-minute cadence) in place for the interval. The same profile is what `KIOSK-2`'s single-instance `pgrep` guard matches on. `KIOSK-2` is **blocked** until `KIOSK-1` merges.
- **The GV Bridge desktop entry is deleted in `KIOSK-2`, not `KIOSK-1`, deliberately.** Deleting it in `KIOSK-1` would remove the owner's on-demand way to start the bridge before `KIOSK-2` supplies the replacement. The bridge watchdog timer is enabled and active, so the exposure is bounded — but there is no reason to open it at all.
- **Plan Task A0 is a gate.** Four later decisions branch on what the box reports (zenity's GTK major, `--timeout`/`--icon-name`/`--default-cancel` support, `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` in the user manager's environment, the `rotary-phone` unit name). Guessing any of them ships either a dialog that cannot be tapped or a `PHONE` row that is a **permanent false red** — which is precisely the cry-wolf failure the design's Google Voice rule exists to prevent.
- **Two checks in `KIOSK-2` genuinely need the owner's eyes, and that is a property of the box, not a gap in the plan.** Shell's screenshot API and `GetWindows` are `AccessDenied` on GNOME 46 and `gnome-screenshot` / `grim` / `scrot` are absent, so the 56 px button size and the icon's legibility at the panel cannot be measured remotely. Everything else is an objective probe — established-connection counts, process flags, CDP reachability, file modes, and the launcher's own `--print-status` / `--dry-run` / `--dialog-demo` affordances, which exist specifically so tier decisions are verifiable over SSH. **Do not substitute plausibility for measurement on the two that are not.**
- **`radio-refresh-browser` is NOT fixed by these rows and no row claims it is.** `KIOSK-1` restores CDP on `:9223` as a side effect of the dedicated profile, which makes a CDP-based refresh *possible*; the shipped helper still drives `xdotool`, which does not work under Wayland. Recorded as a fast-follow, not folded in — it is a different mechanism with its own verification.
- **`ENC-8` and `ENC-12` were planned in one pass, and the reason is not convenience.** `ENC-12`'s third surface (*“the status card carries the field-level `Sent` vs `Read back` table plus retry / restore”*) and `ENC-8`'s card 1 (*“Status — connection, config tier, last verified, last saved-to-device with a staleness comparison”*) **are the same card, described twice by two rows written at different times.** Planned apart it gets built twice and the second build fights the first. **`ENC-8` owns it — the whole page, all four cards, every action button, every provisioning endpoint. `ENC-12` owns the topbar badge and the notification, and adds nothing to that page.** The two transports differ on purpose and that is not an inconsistency: the page **pulls** at 2 Hz only while it is open (handoff §7.9 and the `UI-2` load rule — a background diagnostic poll on this N100 is exactly the incidental load that correlates with audible distortion), while the badge must be right on `/queue`, so it gets **one push event carrying the tier and nothing else**.
- **Order: `ENC-8` before `ENC-12`, and it is a real ordering rather than a preference.** `ENC-12`'s notification copy ends in an action — Designer §7.6 writes it as *“→ Open encoder settings”*. A toast whose entire payoff is a page that does not exist yet teaches the owner that the link is useless. Build the destination, then the sign that points at it. `ENC-12` also edits the same `HidRotaryEncoderService.ConfigStatus` property that `ENC-8` adds retained state around, so taking them in the other order means resolving that twice.
- **Neither row remaps the router, and `ENC-8` is shaped so that `ENC-5`/`ENC-7` can.** `ENC-8` Task 3 replaces the router's `switch` blocks with **one array that dispatch and display both read**, and serves the Settings page's mapping table from it. The remap then becomes an edit to that array with the page following automatically. ⚠ **If `ENC-5`/`ENC-7` land first, `ENC-8` Task 3 converts their switch into the array rather than adding an array beside it** — a descriptive list kept next to a `switch` is two sources of truth wearing one name, which is the defect the task exists to remove. Note also that the mapping table's *contents* are already correct (#489); what this fixes is the cause, not the symptom.
- **The encoder arc is a chain, not a set — punch list §2 `O5` and `O10`.** `ENC-1` (the decoder rewrite) gates every other encoder row, and `ENC-1` → `ENC-2` → `ENC-11` → the rest is an ordering, not a preference. All four are shipped, which is why **`ENC-4` is claimable now**. What `ENC-4` in turn gates: `ENC-5` (SOURCE overlay) and `ENC-7` (PRESETS) both mount into the HUD host it builds, and `ENC-12` (the config-fault notification) needs somewhere to speak — `ENC-11`'s tiered fault model works and is verified on hardware, but a Degraded or Hard-fault outcome is currently **visible only in the log and the API**. The safety response is real and silent until `ENC-4` lands the surface.
- **`ENC-5` and `ENC-7` should be built together or back to back**, per the design handoff §6.6: they are one component with two lists, on purpose — *learn one, you have learned both*. They are also where the `RotaryEncoderActionRouter` index→handler remap belongs, since they introduce the SOURCE and PRESETS handlers the remap would point at. **`ENC-4` deliberately leaves the router mapping alone** and adds a test pinning it, so the change has to be made on purpose rather than drifted into.
- **⚠ `ENC-4` → `ENC-5` → `ENC-7`, and the ordering is mechanical, not editorial.** `ENC-5` mounts into the seam `ENC-4` §2.5 declares and edits four of its files (`EncoderFeedbackService`, `EncoderHudService`, `EncoderHud.razor`, `IEncoderFeedbackSink`); `ENC-7` consumes the selector component `ENC-5` builds and takes the encoder index `ENC-5` parks. Neither can be claimed before its predecessor merges.
- **⭐ The router remap is split across `ENC-5` and `ENC-7` so no index is ever left without a handler.** `RotaryEncoderActionRouter` maps `0=Volume · 1=Tuning · 2=Source · 3=Visualization`; the cabinet reads `VOLUME · SOURCE · PRESETS · TUNING`, and `ENC-11` has pushed device configuration in the cabinet's order since #509. Index 0 is VOLUME under both, so **the knob with a safety hazard behind it has been correct all along** — indices 1–3 were deliberately left wrong because remapping earlier would have pointed encoder 2 at a PRESETS handler that did not exist. `ENC-5` moves SOURCE to index 1 and TUNING to index 3, retires the old source cycler, and parks the visualiser on index 2; `ENC-7` replaces it with PRESETS. **Leaving the old cycler on index 2 during the interim was rejected**: it would give two adjacent knobs two divergent copies of the source selection, which is the defect class Designer §4.4 spends a paragraph forbidding.
- **Two mapping tests are expected to go red, one per PR, and neither is a regression.** `ENC-4` Task 5 pins the current index→handler table *on purpose* so that changing it has to be deliberate; `ENC-5` Task 8 rewrites it to `(0 VOLUME, 1 SOURCE, 2 VISUALIZER, 3 TUNING)` and `ENC-7` Task 5 changes the third row to `PRESETS`. Each plan names the assertion and forbids chasing the red by reverting the remap. **`ENC-5` also removes a live trap:** `ENC-4` hard-codes the encoder index in each handler's `PublishHud` call, so after a remap the tuning card would render in the SOURCE quarter — the card above the wrong knob, which is the one thing `ENC-4` exists to get right. `ENC-5` threads the real event index through instead.
- **`ENC-5` is where tuning acceleration goes live for the first time.** Device config already pushes the tuning tiers `(150 ×2 / 80 ×4 / 40 ×8)` to encoder 3, which today drives the visualiser; after the remap they reach the tuner, `TuningClamp = 8` stops being theoretical, and `StepRadioFrequencyAsync` awaits **one hardware call per step** — up to eight per detent on a box where incidental load correlates with audible distortion. It is the designed feel (§5.5) and the clamp bounds it, but `ENC-5`'s UAT check H2 exists because this is where it first happens.
- **`ENC-7` removes the visualiser's knob, and `ENC-9` is the row that was supposed to replace it — it is P1 and not queued.** The capability survives today on `VisualizerPanel`'s six-segment picker and the System Config dropdown, both shipped, so `ENC-7` does not block on it. But the punch list's own justification for `ENC-9` is *"removing a knob must not remove a capability"*, and `ENC-7` is the moment the knob goes — so this is the moment to notice `ENC-9` is unqueued rather than after the cabinet is closed.
- **⚠ CORRECTED 2026-09-04 — the phone arc is EIGHT PRs, not seven, and `PHN-1a` is only the first — do not read the punch list's `PHN-1` row as one claimable item.** The punch list scopes `PHN-1` at 1.5-2 weeks for "the seam + Feature A". That is not one PR, and the pieces carry sharply different risk, so `design/plans/PHN-arc-pr-breakdown.md` sequences it: **1** core contracts (`PHN-1a`) · **2** `GvMediaClient` + bounded LRU cache + API-side auth (`PHN-1b`) · **3** `EventPlaybackService` + `POST /api/audio/events` (`PHN-1c`) · **4** priority becomes load-bearing (`PHN-1d`) · **5** server-owned playback state + the three stop conditions (`PHN-1e`) · **5b** the mirror-case queue and the ducking-args change (`PHN-1f`) · **6** `PHN-2`, retire the `<audio>` element · **7** Feature C, canned replies. **PRs 1-4 have shipped** (#528, #534, #556, #558). **`PHN-1e` is planned and claimable; `PHN-1f` is queued but has no plan**; PRs 6 and 7 have a scope and an estimate in the breakdown but no plan file, and each needs a Planner pass before it is queued. `O6` (`PHN-1` before or with `PHN-2`/`PHN-3`) is satisfied by claiming these rows in order. ⚠ **What this note said before, because it is the note a reader would have trusted:** *"The phone arc is seven PRs … sequences it into seven: … **5** server-owned playback state + the three stop conditions · **6** `PHN-2` · **7** Feature C … **Only PR 1 is planned.** PRs 2-7 have a scope and an estimate in the breakdown but no plan file."* Two things had outlived it: four PRs have since been planned and shipped, and **owner decision `D28` split PR 5 in two** (see the entry below). The seven-item sequence was never wrong about *order*; it is wrong about *count*, and a count is what a reader uses to know whether they have reached the end. ⚠ `design/plans/PHN-arc-pr-breakdown.md` **has not been re-cut** — its table is still the original seven rows, deliberately, and it now carries a dated amendment at the head of § *The PRs* pointing back here. It is the historical sequencing; **this note is the current one.**
- **⚠ `PHN-1a` registers nothing in DI, and that is deliberate — but PR 2 is where the DI hazard actually lands.** Checked while planning: `RotaryEncoderRegistrationTests` builds a real `ServiceProvider` and resolves from it, but only over `AddRotaryEncoders`; `ActiveSourceAccessorRegistrationTests` covers `AddSoundFlowAudio` by **descriptor inspection only**; and **no test anywhere calls `BuildServiceProvider` with `ValidateOnBuild` or `ValidateScopes`** — zero matches across `tests/`. So a new service registered in `AudioServiceExtensions.cs` with a missing or mis-scoped dependency would be caught by **nothing**, and would surface as a service that will not start on the appliance. `PHN-1a` is exempt because it adds no registration and changes no constructor signature; **PR 2 and PR 3 must each add a build-and-resolve guard test** under `tests/Radio.Infrastructure.Tests/DependencyInjection/`.
- **⚠ Carried into PR 2 from `PHN-1a`'s planning pass: ADR-029 §5.1's masking instruction is wrong about its own example.** It tells `GvMediaClient` to follow `PhoneContactLookupService`'s *"log-masking discipline (it masks numbers to `***1234`)"*. That file masks on exactly **one** line — the success-path debug at `:87-90` — and logs the **raw** number at `:78` and again in the REST catch at `:100`. The rule for `GvMediaClient` is **mask on every line**, voicemail ids included; copying the cited file verbatim would leak.
- **`PHN-1f` before PR 6 (`PHN-2`), not after.** `D28`'s reasoning is that **the queue goes where its visibility goes**: PR 6 is where a user first sees any of it, so the queue must already exist by then or PR 6 ships the mixing `D28` rejected. Ordering it after PR 6 would deliver the half of the decision the owner disliked and defer the half that redeems it — which is the exact argument he used to reject *refusing*.
- **`PHN-1f` is where PR 4's fade-window residual closes, and it is the last cheap chance.** `PHN-1d` shipped two acknowledged residuals: `DuckingService` raises its transition event **after** awaiting the attack fade, so a stop landing inside that ~100 ms window deletes the priority override first and a synchronous `GetPriority` still answers the category default 8; and the `ActiveEventCount == 0` guard that covers it has an acknowledged hole when some *other* source is still ducking. **`PHN-1f`'s `DuckingStateChangedEventArgs` change closes both as a by-product** — the priority is captured at raise time inside the lock, so there is nothing left to race and the guard is **deleted rather than supplemented**. ⚠ **The deadline is not PR 6.** The residual is unreachable while `PhoneIntegration:Enabled` is `false`, and **no row in this arc flips that key** — PR 6 flips `GvMedia:Enabled`, a different key. So the real constraint is *"before anything ever enables phone integration"*. ⛔ **Do not re-assign it to PR 6**, which is a `Radio.Web` row with no place for a `Radio.Core` contract change; bolting a third guard on there would add a defence instead of removing the need for one. The full reasoning is `PHN-1e` plan §5 (*"the fade window"* and *"`ActiveEventCount`'s residual"*), and the two code sites that describe the residual today are `EventPlaybackService`'s `OnDuckingStateChanged` `<remarks>` and `DuckingService`'s matching note — cited by content, because both are XML docs that move.

## Carried risks (baked into the plans as explicit tasks)

1. **Absolute audio URL** — the DTO `AudioUrl` is server-relative and resolves against the Web origin (`:5002`), 404ing. `GvBridgeApiService.GetVoicemailAudioUrl` rebuilds it absolute against `radio:5004`. Unit-tested in GV-1; the most likely silent-failure point.
2. **GV SMS ≠ trunk SMS** — the GV handler is `GvSmsReceived` on `PhoneHubService`/`/hub`; the pre-existing `GvTrunkHubService.SmsReceived` on `/hubs/gvtrunk` is a different product. Kept namespaced apart; cross-referenced in code comments (GV-1).
3. **Open thread to RotaryPhone** (GV-3 deliverable): request GV mark-read be pulled forward (decision 4) — ✅ **closed**, shipped as GV-4; ~~keep the voicemail audio endpoint unauthenticated/token-in-query when `X-RotaryPhone-Auth` ships (native `<audio>` can't send the header)~~ — ✅ **DISSOLVED, not resolved, by `PHN-1b` ([#534](https://github.com/mmackelprang/RTest/pull/534)).** The constraint existed only because the **browser** was the thing fetching the recording, and a native `<audio>` element cannot attach a request header. Radio.API now fetches it **server-side** through `GvMediaClient`, which attaches `X-RotaryPhone-Auth` itself (`GvMediaAuthHandler.cs:26`) — so the endpoint is free to require the header and there is nothing to ask RotaryPhone for. The `PHN-1b` commit calls this *"the mechanism that closes carried risk #3"*. ⚠ **Do not re-raise it when `PHN-2` retires the `<audio>` element** — that PR is the one that finishes removing the browser from the fetch path, which makes this *more* closed, not less; and confirm `SendSmsResponse` before wiring send — ✅ **closed by GV-5/ADR-028**, which read the as-built controller directly rather than their docs (several RotaryPhone-side handoff docs are stale and still claim the send endpoint does not exist).
4. **Trust their source over their docs for gvbridge contracts.** `D:/prj/RotaryPhone/docs/handoffs/radioconsole-gv-voicemail-sms-ui-handoff.md` still states "No `POST /api/gvbridge/sms/send` endpoint exists yet." It does. ADR-028 was written against `GvSmsController.cs` / `GvBridgeReadDtos.cs` / `GvThreadPoller.cs` directly; do the same for any future contract work.

## Cross-repo handoffs (RotaryPhone — NOT claimable here)

> These live in `D:\prj\RotaryPhone` and are **not** Radio Console queue rows. Routed via the documented protocol (boundary doc § "Passing Work Between Sessions" → create a file in `D:\prj\RotaryPhone\docs\prompts\`). Request files: **`D:/prj/RotaryPhone/docs/prompts/radioconsole-cdp-spam-and-build-stamp-request.md`** (items 1-2) and **`D:/prj/RotaryPhone/docs/prompts/radioconsole-gv-threadid-decode-and-auth-blackout-request.md`** (items 5-6).
>
> The boundary doc's Change Log was deliberately **not** touched — it is scoped to BT/audio ownership, and neither item is a BT/audio boundary change. The prompts channel is the right lane.

1. **`CdpCookieExtractor` log spam — `CDP: Cannot reach Chrome on port 9224` every ~20 min with a ~20-line stack trace.** Non-fatal (cookies stay valid via another path), but per project memory heavy journald churn on the Intel N100 **competes with the audio pipeline and correlates with audio distortion** — so this is a performance issue wearing a log-noise costume. **Concrete finding to hand them:** `GVBridgeConfig.ChromeCdpPort` defaults to **9224** (`GVBridgeConfig.cs:23`) and the extractor expects a Chrome with a live Google Voice tab; the only Chrome our deploy launches on that box is the **kiosk on port 9223** pointed at `http://localhost:5002` (`Deploy-ToLinux.ps1:363`) — a different instance for a different purpose. So nothing is listening on 9224 unless someone separately starts a GV-session Chrome. **May be absorbed into a larger bug** — a Tester is investigating whether this same CDP failure explains GV read endpoints returning **empty lists while reporting healthy**; see the request file for the causal chain.
2. **Build stamp for `rotary-phone`.** The 2026-07-29 incident (stale `rotary-phone` binary after a deploy restarted only `radio-api`/`radio-web`) is the same class of failure OPS-1 fixes on our side. Recommended: mirror our approach — `SourceRevisionId` → `AssemblyInformationalVersion` → a `/version` endpoint → deploy-time verification. ~~**Open question:** which tree is authoritative.~~ **✅ SETTLED 2026-07-31 — the deployed tree is `D:\prj\rp-deploy` @ `0a86898`, NOT `D:\prj\RotaryPhone`.** Confirmed on the live box by a Tester. This has real blast radius on our side: **ADR-028 was derived by reading `D:\prj\RotaryPhone`**, so GV-5 is now 🔒 blocked pending re-derivation. It also raises the priority of the stamp — source-tree parity is not deployed-binary parity, and until a stamp exists nothing proves the running binary came from `0a86898` either.

3. ~~**GV read has NEVER worked — positional parser defect (`rp-deploy`).**~~ ✅ **STALE — CORRECTED 2026-09-01 (`XR-1a`). Do NOT file a request for this; there is no cross-repo defect here.** The owner, answering D17: *"I can see voicemail and text messages in the Radio Console UI today. I can listen to the voicemail and read the texts on the screen."* **GV read works.** The original finding — that `PositionalGvThreadParser.ThreadsArray()` required a JSON object root while `alt=protojson` returns arrays, yielding 0 items behind a clean HTTP 200 — was accurate when written on 2026-07-31 and has since been fixed upstream. It is kept here struck through rather than deleted because the *shape* of the bug is worth remembering: `Succeeded: true` meant only "the JSON parsed", never "data was returned", so the failure was invisible to every caller. ⚠ Left uncorrected, this entry would send the next session chasing a defect that no longer exists, in a repo it does not own.

4. **Configured GV number does not match the live session.** The `GvPhoneNumber` value in RotaryPhone's config is **a different number** from the one the owner's live Google Voice session is actually bound to. _(Both values are deliberately **not recorded here** — this is a public repo. The configured value is in the box's `GVBridge` config; the live value is visible in the Google Voice session. Compare them there.)_ Independent of the parser defect — and note that **nothing validates configured identity against the live session**, so a mismatch fails silently. Two distinct faults could each present as "empty lists, healthy status," so this should be corrected *before* the parser fix is evaluated, or the fix will be assessed against a confounded baseline.

5. **Thread ids containing `/` are never decoded → HTTP 200 with `messages: []`; every group/MMS conversation is permanently unreadable.** **Confirmed by direct reproduction** in a verified-healthy window, 2026-07-31. We call with `Uri.EscapeDataString(threadId)`; **Kestrel deliberately leaves `%2F` encoded in the path** so it cannot forge a segment boundary, so their route value keeps the literal `%2F` while `%20` decodes normally — visible in their own log line: `Listed 0 SMS for thread g.Group Message.d5Mri%2FNrDUQgXNXNQehOfw (of 149 parsed)`. `GvSmsClient.ListMessagesAsync` then exact-string-compares (`all.Where(m => m.ThreadId == threadId)`), matches nothing, and `GvSmsController.GetThreadMessages` returns **200 + empty**. **Framing correction worth carrying: the predicate is "the thread id contains `/`", NOT "the thread is MMS."** GV group threads are `g.Group Message.<base64url>` and the base64url alphabet includes `/`; group threads merely *happen* to be the MMS threads, which is why the symptom looked MMS-shaped in the UAT. **Ask:** decode the route value in **both** `GetThreadMessages` **and** `MarkThreadRead` (`Uri.UnescapeDataString`), or move the id to a query parameter — **plus a per-thread sanity check**, because *their existing honest-status guards structurally cannot catch this*: `ShapeIsSane` and the `Succeeded` flag both pass, since the fetch and the parse genuinely succeeded and only the **filter** matched nothing. **We cannot fix it here:** double-escaping (`%252F`) still yields 0 messages, and a raw `/` misses the API route entirely and falls through to their SPA fallback, returning `index.html` with HTTP 200. **Ask them to do this one first** — it is cheap, and until it lands group conversations stay unreadable even in a perfectly healthy window, which also confounds any test of item 6.

6. **GV PSIDTS staleness → a deterministic ~9-minute auth blackout every 20 minutes.** **Confirmed from server logs**, 2026-07-31: `api2thread/list returned Unauthorized for folder Sms` **271 times in one day**, in a clean 20-minute square wave, surfacing to us as **HTTP 502**. Mechanism captured at a boundary: a `CDP cookie refresh` at 15:00:02 restores service and the next `Unauthorized` lands **11m42s later** — **Google's PSIDTS is good for ~11 minutes, their refresh fires every ~20, and there is no reactive refresh on 401.** **11 of 11** of our 502s fall inside a dead window. **Throttling is FALSIFIED three ways** — this was the UAT's own first guess, so it is worth stating plainly: our constant-rate 60-second poller shows the identical on/off pattern, so **failure tracks wall-clock, not request volume**; the status is **401, never 429**; and recovery lands on fixed 20-minute boundaries rather than after a variable cooldown. **Concrete lead to hand them:** deployed `appsettings*.json` declares **`CookieRefreshIntervalMinutes: 5`** (matching the default at `GVBridgeConfig.cs:23`) while the **observed** cadence is 20 minutes — so either the value is not being read or something downstream sets its own interval; a genuine 5-minute refresh would make most of this disappear. **Ask:** align the interval with the ~11-minute observed lifetime, add **refresh-and-retry on the first 401** (a purely time-based fix stays fragile if Google shortens the lifetime), and **make `/api/gvbridge/status` honest** — measured at 15:13:03 while both SMS endpoints were returning 502, it still reported `{"available":true,"cookiesValid":true,"degraded":false}`, which is why our **"Google Voice is reconnecting"** banner (`PhoneMessagesPanel.razor:14-20`) never fires during the exact window it exists for. _Same lesson as `Succeeded` meaning only "the JSON parsed": a health field derived from a probe rather than from "did the last real call return data" will report healthy straight through an outage._ **Our GV-8 does not wait on this** — theirs makes the failure rare, ours makes it honest.

7. **Two boundary-doc `Change Log` entries are sitting UNCOMMITTED in the RotaryPhone repo — including the one that established the rule they are both breaking.** Verified 2026-08-10: `D:\prj\RotaryPhone` is on branch **`diag/gv-srtp-receive`** with `docs/prompts/RADIO-CONSOLE-BT-AUDIO-BOUNDARY.md` modified, **1 file / +2 lines**, both additions to the Change Log table. **Deliberately NOT a Radio Console queue row** — there is nothing to build here; the fix is a commit in the other repo, per that doc's own § "Passing Work Between Sessions." **(a) The 2026-08-10 entry — a shared-system change that reaches RotaryPhone.** It records that **`systemd-coredump` (255.4-1ubuntu8.16) now handles core dumps machine-wide** on `192.168.86.50`, replacing `apport`. The motivation was ours: `core_pattern` piped to apport, which retains exactly **one** `.crash` per executable, so **two `radio-api` SIGABRTs on 2026-08-10 (16:16 and 16:42) produced a single file — the second silently overwrote the first.** `apport-core-dump-handler` was APT-removed to satisfy apport's alternative dependency; the `apport` package itself remains. **Consequence for RotaryPhone: their crashes now land in `coredumpctl` / `/var/lib/systemd/coredump/` (zstd-compressed) instead of `/var/crash/*.crash`** — strictly more forensic data, not less (`coredumpctl list` / `info <exe>` / `debug <exe>`). Retention is bounded in `/etc/systemd/coredump.conf` (`Compress=yes`, `ProcessSizeMax=2G`, `ExternalSizeMax=2G`, `MaxUse=2G`, `KeepFree=10G`). **Action required of them: none** — unless some RotaryPhone tooling greps `/var/crash/`, in which case switch it to `coredumpctl`. Verified at install time that `rotary-phone.service` stayed `active (running)` with MainPID unchanged and `NRestarts=0`. **(b) ⚠ The 2026-07-16 entry is uncommitted TOO — and it is the precedent.** That entry (the IAC "save" that relocated the boundary-owned WirePlumber rules into `deploy/common/`) is the one that wrote down *"this Change Log edit lives in the RotaryPhone repo and must be committed there separately — it is NOT part of the Radio Console PR."* **It was never itself committed.** So this is not one missed commit but the **second consecutive miss of the protocol the entry documents**, which is the part worth acting on: the boundary doc is the cross-service contract, and an uncommitted contract is invisible to the other session by construction. **Ask:** commit both rows in `D:\prj\RotaryPhone` (they belong on that repo's own branch/PR flow, not ours), and consider whether the "commit separately" step needs to become a checklist item in § "Passing Work Between Sessions" rather than a note inside individual rows.

8. **Record Radio Console's kiosk launcher as a SECOND CONSUMER of `gv-bridge-ensure.sh`.** `KIOSK-2` adds a caller that depends on that script's **path** and its **exit code** — it runs it to repair a down bridge and reads the result to decide whether the `VOICE` row is amber. It does **not** own, reimplement, edit, install or `pkill` any part of bridge startup; the watchdog timer and the nightly restart timer stay entirely RotaryPhone's. **Why this matters now rather than later: a Builder in `D:\prj\RotaryPhone` is bringing that script and its timers under version control as this is written**, and a relocation would silently break the new consumer. `KIOSK-2` therefore resolves the script from a candidate list (`~/bin/` → `/usr/local/bin/` → `/opt/rotary-phone/bin/`) rather than hard-coding one path, so a move degrades to a reported failure instead of a wrong one. **The ask is one Change Log entry** in `RADIO-CONSOLE-BT-AUDIO-BOUNDARY.md` naming the new consumer and pinning the contract as *path + exit code*. Per the 2026-08-10 precedent this is a commit in the other repo, so it is not claimable here — **and note that entry also recorded two boundary-doc Change Log edits sitting uncommitted in that repo, one of which established the very rule they were breaking, so this is a recurring miss of the protocol rather than a one-off.** Also worth passing along, already handled on the box today and needing no work from us: `gv-bridge-ensure.sh` now carries `--remote-debugging-port=9224 --remote-allow-origins=*`, whose absence was breaking cookie extraction and producing empty SMS/voicemail lists.

9. **`GetAudio` answers `404` during a GV auth blackout, because `FindNodeAsync` never checks `Succeeded` — filed as punch-list `XR-6`, 2026-09-03. This is the SECOND unfiled cross-repo request** (see `XR-5`, and note that both were found by reading their source rather than their docs). **Verified directly in `D:/prj/RotaryPhone/src/RotaryPhoneController.GVBridge/Api/GvVoicemailController.cs`:** `GetList` guards the list result at `:45-46` — *"Do not mask an auth/transport failure as 'no voicemails' — RadioConsole cannot tell the difference from an empty 200"* — and returns **502**. Its sibling `FindNodeAsync` (`:123`) calls the same `ListVoicemailsAsync` and does **not** guard it; it just does `result.Items.FirstOrDefault(v => v.MessageId == id)`. During the `XR-3` blackout a failed authenticated list returns an **empty** item set, so the lookup yields null and `GetAudio` answers **`404 "has no recording"`** (`:65-66`). **Why it matters on our side:** `GvMediaUnavailableException.IsPermanent` (`src/Radio.Infrastructure/External/GvMediaUnavailableException.cs:73`) maps `NotFound` to *"retrying will not help"*, so we tell a guest a voicemail is permanently gone **~45% of the time** — the blackout is ~9 minutes in every 20. **Ask:** propagate `Succeeded` through `FindNodeAsync` so `GetAudio` answers `502` exactly as `GetList` already does; it is a three-line change against a guard they have already written once. **Paste-ready text:** `design/plans/PHN-1c-event-playback-service-and-route.md` §5 item 2. **Route it the documented way** — a file under `D:\prj\RotaryPhone\docs\prompts\`, per the boundary doc's § "Passing Work Between Sessions"; the boundary doc's own Change Log is **not** the lane, since this is not a BT/audio ownership change. ⚠ **Our side is correct without it** — `PHN-1c` Task 10 narrows `IsPermanent` to `Disabled`-only, which stops us lying; only their fix makes `MediaNotFound` mean what it says.

## Documented fast-follows (NOT in these PRs)

- **Voicemail Call back / Text back quick actions** (decision 3) — deferred; markers left in the player.
- **GV mark-read / durable read-state** — ✅ **SHIPPED as GV-4 (PR #441)**, flag-OFF. Durable via GV write-through per ADR-024; Google is single source of truth, no local store. `MarkVoicemailReadAsync` (repointed) + `MarkSmsThreadReadAsync` + the unified `ReadStateChanged` SignalR event on `/hub`, reconciled idempotently by `(id-or-threadId + isRead)`.
  - **Remaining work is a config flip, not a build.** RotaryPhone's side is **shipped, not owner-HELD**: `GvVoicemailController.MarkRead`, `GvSmsController.MarkThreadRead`, `ReadStateChangedDto` + unconditional broadcast all exist and their routes match our call sites verbatim. Both sides are dark by config: theirs `GVBridge:EnableMarkRead=false`, ours `RotaryPhone:Gv:MarkReadEnabled=false`. **Flip theirs first, confirm the routes stop returning the dark rejection, then flip ours** in `appsettings.Production.json` (deploy overwrites `appsettings.json`).
  - **Unread support** (informational) — their `GVBridge:AllowMarkUnread` is also `false`, so `isRead:false` returns `400 unread_unsupported`. Our UI never sends `false` and the toggle stays hidden (ADR-024 §6). No change needed on either side unless unread is wanted.
  - **Path (b), phone→kiosk LIVE push** (informational) — their poller-flip fast-follow. Until it lands, externally-originated reads reconcile on our next list refresh rather than instantly. Same `ReadStateChanged` handler covers both; no RadioConsole-side change when it ships.
- **SMS send contract reconciliation** — ✅ **SCOPED AND QUEUED as GV-5** ([ADR-028](../design/decisions/2026-07-30-gv-sms-send-contract.md)). The earlier note here framed this as a response-shape mismatch; investigating the as-built endpoint found it is **larger on three axes**:
  - **The request is wrong and fails 100% of sends.** Ours is `SendSmsRequest(string ThreadId, string Text)`; theirs is `SendSmsRequest(string ToNumber, string Text, string? ThreadId, string? ClientCorrelationId = null)`. We POST `{"threadId":…,"text":…}`; `ToNumber` binds **`null`**, their `PhoneNumberNormalizer.TryNormalize(null)` returns false, and the route returns **`400 invalid_number`** — in both reply and new-recipient mode. Send was never functional, only silent.
  - **The taxonomy is nine codes, not eight.** `invalid_text` (400) was missing from the list previously recorded here. Full set: `queued` (200) | `send_disabled` (409) | `rate_limited` (429) | `invalid_text` (400) | `invalid_number` (400) | `auth_unavailable` (502) | `upstream_error` (502) | `timeout` (504) | `error` (500).
  - **We never subscribe to the outbound echo channel.** Outbound messages are broadcast on **`SmsSent`**, never on `SmsReceived`. `PhoneHubService` does not handle it, so GV-3's optimistic→confirmed de-dupe in `PhonePage.OnGvSmsReceived` is **unreachable dead code** and the optimistic bubble would linger un-reconciled. RotaryPhone's poller also re-surfaces the message later with a **different `Id`** by construction, so exact-id matching cannot be the only tier — their own source calls the fuzzy `(text + counterparty + ≤120s)` match "REQUIRED, not optional."
  - **Their `EnableSmsSend=false` is not a blocker.** A dark server returns `409 send_disabled` with no GV call; GV-5 treats that as an **availability state, not a failed send** (no red bubble, no Retry — nothing was sent). Flipping `RotaryPhone:Gv:SendEnabled` remains a separate, later two-side decision; GV-5 ships with it `false`.
- **Audible new-text chime** — belongs in the Radio.API audio layer (ducking-aware), not Blazor (handoff).
- **`NowPlayingPanel` volume-debounce tests carry `TEST-4`'s exact defect, and closing it needs a production seam — so it is filed here rather than folded into `TEST-4`'s PR.** Found while fixing `TEST-4`; **not fixed**, and deliberately not fixed, because the two PRs would not have been reviewable together. `TEST-4` was test-only; this one is not. Three tests in `tests/Radio.Web.Tests/Components/Shared/NowPlayingPanelVolumeDebounceTests.cs` — `VolumeDrag_PersistsThePreferenceOnce` (`:129`), `VolumeDrag_PersistsTheFinalValue` (`:165`, the one the `OPS-5` Builder saw fail under whole-solution contention) and `SeparatedVolumeChanges_EachPersist` (`:192`) — sequence with `await Task.Delay(1500)` against `NowPlayingPanel`'s own hardcoded 300 ms `System.Threading.Timer` (`src/Radio.Web/Components/Shared/NowPlayingPanel.razor:1054-1078`), with no rendezvous. **Be precise about which assertion is at risk:** `Assert.Equal(88d, pending)` is *safe* — `_pendingVolumePreference` is assigned synchronously inside `QueueVolumePreferenceSave` before the awaited call returns, so starvation cannot break it. It is `Assert.Equal(1, CountConfigWrites(handler))` that is unsafe, and it fails in **both** directions: **undershoot to 0** if the timer callback plus the *two* HTTP hops it makes (`ConfigurationApiService.cs:83-97` does a GET before the POST) do not all drain inside the 1500 ms; and **overshoot to 2** if a stall inserts >300 ms between the un-slept setup invokes, re-arming the trailing edge. The overshoot mode is the one `TEST-4` did not have. **Lower flake rate, same shape** — the margin is 5x (1500/300) rather than 3x on a 20 ms poll. **The fix needs a seam that does not exist:** `VolumePreferenceDebounce` is `private static readonly` and the timer is a raw `System.Threading.Timer`, so `NowPlayingPanel` must take an injectable `TimeProvider` first. The house idiom is already in `Radio.Web` (`EncoderHudService.cs:32,45-48`) and `FakeTimeProvider` is already used in this same test project (`EncoderHudServiceTests`, `SleepTests`). ⚠ Advancing a fake clock is **not sufficient on its own** — the callback is `async void` over two awaited HTTP hops, so the fix still needs a completion rendezvous before asserting on the write count. Sibling `VolumeDrag_StillAppliesEveryTickToTheAudioEngine` (`:150`) is genuinely safe and needs no change. The panel carries a second hardcoded timer with the same exposure (`_gainDebounceTimer`, 200 ms, `:891-909`) — worth pulling into the same seam. ~~**Planner: this is a row, not a note.**~~ ✅ **DONE 2026-09-03 — it is now a row.** Claimable as **`TEST-7`** in § Queue above, tiered P1 at [punch list §4.6](HANDOFF-GA-PUNCH-LIST.md). This entry stays only as the pointer; **the row is authoritative**.
